## High-Performance Distributed Filesystem Architecture

### Target Scale & Layout
* **Scale:** 15,000+ Concurrent Nodes — the **design target**, meaning (user ruling D1, 2026-08-02) 15 k nodes all reading AND writing but rarely the same files (at-scale AI-training mixed workloads). **Shipped (spec §6.12 correction, restated at the MW program close 2026-08-18; per-volume claim admission accepted 2026-08-24): ONE metadata authority per volume set (the D0 write mount) plus N read-only mounts plus N co-writer mounts — or, on a set the offline `squeezefs volume set-owners` verb assigned, K PER-VOLUME owners (D20: the slot-0 volume's owner is the SET authority — custody, lanes, the one grace ring; every other owner is a PARTIAL authority that appends to its own volumes and ships the rest). Under the ARMED symmetric plane (bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`, DARK — PR 12) the RW mount is a WRITER through the join ladder with no role declared and every posture knob retired beside the plane; and since PR 12b (2026-09-17, the N-daemon posture) N RW mounts per set — the D0 winner is the MANAGER, every later RW mount JOINS it over the wire as a full writer (its own ring, page, checkpoint task, listener; tree 0 a projection), N unbounded by design (per-writer pages + rings with no ledger bound, per-slot leases over 2^16 × 64 rotors, membership sharded by home volume) — plus N `-o ro` token readers; N = 2 and 3 are pinned in one process, N = 3 processes on kernel nvmet (the fidelity tier), a manager + 3 joined writers + a reader on the fleet rig (`sym-crash` 5/5, `sym-storm`) — all dev-box scoping; the counted box rows are PR 13's.** Readers (`-o ro` / `--read-only` — DLM S5: no lease, no claim, no PR key, orthogonal to the guard) serve the newest checkpoint they have polled, with a monotone staleness bound published live as `reader_staleness_bound_ms` — the shipped posture; under the dark symmetric plane (PR 5, `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` on a bit-17 set) a reader is a member-reader + READ-TOKEN client and user-visible metadata is EXACT at the next resolve, the bound reading 0; the DATA half of that bound is **eliminated when the S6 membership plane is armed** (§6.8 item 3's freed-offset grace period, `src/free_grace.rs`) and stays bounded-not-eliminated with the plane `off`, the shipped default. Co-writers (DLM S9, `SQUEEZEFS_MULTI_WRITER=1` + the five-rung admission ladder — PR-capable data namespaces required, refused loud otherwise) DMA their own data under authority-granted custody and SHIP every metadata mutation; byte-range custody of one shared file (S11) exists behind the default-off `SQUEEZEFS_RANGE_CUSTODY`. **Evidence tiers per `docs/rc-manifest.md` (mandatory on every scale claim):** what is *measured-real* is the single-node proving fleet (up to 32 co-located mounts + a qemu/KVM guest member, real nvmet-tcp PR target — `.benchmarks/2026-08-16-mw-s{6,7,8,9}-arm.md`, closing record `.benchmarks/2026-08-18-mw-program-closing.md`); fleet fan-out rows are *measured-simulated* (one box, one memory bus); **every 15 k number is arithmetic-on-measured-constants** — and the published arithmetic itself says the single-authority publish plane bounds aggregate co-writer ingest at ≈ 2.6 GiB/s independent of writer count (the S9-a note), which is the wall the S10 fleet-of-authorities recipe exists to move — **built and accepted 2026-08-24** (per-volume claim admission, `docs/design-per-volume-claim-admission.md`: `squeezefs volume set-owners` + `SQUEEZEFS_MW_ROLE=set-authority|partial-authority` stand a K-owner fleet where each owner appends to its own volumes and ships the rest; the S10 tar-x gate is MET at 0.98× of S0 on a 2-owner fleet, closing record `.benchmarks/2026-08-22-pv-claim-admission.md`). 15 k has never been measured; claims that imply otherwise are wrong.
* **Architecture Type:** Decoupled metadata (MetaLV) + **block data** (local NVMe / NVMe-oF), exposed via POSIX FUSE.

### Technology Stack
* **Client Daemon (FUSE Engine):** Built in **Rust** on `io_uring` (FUSE-over-io_uring after INIT) with the in-house executor (`crates/squeezefs-ipc/src/sqz_{exec,time,channel,blocking,taskset}.rs` — rip-tokio-total, 2026-08-13: the scheduler-bug remedy was REMOVAL; tokio survives as a `#[tokio::test]` dev-dependency only, enforced by `tests/no_tokio_convention_tests.rs`).
* **Metadata & Distributed Lock Manager (DLM):** Local or distributed logical volume backend (**MetaLV**). Primary meta store for attrs, layout maps, leases, and volume format.
* **Data Backend (primary):** **NVMe / NVMe-oF block devices** via `NvmeBlockDev` (io_uring workers). Progressive layouts (inline / staged / striped) live on this path.

### Progressive Data Layout & I/O Routing

Logical file growth uses three layouts. The inline ceiling is **DERIVED, and derives to one page** (`routing::INLINE_MAX_FLOOR` = 4 KiB; the 2026-09-09 inline-raise campaign made it a derivation and its phase-B sweep `.benchmarks/2026-09-09-inline-raise-sweep-local.md` set the derivation's value): an inline file's payload IS its layout record, so every write of it rides the metadata plane twice (journal entry + CoW node append, ≈ 2× its bytes) against the staged layout's fixed ≈ 0.3 KB of metadata per file — one page is where the record's payload costs about what a block mapping does (the sweep read 16 KiB inline at −34 % files/s and 59× the metadata bytes per file, 32 KiB at −68 %/117× and a fail-stopped 1 GiB metadata volume). The registered override `SQUEEZEFS_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` raises it up to the volume's **format bound** (`routing::inline_max_bytes_ceiling` = the KV xattr value cap `min(64 KiB, node_size/4)` minus the layout wire's 4 KiB framing headroom — the largest payload one KV value holds; range 4096..=61440; the operator's lever for generously sized metadata volumes with a hard small-file cross-client-visibility need, and the measurement lever); the ceiling in force is published live as `inline_max_bytes`. Above it: up to one block (4 MiB) staged when staging dirs exist, else striped.

1. **Inline (small — up to `inline_max_bytes`):** Payload in the Metadata Volume as the layout record's `data_key` (`inline_data:…` key/attribute) with type `inline` — visible to every client of the set at the layout commit, no block, no promotion step. `layout_inline_writes` is the write-path gauge.
2. **Staged (small):** Local NVMe staging (`file_id` + optional `mapping:…`) — the whole payload is one ring entry whose ONLY copy is the writing host's staging root until it is **promoted** (`DataRouter::promote_staged_file`, the ONE primitive every promoter runs — it **dispatches on size**: a file whose logical size fits the inline ceiling promotes INTO INLINE (the payload becomes the layout record's `data_key` in the same commit that flips the type — no block, no durable reference; `layout_promoted_inline`), a larger one into one placed striped block (one block DMA + one layout commit + the ring-entry release; `layout_promoted_block`) — the whole-block promotion of a small file was the 64× space law the fsync-promotion pricing found). Promotion happens at exactly three points (`.benchmarks/2026-09-09-dismount-staged-residue.md` §7): **staging-pool pressure** (the merge worker past the 75 % high-water mark), the writing mount's **clean unmount** (the dismount pass, 2026-09-09 — `dismount_promoted_*`), and **`fsync(2)` when `SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_PROMOTE_STAGED=1`** (default OFF — the squeeze-test A/B rejected the unconditional arm on the space law, `.benchmarks/2026-09-09-fsync-promote-staged-ab.md`; its own `fsync_phase_ns.staged_promote` leg, `fsync_promoted_*`; a cold recovered ino's layout is fetched for it). Under the one-page default every staged file is above the ceiling by definition, so the promotion arms dispatch INTO BLOCKS — the dismount pass's 64× space law stands and is the **packing** campaign's to fix (data-plane bytes, metadata-plane mapping); the inline dispatch engages only where an operator raised the ceiling over staged custody (a `SQUEEZEFS_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` mount recovering a default-ceiling ring). Until promoted, every other client of the set reads the file as size-consistent zeros (`staged_payload_lost_reads`); plain `fsync` alone makes it durable on that ring only.
3. **Striped (large):** 4 MiB (configurable) blocks on the active block backend with `block_map:…` and refcounts.

Writes that grow past thresholds promote layouts **durably** (block I/O before meta type flip — see P0 layout atomicity).

### Distributed Lock Manager (DLM) & Consistency

**Shipped scope (corrected 2026-08-02, spec §6.1/§6.12 — the prior text described an unimplemented design):** `src/dlm.rs` is a **process-local** lock manager — a `scc::HashMap` with no network, no persistence, no TTL on a held lease, no renewal, no revocation, no shared mode, and no cross-process visibility. It serializes writers *within* one mount; **cross-mount exclusion is the D0 single-writer mount guard's job, not this module's.** Fencing tokens are monotonic per file **per process** and restart at 0 on every mount (spec §6.11 — the "stale tokens discard staged work" remount law cannot fire at mount time today; the staging generation stamp is what actually protects that path). The DLM program that makes this genuinely distributed is spec §6.9 stages S0–S11; treat every claim below as *design intent under construction*, and never write code that assumes a capability the stage table has not landed.

**The full multi-writer program is COMPLETE** (2026-08-18): `docs/design-full-multi-writer.md` (consensus 2026-08-15 — arm S6–S9, build S10–S11, single-node client fleets) ran all 19 rungs plus its fix campaigns 2026-08-15 → 2026-08-18; the closing record is `.benchmarks/2026-08-18-mw-program-closing.md` and the design doc carries the per-rung landed SHAs and the **rung-20 residual board** (the next program's input). Landed and load-bearing: the KD-MW-2 `(node_token, mount_slot)` client identity, per-mount NVMe-oF identity (`SQUEEZEFS_HOSTNQN`/`SQUEEZEFS_HOSTID`, pair-or-neither) + durable `fabric_endpoint:` records, the KD-MW-14 `SQUEEZEFS_FLEET_SHARE` root divisor, one-act nine-bit stamping with **`format` multi-writer-capable BY DEFAULT** (rung 10b — `--single-writer` is the opt-out), the sqz-kernel host-scoped fabric subsystems (patch 0030, `docs/design-mw-multipath-kernel.md`), S6–S9 armed and crucible-proven, KD-MW-16 fleet-parallel maintenance, S10 delegations/intents/placement (the tar-x gate adjudicated as the honest product statement — 6.73×, structurally unmeetable while one node owns every volume), and S11 byte-range custody with the MPI-IO verdict **MET** (`SQUEEZEFS_RANGE_CUSTODY` stays default-off pending the indirect-map width-N composition). Every distributed plane still ships **dark by default** — a plain mount arms nothing and re-proves the solo re-gate (`dlm_rpcs == 0`); consult that design + the residual board before touching any DLM/membership/custody surface. Under the dark symmetric plane every RW mount of an ARMED bit-17 set is a WRITER through the join ladder — no authority, no roles, no `set-owners` (PR 12, 2026-09-16; the MANY-writer posture — N RW daemons per set, N unbounded by design — PR 12b, 2026-09-17) — and a node's DEATH is recovered by the set itself (PR 10, 2026-09-16): the S6 eviction writes the death ledger, every manager replays the dead appender's ring into its trees at the next projection, and a death the plane never recorded is the operator's `squeezefs appender clear` — an unarmed mount recovers only its own residue, exactly as shipped. The paragraph above (the process-local `src/dlm.rs` core) remains accurate for an UNARMED mount, which is every mount that does not opt in.

Write custody maps to leases on Metadata Volumes:
* **Acquisition:** Lease locking + fencing token `INCR` (no external distributed database required).
* **Granularity:** File-level write leases; never directory-wide for data.
* **Leases & Heartbeats:** the **process-local** per-inode lease has no expiry by design (the `ttl` argument is the *waiter's* deadline) — a solo mount needs none. TTL + background renewal + revocation SHIP on the distributed planes: S6 membership leases (owner TTL vs the member's stricter `T_self`) and S9 custody leases (pull-based revocation, self-fence, dead-epoch quarantine) — armed only by opt-in.
* **Fencing Tokens:** Monotonic per-file tokens; writers present tokens; stale tokens → `FencingTokenExpired` / reject. **Monotone per process only** until S1/S2 (global `grant_seq` + durable `WriterClaim.term`).
* **POSIX advisory locks (fcntl byte-range, OFD, flock) are KERNEL-LOCAL** (2026-07-22, VL10 release gate — fstests generic/131/478/504): the INIT reply never advertises `FUSE_POSIX_LOCKS`/`FUSE_FLOCK_LOCKS` (pinned in the fuse3 fork's negotiation tests), so the kernel's canonical `posix_lock_file` arbitrates per mount — full POSIX semantics (unlock-on-close, splits, OFD owners, `/proc/locks`) for free. Intra-mount arbitration is the whole requirement under the D0 single-writer mount guard; the former daemon lock table and the DLM per-inode delegation surface it rode were deleted (never construct a daemon lock path "for the cluster" — cross-mount write exclusion is D0's job).

### Tiered Caching & Paths

* **Tier 1 (optional GDS):** GPU Direct path when `gds` feature is enabled.
* **Tier 2 (RAM LRU):** Sharded Clock/LRU read & write caches.
* **Tier 3 (Local NVMe staging / read cache):** Staging segments + optional read block cache; dehydrate on eviction.

### FUSE Client & Asynchronous I/O

* The in-house `sqz_exec` lane executor (per-core handler lanes, `sqz-meta` lanes, blocking pool); core pinning where configured. No tokio in product code.
* **Block path io_uring:** `NvmeBlockDev` worker (bounded request queue, backpressure, fixed-file register when available).
* **Path file I/O io_uring:** `crate::uring_fs` for ad-hoc local files (e.g. GDS cache materialize). Staging **mmap** segments stay mmap for zero-syscall get/put.
* **FUSE transport:** the first-party **fuse3** fork (`crates/fuse3`) — classical `/dev/fuse` only for `FUSE_INIT` (kernel requires initialized connection before REGISTER); **FUSE-over-io_uring is required** for the request hot path after arm (`flags2` `FUSE_OVER_IO_URING`, `REGISTER` / `COMMIT_AND_FETCH`). No userspace opt-out; mount fails if setup fails. Auto-enables `fuse.enable_uring=Y` when possible. One queue per possible CPU; session arms only after all queues REGISTERed.
* Always use io_uring when we can (non-negotiable) — see Non-Negotiables section.
* No dead code (non-negotiable) — see Non-Negotiables section.
* Not uring: TLS peer paths (network). Staging mmap segments stay mmap by design.

### Reference fast-FUSE clients (user directive, 2026-07-14)

The **DAOS client** (`github.com/daos-stack/daos`, `src/client/` — dfuse / libdfs / libioil / libpil4dfs) and **JuiceFS** (`github.com/juicedata/juicefs`) are the designated reference fast-FUSE clients. **Consult them for perf/recovery patterns before designing new client-side machinery** — both independently converged on the levers the metadata-throughput program validated (negative-dentry TTLs, clean-handle FLUSH elision, submit batching, per-class kernel TTLs), and their recovery/ops machinery (external supervisor + FUSE-connection abort, fsck/dump/backup, cache-disk health FSM, seamless-upgrade fd handover) is the standing gap board. The full survey — findings classified HAVE / IN-PROGRAM / GAP / N/A with ranked P1/P2/P3 adoption boards and code anchors — lives at **`docs/reference-clients-survey.md`**; extend it (same classification) when surveying them again.

### Metadata Cluster Topology

* Keys for volume control use `fs_prefix` / `fs_key!` helpers.
* **Layout keys** (`metadata:…`, `inline_data:…`, `block_map:…`, `mapping:…`, `active_block:…`) are **unprefixed historical** forms — use `crate::keys::*` helpers; do not migrate under `FS_PREFIX` without an on-disk format change.

### Metadata format: v3 CoW KV (the only format)

Format v3 is the **only** on-disk metadata format. Legacy v2 support was removed entirely (user directive: always forward — no backwards compatibility): a v2 superblock refuses to mount loud ("no longer supported; reformat required"), `squeezefs format --force` reformats it to v3, and the offline `squeezefs migrate` converter was deleted with the v2 reader. Normative spec `docs/design-cow-kv-metadata.md`, measured gates `.benchmarks/2026-07-09-kv-v3-gates.md`.

* **v3:** a bcachefs-style **copy-on-write typed-KV btree** (`src/meta_backend/kv/`). One node layer, three logical trees per volume (`TREE_INODES`/`TREE_DENTRIES`/`TREE_XATTRS`) plus, behind incompat bit 9, a fourth (`TREE_BLOCK_REFS` — durable data-block references, see below), memcmp-ordered keys. **256 KiB log-structured CoW nodes** (format knob `--meta-node-kib`, 64 KiB–1 MiB) with internal sorted-bset appends; a **lock-free reservation journal** (ring `clamp(volume/64, 8 MiB, 32 MiB)`, `--meta-journal-mb` override, **read at every mount**) carrying one checksummed entry per transaction; **checkpoints** flip per-tree roots in an A/B root ledger on the background flush cadence, **never on the commit path**; **monotonic ino allocation** (no reuse, no seeding scan); an **A/B extent-bitmap allocator** (1 bit / 256 KiB extent) with a journaled pending-free protocol. Superblock: magic `METALV01`, version 3, `features_incompat` bit 0 = `KV_V3` (`src/meta_backend/kv/superblock.rs`). Caps: ≥ 100 M inodes, 1 M+ entries/dir, unlimited xattrs (value ≤ `min(65536, node_size/4)` B). Reads stay RAM-authoritative and latch-free (scc node index + arc-swap snapshots, demand-paged, clock eviction). Every on-disk unit is checksummed and copy-on-write: whole-transaction atomicity (one tx = one checksummed journal entry) + torn-write immunity (torn writes detected-and-ignored) — strictly stronger than the retired v2 D0/D1/D2 sector contract (§4.10).
* **Env knobs:** `SQUEEZEFS_META_NODE_CACHE_MB` / `SQUEEZEFS_META_NODE_CACHE_PCT` (node-cache budget; default derived `max(budget/16, 512 MiB)` per volume — derivation sweep 2026-08-04, absolute > pct > derived), `SQUEEZEFS_META_CHECKPOINT_MAX_DIRTY_NODES` (dirty-node checkpoint cap = mount-replay working-set bound; default derived `max(4096, budget/32 ÷ node_size)`); `SQUEEZEFS_META_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` is the journal/checkpoint cadence (`0` = strict per-commit).
* **Stats fields (stats inode):** `meta_format_version` (constant `"3"` per volume — kept because operators key on it); `meta_volume_atomicity` (contract class — constant `cow-checksummed`) alongside `meta_volume_atomicity_physical` (the informational hardware probe); and the `meta_kv_*` family — `node_cache_{hits,misses,evictions}`, `node_appends`, `node_{append,rewrite}_bytes`, `node_{compactions,splits}`, `node_freeze_shadow_dropped` (freeze-time shadow-fold engagement, 2026-07-30 meta-plane-writes: overlay records excluded from frozen bsets because a newer same-key Put/Delete in the same freeze shadows them — high under same-key commit storms by design), `journal_{bytes,entries,full_stalls}`, `journal_{entries,bytes}_per_volume` (per-volume journal attribution arrays — the meta-plane balance instrument; a healthy multi-volume set moves EVERY volume under load, the 2026-07-30 field conviction's shape was one volume at ~21–25k device-writes/s and its sibling at 0.00), `checkpoints`, `commit_smo_retries`, `node_dropped_tail_bsets`, `dentry_collision_overflows`, `delta_orphans`, `replay_{entries,dropped_torn,ms}`, `block_refs_{staged,released,recovered,drift,unresolved}` (durable block-reference accounting — see **Durable block refcounts** below; `drift` is a must-stay-0 tripwire), `free_extents`, `pending_free`, `pending_free_{parked,released,overflow}` (SMO frees parked on / released by durable-tail coverage — design-smo-replay-currency Option A; `parked − released` tracks the `pending_free` gauge, the wedge detector; `overflow` = retirements forced past the at-cap FIFO into the unbounded overflow — the 2026-07-27 §4.7 pinned-floor cycle-break's engagement gauge, ≈ 0 in steady state. The at-cap FIFO is a pressure valve for threshold SMOs only: the checkpoint flush pass's compactions and mount-side re-parking are never refused, successor leftover floors are exact per-record `entry_floor` stamps, the clause-b progress audit rides EVERY barriered cycle, and the mount gate pre-claim-drains an exhausted ring — evidence `.benchmarks/2026-07-27-kv-pending-free-cycle.md`), and the **full-heap posture** `heap_full` (0/1 per volume) / `enospc_refusals` / `heap_full_cycles` / `heap_promised` (2026-09-11, the inline-raise sweep's P1: a user commit never claims an extent — the flush pass does, for the SMOs its records force — so the §4.7 reserve had no user side and a FULL volume fail-stopped through the wedged-tail bound; now the commit pass PROMISES every projected SMO's extents at admission (`NodeCache::heap_promised`, the ledger — 0 at quiesce) and refuses growth that needs a new leaf with `ENOSPC` while compactions/deletes are admitted down to the compaction floor `reserve/2`; a cycle that defers a node for `NoSpace` is the counted, loud-once, NON-terminal space class and the FAILED terminal is the wedge class alone; since §4.6a leaf merge (2026-09-11) deletes return EXTENTS too: adjacent underfull leaves merge into one successor at the split's ¾ fill read backwards (`merge_pair_capacity`, candidate ≤ ¼ — hysteresis structural), interior recursion + root collapse as a root-swap SMO, net −1 extent per merge at the compaction floor; triggers = the flush pass's sibling check, the heap-full recovery sweep (level by level, leaves up), the D4 defrag arm; gauges `meta_kv_node_merges`, `meta_kv_root_collapses`, `meta_kv_merge_candidates`, `meta_kv_merge_sweeps`, `frag_d4_mergeable_leaves`, `defrag_meta_merges`). The retired v2-only counters (`meta_commit_sectors`, `meta_sector_lock_*`, `meta_inode_alloc_*`, `meta_tx_concurrency*`, `meta_quarantined_inodes`) no longer exist.
* **Filesystem generation identity:** per-volume the v3 superblock `uuid` (random per `format` invocation), joined in volume order (`meta_backend::volume_set_generation`) — local staging is bound to it. (The v2-era `FormatConfig.fs_uuid` config stamp was deleted with v2; the superblock uuid is the sole generation identity.)

### Durable block refcounts and free list (Implemented 2026-08, incompat bit 9 — stamped by the DEFAULT format since the rung-10b flip)

Pre-RC engineering spec §6.2 **item 1** ("the largest item"), ruling **D9**. `BlockAllocator`'s refcount map, free list and allocation cursor had **no on-disk representation** — all three were re-derived at every mount by walking the live inode tree and reading each ino's `layout` xattr ("pure derived state"). §6.2's verdict: *"Without durable shared ownership accounting, no multi-writer data path is expressible"* — two writers each derive a private answer from the subset of the tree they walked, so a block node A holds at refcount 2 reads 1 on node B, whose W1 sole-owner patch then rewrites it in place with `patch_ineligible_shared` never incrementing (silent on a passthrough volume). Design + rationale `docs/design-durable-block-refcounts.md`; measured cost `.benchmarks/2026-08-04-durable-block-refcounts.md`; contracts `tests/durable_block_refs_tests.rs`.

* **The representation is one record per REFERENCE** (a backpointer), in a fourth logical tree `TREE_BLOCK_REFS = 6` (`src/meta_backend/kv/block_refs.rs`): key `(vol_tag u64 ‖ block_idx u64 ‖ owner_ino u64 ‖ block_index u32)` BE, value `(version u8 ‖ flags u8 ‖ reserved u16)`. `refcount(block)` = the population of the 16-byte `(vol_tag, block_idx)` prefix. A count record would need a lost-update-prone RMW (two inodes cloning one block share no lock); a delta record cannot express the FIRST reference (Δ-without-base is a counted no-op). `vol_tag` decodes the durable `vol-{16 hex}` id verbatim (KD-5) — never a path, an ordinal, or a set position.
* **The delta rides the layout transaction** — `set_layout_and_size` / `merge_layout_and_size` / the Lever-B aggregated pass stage the ops into the SAME `KvTx` (one tx = one checksummed journal entry, §4.10): the ledger can never disagree with the layout that justifies it, and the publish stays ONE commit (**pinned**: `accounting_rides_the_publish_transaction_and_adds_no_commit` asserts journal-entry equality vs an un-stamped volume). Never re-split it.
* **O(batch), never O(file size)** — §5.3's one merge discipline means `merge_block_mappings_if_epoch` / `publish_pass` already know every inserted and displaced key; measured **40–52 ns CPU + 46 B journal per reference**.
* **The free list needs no durable structure**: it is the complement of the referenced set below the cursor — the arithmetic `recover_block` / `fsck_reconcile_accounting` already run. `begin_free → reclaim → finish_free` has **no intermediate durable state** (the terminal free's durable effect IS the `Delete` that rode the publish), so a crash recovers to referenced or free — never a leak, never a double free; what it loses is the queued discard (hygiene, which is why `trim --full` walks the free list). **On the FOREST (symmetric PR 7, design §5.4.3 law 2) that derivation is the SEAM, not the law**: the referenced set is scattered over slot trees, so the free list becomes design §5.5.1's per-data-volume allocation bitmap (PR 8's); until it lands the mount still derives it from ONE by-block scan, while the two DECISIONS that read a refcount — the W1 sole-owner predicate and the terminal free — read the durable truth on an armed set: one range probe in the referencing ino's slot tree (`block_ref_probes`) plus the SHARED mark of the clone protocol, never the RAM count alone.
* **The write-path wiring is complete and the oracle is the proof**: fsck class **C8** (`C8DurableRefDrift`) detection is **UNGATED** and `meta_kv_block_refs_drift` is a live must-stay-0 tripwire; the oracle runs clean with the ledger engaged across all 28 layout-publishing suites (`SQUEEZEFS_TEST_STAMP_BLOCK_REFS=1` is the seam that engages bit 9 in isolation on a `--single-writer` base; the default format stamps it since the rung-10b flip). The last gap was a CLASS — a site that mutates the RAM map and leaves the layout dirty defers its accounting to a save that is handed a map already containing the change — closed structurally by the per-ino deferred-op accumulator (`pending_block_refs`), so any future deferring site is correct by construction.
* **The derived walk survives as the ORACLE, not the truth** — `verify_durable_block_refs` diffs the durable census against the layout walk's, both through the SAME extraction path. Drift is fsck class **C8** (`C8DurableRefDrift`, report-only — restating the ledger from the walk would erase the evidence of why the invariant broke; the layouts stay authoritative) and `meta_kv_block_refs_drift` must stay 0. Mount runs it only under `SQUEEZEFS_BLOCK_REFS_VERIFY=1` (running it would pay the very walk the records delete).
* **Compatibility**: **bit 9 is stamped by the DEFAULT `format` since the rung-10b Phase-B flip** (2026-08-16, one act with the other eight multi-writer bits — `.benchmarks/2026-08-16-mw-default-flip.md`; ruling D9's build-dark posture governed until the §6.3 evidence table + the S9 acceptance went green, and the D9-era history is why the number matters: bit 8 is `KV_PARTITIONED_APPEND` — the two were authored in parallel against the same free bit; partitioned append landed first, so durable block references took bit 9, `src/meta_backend/kv/superblock.rs`). A `--single-writer` or pre-flip volume mounts DERIVED exactly as before, sector 0 byte-identical (pinned); `set_block_refcounts_bit` / `squeezefs volume enable-multi-writer` is the offline upgrade path, after which the first writable mount mints the root; a read-only mount never mints and never accounts. The stamped-solo cost is measured ≈ 0 and the ledger ran drift-free live under the S4 re-gate (`.benchmarks/2026-08-15-mw-s4-regate.md`).
* **What S9 still owes**: per-writer key scoping (§6.2 item 8 — `active_block:` writer ids; this key layout leaves room), the other eight §6.2 format assumptions, cross-writer custody arbitration (the ledger states what the set believes; it does not serialize writers), the §6.8 freed-offset grace period, and node-cache revalidation/partitioning for a second reader of this tree.

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 1, the slot-tree forest (2026-09-13, DARK; incompat bit 17)

The symmetric shared-disk metadata program (`docs/design-symmetric-metadata.md`, Rev 7 — every mount an equal metadata authority; owner rulings R-SYM-1..8, D18/D19/D20 reversed) lands in 16 rungs, every one dark until the PR-14 flip. **PR 1 landed the FOREST** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-13-sym-forest.md`; `tests/sym_forest_tests.rs`): under `FEATURE_INCOMPAT_KV_SYMMETRIC_FOREST` (bit 17) a metadata volume holds **one mixed-kind KV tree per routing slot** (KD-SYM-2) plus the control tree **`TREE_CONTROL = 8` ("tree 0")** whose `slot_state` records (`src/meta_backend/kv/slot_state.rs`) name every guest slot tree's root; the fixed ledger names tree 0 and the native slot tree only. **The kind bytes ARE the tree ids** (`record.rs`: `KIND_INTERIOR = 0` for a slot tree's interior records — `tag_for(0, level ≥ 1)`, journaled under a slot-prefixed key `slot: u32 BE ‖ separator` (`forest::interior_journal_key`) because the separator alone cannot name the tree: the rightmost child's is `KEY_SPACE_MAX`, which every tree's top separator shares; `TREE_SHARED_INDEX = 9` reserved; `TREE_ID_MAX` 7 → 9); forest keys are `ino ‖ kind ‖ rest` (inode 9 B / dentry 17 B / xattr 17 B / block-map 13 B) and `0x06 ‖ …` for block references (29 B), memcmp order preserved within a kind, "a kind byte is never another tree's id" refused at the one codec — so `stat + layout` is one leaf and a record's slot is `key_ino >> 40` (0 = native, `s + 1` = guest slot `s`, `FOREST_SLOT_MAX = guest_forest_slot(u16::MAX)` the one bound enforced at the encoder AND the decoder; refs by their OWNER ino — so a block's population is the union over EVERY slot tree, and `SlotTrees::refs_window` walks each tree with its own cursor: `block_ref_count` / `block_ref_scan` / the mount-time ledger seed / the C8 oracle are exact on a forest). **`kv/forest.rs` (`SlotTrees`) is the ONE router**: `(kind, legacy key) → (slot tree, forest key)`, slot trees minted lazily on a slot's first record ("an empty slot owns no extent"), kind-filtered slot-ordered range scans (the ino-major kinds; the refs family is scanned whole-window per tree); `KvMetaBackend` routes EVERY record access through the kind-keyed helpers `lookup_kind` / `range_kind` / `delta_chain_probe_kind` / `insert_kind` / `delete_kind` / `tree_for_record` / `tree_of_node` / `record_locator` (`TreeSet::{Flat, Forest}` is the one place the layouts diverge; the FLAT arm BORROWS its per-kind tree — `TreeRef::Borrowed(&KvTree)` — so the shipped hot path pays no refcount RMW, the W-6 shared-line law; `flat_trees()` is the per-kind surface of a FLAT volume only — empty on a forest — and production code never indexes it). Entry planners price the STAGED key (`staged_frame_len` — legacy + 1 on a forest), so the corpse sweep's chunked destroys fit the cap by the arithmetic the commit enforces on either layout. The kind-routed walks SKIP a slot-tree record whose key the codec refuses (counted on `meta_kv_forest_key_violations`, logged) — a failed page hid every neighbour of one malformed record and truncated fsck's census into false C2 "leaked" findings whose repair FREED live blocks (found by the forest arm of the C1 repair contract, round 3); fsck's C1 on a forest is ONE raw walk per slot tree (`C1Unit::Slot`), where such a key is the record-level `C1RawKey` finding, repaired in place (`slot_tree_delete_raw`). The staging choke point frames the forest key once; both commit pipelines resolve leaves through `tree_for_record` (a mint happens before any lock); the checkpoint task publishes every moved guest root into tree 0 as one checkpoint-class entry BEFORE its flush pass, with the named images barriered first (a fresh mint's root is written with no barrier of its own); **an unpublished moved root is a FLOOR** — `KvTree::root_floor` (the mint's ring head / the swap's reservation) clamps the cycle's tail through `SlotTrees::unpublished_root_floor` until tree 0 names the root, so a publication the checkpoint reserve defers leaves every acked record under that root in the replay window (the same law the flush pass's skipped SMOs obey), and a publication entry that fails to write is the journal-failure class (`note_journal_failure`), never a warning; mount replays tree 0 FIRST, opens the guest trees it names, then runs the two-phase per-ring replay routed by slot (interior records by their journal key's slot prefix). A `-o ro` reader of a forest skips the ledger's per-id root for guest trees (`revalidate_trees` — every slot tree carries id 0, the native root is not theirs) and `forest_reader_resync` re-reads tree 0 at each epoch step, adopting guests minted after its mount; **the open's POSTURE governs the replay's mint** (`OpenPosture` set by the door → `forest::MintPolicy`): the write mount's replay mints a window's unpublished slot tree in the RECOVERY class (`claim_internal` — a `heap_full` volume with an unpublished mint in its window must still mount), the commit path's runtime mint stays USER growth, and a NON-WRITER (reader / co-writer / probe / peer-owned) never mints — its replay SKIPS those records (`meta_kv_forest_reader_window_skips`; served at the poll after the writer publishes, S5 bounded staleness) and a mint reached under its policy is an invariant tripwire, never a silent write; its view is partial BY SLOT and CONSISTENT — the routed directory pager (`RoutedMetaBackend::readdir_page_held`, the one both listing faces ride) lists a child iff the mount holds the child's slot tree, the condition its `lookup` answers, so `readdir` never lists a name `lookup` refuses (`meta_kv_forest_reader_unpublished_children`). The by-block refs scan REFUSES a key the codec rejects (the flat decode's law — a block's population is never answered short; only the kind-routed record walks skip), and fsck's raw C1 repair deletes ONLY a key whose kind byte this binary knows in a shape it never takes (`record::classify_refused_key`) — an unknown kind is `report-only`, since design §7.1 makes the slot-tree kind set part of bit 17's meaning (a kind added after the flip ships under a new incompat bit). Every slot tree's nodes carry header `tree_id` 0, so `CachedNode` carries a RAM-only **forest-slot owner stamp** (set at resolve/publish; `KvTree::owns` at every maintenance/merge filter) and the flush pass dispatches by it. The §4.10 digest walk (`digest_backend`) runs over `(kind, legacy key, value)`, so **a forest volume and a flat volume with the same records digest EQUAL** — the forest is a relayout, never a rewrite. **Bit 17 lands DARK**: at PR 1 nothing stamped it but the test seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_STAMP_SYMMETRIC=1` (`Kind::Bool`, registered — a bad value refuses at startup like its `TEST_STAMP_*` siblings); since PR 11 (2026-09-14) the seam OR `format --symmetric` OR the offline `volume enable-symmetric` conversion stamp it — three explicit acts, all dark until the default flip, PR 14's — and **a bit-17-absent volume takes the shipped code path verbatim** (pinned: the un-stamped builder image names exactly the three §4.2 roots, the digest is layout-independent, every pre-forest suite runs unchanged). **The parametrized gate** `tests/run_sym_forest_suites.sh` (= `task check:sym-forest`) runs the fifteen pre-forest KV suites flat THEN under the seam and fails on the first red leg — the PR-1 row's "every existing KV contract green on the forest" made an instrument (its first stamped run is what found round 1's defects: the write-side audit fed the forest key to the legacy decoders, so a forest volume could not publish a striped layout in the gate tier; the refs probes saw the native slot only; a deferred publication left no floor); the harnesses reach the trees through the kind-routed helpers, never `flat_trees()[i]`, and the SMO build-pause seam arms a slot tree by SLOT (`test_smo_build_pause_arm_slot`). Fuzz: `slot_tree_record` (the forest key codec + the slot-prefixed interior journal key; absorbed `kvmap_record`) and `slot_state_record`, mirrored on stable in `tests/decoder_property_tests.rs`. Gauges: `meta_kv_forest_slot_trees_minted`, `meta_kv_forest_root_publishes`, `meta_kv_forest_key_violations` (must-stay-0), `meta_kv_forest_reader_window_skips` / `meta_kv_forest_reader_unpublished_children` (0 on every write mount). Owed to the later rungs: appender pages/rings (PR 2), the manager lease (PR 3), slot leases + the `apply_locked` lease gate (PR 4), tokens (PR 5), the ONE-walk fsck census over the mixed leaves for C2–C10 (C1 already walks a forest's slot trees once, raw; the other classes' kind-routed walkers read them once per kind), the lazy mint's one device write off the conveyor pass task (design §5.3.3 — the appender's grant, PR 3), the instrumented fuzz campaign (nightly tier), and the squeeze-test solo re-gate A-B-B-A (gate 1). Found and fixed on the way: `TEST_SMO_BUILD_PAUSE_TREE` used `0` both as OFF and as the compared tree id, so every slot-tree SMO parked forever on an unarmed seam; `forest_key` framed an ino no slot names while the decoder refused it; **the heap admission stranded a promise on a refused multi-leaf member** (§4.7 P1, layout-independent — the forest exposed it as the stamped mid-wave test's 32-cycle ENOSPC fixpoint: `heap_promised` one high for ever, `claimable = free − 1 = compaction floor`, no delete's compaction and no merge admissible again; a refused member now retracts every promise its earlier leaves drew — `NodeDirty::retract_promise`; pinned to the byte on both layouts by `a_refused_multi_leaf_member_strands_no_heap_promise`).

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 2, the appender region (2026-09-13, DARK; incompat bit 17)

**PR 2 landed the APPENDER REGION** on the forest (`.benchmarks/2026-09-13-sym-pr2-appender-region.md`; `tests/sym_appender_tests.rs`; design §5.3 B(ii), §5.7.3, §5.9, §11), dark behind the same bit 17 and the same seam — **a bit-17-absent volume is untouched** (sector 0 byte-identical: `superblock.appender_dir` at offset 136 lives in the zero padding and the decoder refuses it without the bit, after the feature gates; the ring is the whole journal extent; `appender_stats()` is `None` and every Appender gauge 0; pinned). Under bit 17 every writer is an **appender** with a 4 KiB **page** (`src/meta_backend/kv/appender.rs` — magic, id, generation, xxh3 with the field zeroed, the KD-MW-2 identity `{node_token, mount_slot: u32, writer_id: u128}`, term, state `{Free, Live, Recovering, Recovered}`, `recovered_by_term`, `is_manager`, `home_volume`, the ring's segment table `≤ RING_SEGMENTS_MAX = 8` (one 256 KiB segment is 64 pages; eight = the 2 MiB largest growth step before the page's bytes bind), head hint, `ledger_tail_seq`, `ckpt_seq`, the extent grant `≤ GRANT_RUNS_MAX = 4` runs, and the leased slots — `slot: u16` = the ROUTING slot, converted against the native slot — with `g`, `slot_tree_extents`, root, cursor at 35 B each; **`SLOT_PAGE_BUDGET` is COMPUTED from the page's fixed part = 108** — the design's 122 predates `slot_tree_extents`; decode is total and canonical, fuzzed by `appender_page` + the proptest mirror). Pages are read **newest-valid-wins over FOUR slots** (`page_slot_for(generation)`: the directory pair `[A, B]` + the ring-side `[R0, R1]` in the ring's first segment — a torn newest falls back twice) under the **DIRECTORY-FIRST law** (`page_write_slots`, `AppenderRegion::dir_named`, `write_region_page` the ONE page writer — review round 1, Issue 1): a page naming a ring table the directory pair does not yet fully name (the join onto a fresh ring, a growth, the leave) is written to every such directory slot in the same barriered step, and only a page whose table both directory images already name rides the rotation — `read_directory` reaches a ring-side slot only through a directory image naming that ring, so before the law the join after a clean unmount put its Live page where no directory image could find it on two of the four generation residues and a crash before the next-but-one checkpoint read the region as Free (acked records lost). **Appender 0's four page slots are the fixed journal extent's first four pages and its ring the rest** (`appender0_ring_extent`; `KvMetaBackend::fixed_ring_extent` is the one place the mount reads the geometry — harnesses that arm faults by journal position use `JournalRing::physical_offset_of`, never `journal.start` arithmetic); appenders ≥ 1 live in the **directory** — a chain of heap extents (`appender_dir` names the first; each holds `(node_size/4096 − 1)/2` pairs and a checksummed `DirHeader` LAST with the chain index and `next`) — which format under the seam allocates and writes together with appender 0's `Free` page. **`JournalRing` is a segment table** (`RingSegment`; `page_offset` the one logical→physical step; the shipped ring is the one-segment case, byte-identical; `new_segments` / `recover_segments` read one segment at a time into one image); **growth (`grown_with`) needs a DRAINED ring** (head == reusable_upto, no reservation open) because the page map changes for every position on lap ≥ 1 — a region's ring is REPLACED, never grown in place, and region 0's fixed ring never grows (it is the format's `--meta-journal-mb` decision). Derivations: ring floor `SYM_RING_FLOOR_BYTES` = the §4.4 pt 5 reserve + one `MAX_ENTRY_LEN` rounded to a power of two (512 KiB), ceiling = the solo ring's `journal_ring_len`, default `clamp(2 × ewma × CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE, floor, ceiling)` (a fresh join takes the floor), `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_RING_KB` (int, floor KiB..=32 MiB) explicit-wins, `appenders_capacity = heap/16 ÷ ring`, the flush ceiling `appender_flush_ceiling_ms() == CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE_MS` — all tie-tested in `derivation_sweep_tests`. **The mounted region** (`AppenderSet` / `AppenderRegion`, `KvMetaBackend::appenders`): the writer open reads the directory, binds `(node_token, mount_slot)` (the engaged writer scope, else the node token + `mount_slot()`), counts a `Live` page of its own NODE as **own-residue recovery** (`appender_self_recoveries` — for region 0 the ledger replay IS it; `AppenderIdentity::owned_by_node` is the one decision point: the writer holds the D0 flock here, the kernel's same-host death proof, so a same-node holder at ANY mount point is dead — the kill-9 successor remounting at another mount point derives another slot and must reclaim, exactly as the flat path does; the `(node, slot)` narrowing is PR 10's death ledger's), REFUSES a FOREIGN-node `Live` page — or a `Recovering` page of any identity — at the JOIN, loud, naming PR 10 as the driver that owns the remedy and no verb this binary lacks (deferred past the D0 claim gate so a live foreign holder gets D0's own message and `claim clear` / every non-joining Writer open is never blocked; a probe/reader lists it and mounts what tree 0 names), ADOPTS a page's NEWER roots (node seqs mint monotonically; a declared region's page is its content's only durable home once the tail passes the flushed leaf), JOINS after the D0 gate (`join_appender_regions`: Live, term + 1, one barriered cycle) and LEAVES at unmount in the order pages → barrier → release → bitmap → barrier (`Free` into both directory slots, id and term kept; a declared region's ring extents are released and their bits WRITTEN before the process exits — before Issue 2's fix the RAM release died with the process and every clean unmount leaked the ring; a crash between the two barriers is the honest C13 leak; the immediate release is the right op because no journal record ever references a ring extent). **Every checkpoint writes every page** (`write_appender_pages`, after the ledger: region 0's mirrors it and names the roots it holds ≤ the budget — never tree 0's root or the stamp, KD-SYM-3; a declared region's names ITS tail and roots; each tail rides the region's own pending-reclaim ledger with the barrier epoch, `after_durable_barrier` advances each ring's `reusable_upto`, and tombstone elision is PER RING — `NodeCache::durable_tail_for_slot` / `KvTree::durable_tail`: a leased slot's leaves elide below their lessee's durable tail, ring 0's below the ledger's; the first build's MIN over regions pinned elision at 0 volume-wide whenever a declared region idled). **The declared partition** `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_SYM_APPENDER_SLOTS` (`Kind::Harness`, `<id>:<forest slot>,…;…`) is PR 2's seam for PR 4's lease gate: `build_queued_tx` routes a tx by its records' slot (`region_of_records` — a tx spanning two appenders is PR 6's cross-owner op and refuses), `run_batch` runs the pipeline once per region group reserving in THAT ring (`PassSentinel.ring` / `ConveyorWindow.ring`; the lane completes, prefix-waits and hole-checkpoints per ring — `checkpoint_past_region`), the checkpoint computes ONE TAIL PER REGION (`appender_region_tails`: its head, its open reservations, the floors of ITS slots' leaves — the node cache keeps slot-stamped LEAF dying floors per slot, `take_dying_leaf_floors`, because a leaf's floor is a position in its lessee's ring; interior nodes, tree 0 and every flat node fold into the manager's), and grows a stalled, drained ring by one segment (`grow_stalled_regions`: bitmap bits + barrier, the page naming the grown table + barrier, THEN the swap, under the Dekker pair `passes_inside`/`growing` with `run_batch_group`). **Structure stays the manager's in PR 2**: interior flips and allocator deltas journal into ring 0 (an appender's alloc record is its own only under PR 3's grant — design §5.2.3 owed to PR 3), which is why `AppenderViolation::Lease` names the manager's structure in a content ring and `Extent` any allocator delta outside ring 0; `Key` = one key in two rings (`journal::detect_appender_violations`, window-scoped like its bit-8 predecessor; must-stay-0 gauges `meta_kv_replay_{key,lease,extent}_violations`; a mount that counts one REFUSES). **KD-SYM-10**: `note_flush_ceiling` audits the AGE of a region's oldest dirty slot-tree leaf at the flush pass's covering barrier — `CachedNode::dirty_since_ns`, stamped at the clean → dirty transition of a forest-slot node only (a flat volume's nodes read no clock) — against the checkpoint LANDING ceiling of the cadence in force (`appender_flush_ceiling_ms(flush_interval)` ≡ `checkpoint_landing_ceiling_ms`: the trigger `CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE_MS` + two tick periods, 1,100 ms shipped, resolved at open on `AppenderSet::flush_ceiling_ms` and published as `appender_flush_ceiling_ms`); `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns` is must-stay-0 and a steady stream at the default cadence keeps it 0 (pinned) — the contract moves it under `uring_fs::arm_device_latency` only. The first build measured the pass wall (0 for every violation up to ≈ 2× the bound); the second compared the age against the TRIGGER, which the tick fires AT, so a healthy mount read as overrunning by the pass (review round 2, Issue 22). The §4.4 pt 4 compensation of a FAILED window write journals into the window's OWN ring (`rollback_failed_tx` / `commit_compensation` take it — in ring 0 it was the `Lease` violation the next mount refused on). Growth's and the leave's bitmap writes CONSUME a checkpoint seq (ledger slots are `seq % 32`) so DUR-4's raise log stays a signal — and the mount RESUMES `checkpoint_seq` at `max(ledger.seq, alloc.resume_generation())` (DUR-4's own resume law, true since round 2: the leave's seq is carried by no ledger record, and resuming from the ledger alone made every clean partitioned remount take the raise WARN; tripwire `meta_kv_bitmap_generation_raises`, 0 on a healthy mount); the pass parks on the region's `growth_done` Notify (no poll); a stage-B window counts against growth from its handoff to its terminal outcome (`AppenderRegion::windows_inflight` beside `passes_inside` — a failed window's reservation is completed BEFORE its §4.4 pt 4 compensation reserves on the ring the window holds, so `drained` alone let growth swap that ring; the seam `TEST_CONVEYOR_HOLD_PRE_ROLLBACK` parks the lane in that exact window). Growth needs a DRAINED ring: an appender that saturates its ring without pause stays at its size and every drain rides one tick (`admissible ÷ cadence` per region — the knob is the lever until PR 3's drain-then-grow arm); PR 2 joins at the floor (the EWMA input is PR 3's). **The cadence tick reads every region's ring** (`AppenderSet::ring_pressure` / `rings_uncovered` beside ring 0's shipped law): a committer parked at a full DECLARED ring holds no node lock, so nothing is dirty and ring 0 is idle — read alone, the tick never made a cycle due and nobody advanced that ring's `reusable_upto` (the wedge `a_full_declared_ring_kicks_the_next_cadence_tick_not_the_ceiling` pins: 60 s undrained → ≈ 0.4 s); a region's pressure law is half its ADMISSIBLE window, since the shipped `distance > logical_len/2` is unreachable on a floor ring whose reserve IS half of it; gauge `appender_pressure_cycles` (0 unpartitioned). `squeezefs appenders <sqmeta-uri> [--json]` lists the directory off a probe. Found by the contracts while landing: the page's segment table was re-derived from the ring's page ranges every checkpoint, chopping a declared region's two ring-side pages off its first extent per cycle so recovery read the ring 8 KiB off — the table is maintained at open and growth only; and the live-FUSE leg (`inline_raise_tests` stamped, both kill-9-then-remount-at-`mnt2` contracts) found the identity match narrowed by the mount slot, which made the forest volume the one layout a same-host crash could not remount over — the node-token match above is that fix; and attributing the growth contract's 1-in-20 stamped stall race (a legal schedule — the test's own poller drained faster than the storm filled; the contract now parks the cadence and waits for the stall it asserts) found the tick's ring-0-only read, the wedge above. Owed: the manager's `JoinAppender` + the directory chain's growth (PR 3), extent grants and an appender's own SMOs in its own ring (PR 3), slot leases + `g` + `slot_tree_extents` (PR 4 — written 0), a globally monotone record-seq space across rings (PR 4 — the fold's per-key LWW compares raw ring positions, sound while a slot's records come from one ring per mount), foreign-ring recovery + `Recovering`/`Recovered` + the operator remedy verb (PR 10 — region 0's page must be consulted BEFORE the fixed-ring replay for §5.8.3 to hold there), a reader's per-region window (PR 5's tokens), the squeeze-test A-B-B-A (gate 1), the instrumented fuzz campaign. Stats: `appender_live_pages_at_mount` / `appender_native_slot` joined the family; `appenders_live` counts JOINED regions only.

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 3, the manager lease (2026-09-14, DARK; incompat bit 17)

**PR 3 landed the MANAGER LEASE** on the appender region (`.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr3-manager.md`; `tests/sym_manager_tests.rs` + `tests/sym_fence_tests.rs`; design §5.3.3, §5.3.5, §5.8.1, §5.8.5 C13, §5.9, §6.3, §11; KD-SYM-3/7/13/18/22/23), dark behind the same bit 17 and the same seam — **a bit-17-absent volume is untouched EXCEPT for two shipped-bug fixes** (below). Under bit 17 the writer that wins the D0 ladder IS the volume's **manager** (KD-SYM-3 — an ordinary appender with the `is_manager` bit; `manager_lease` = `held` / `peer:<node token>` / `vacant`, `manager_failover_bound_ms` = `CLIENT_STALE_TTL_SECS` + the ladder's measured wall + the replay's measured wall, DERIVED and published — also `manager_dependency_stall_bound_ms`; a successor that wins the ladder over a dead manager's `Live` page 0 adopts its newer roots at replay). **Extent grants** (`src/meta_backend/kv/slot_state.rs` `extent_grant:{appender}` in tree 0 — the WHOLE grant; the page's `grant` runs = the UNCLAIMED remainder, ≤ `GRANT_RUNS_MAX`; `appender::RegionGrant` in RAM: claim lowest-first, `free_pending` parked on the REGION's tail, `advance_durable` → returnable → `ReturnExtents` at the cadence; `recover(record, page)` at open then the region ring's in-window `alloc`/`free` fold): the manager carves `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_GRANT_EXTENTS` extents (int 8..=65536, derived `clamp(2 × ewma_smo_rate × failover_bound_s, 8, free_heap/(4 × appenders))`, tie-tested) with the alloc deltas + the merged record as ONE ring-0 control entry (`write_control_entry`), refills at 50 % consumption (`grant_cadence`) AND reactively when a flush pass finds the grant empty with the manager reachable; an appender that exhausts its grant while the manager is unreachable (seam `test_set_manager_unreachable`) DEFERS its compactions — records stay in its ring, `manager_dependency_stalls` counts (must-stay-0 at the sized grant), a user commit that needs the extent refuses EAGAIN-class (`KvError::GrantExhausted`), never `ENOSPC` / `heap_full`; closure law `extent_grant_extents ≡ claimed + returned + granted_unclaimed` published as its terms. **PR 2's deviation 3 is REVERSED**: with a grant a declared region's interior flips and allocator deltas journal into ITS OWN ring — `SmoContext` carries a `SmoRegion { ring, grant }` and SCOPES ITSELF by slot through a resolver at EVERY SMO entry point (`smo_replace` / `try_merge_node` / `collapse_root_chain` / the lazy mint), so the flush pass, threshold maintenance, the merge sweep and the defrag arm can never run a leased slot tree's SMO in the manager's ring by omission (the first build missed the split `ctx.alloc.free_pending` in `smo_replace` and a ring-1 gate seq landed in the bitmap's FIFO — the `retire seqs must be non-decreasing` assert now names both seqs and the extent); per-slot dying floors for INTERIOR nodes and root swaps (`NodeCache::note_slot_dying_floor`) clamp the region's tail; the checkpoint folds `unpublished_root_floors()` PER SLOT; the replay detector `detect_appender_violations(rings, leases, granted)` makes an interior record legal for a leased slot and an alloc delta legal inside a grant — PR 2's `Lease` / `Extent` classes are the tripwires that prove the reversal. **The verbs** (`src/meta_ship/manager.rs`): `ManagerCall::{JoinAppender, ExtentGrant, ReturnExtents}` ride the S8 owner service as ONE verb family (`VERB_MANAGER_CALL`, `MANAGER_SCHEMA` 1; `CLUSTER_WIRE_SCHEMA` 4 → 5 — bumped ONCE for this program's wire, KD-7 same-commit fleets; PR 4+ extend the enum), `ManagerService` on the listener's connection lane recording `manager_service_ns` {admit, execute, reply, total} exact-sum + `manager_verbs` / `manager_verbs_per_s` / `manager_load_pct` (MEASURED Σ service ÷ wall) / `manager_verb_replays` / `manager_verb_refusals` (must-stay-0) / `manager_verb_rejected` (review round 1, Issue 2 — "bounded codec = bounded EXECUTION": every wire-carried integer is validated against durable state before anything proportional to it is allocated — a return run must lie inside the volume and `start + len` must not overflow, else the frame is REJECTED with its own status `STATUS_REJECTED`, the buggy/hostile-peer class kept off the must-stay-0 gauge; runs are intersected with the caller's record as INTERVALS so the materialized list is bounded by the record, never the frame; an explicit `ExtentGrant { want }` is CLAMPED to the derivation's cap, `with_capacity` bounded by the free heap); every verb is idempotent against DURABLE state — the joiner's `Live` page is `JoinAppender`'s witness (`Joined{already}` against this manager OR a successor), the grant record `ReturnExtents`' (`already`) — never a RAM dedup window; `KvMetaBackend::manager_join_appender` reuses the lowest `Free` directory page or grows the chain by one extent (header LAST, then the previous header's `next`, each barriered), carves ≤ 8 coalesced ring extents, writes the page `Live` into BOTH directory slots (PR 2's directory-first law), mints the initial grant, and refuses past `appenders_capacity` naming the metadata volume COUNT as the lever (32 joiners on a 512 MiB volume grow the chain past its first extent; the join refusal at open is narrowed to a `Recovering` page or a foreign `Live` page whose id THIS mount's partition claims — a wire joiner's page is a peer); `Joined { already }` answers the joiner's page `grant` — the UNCLAIMED remainder, never the whole record — and a join the manager died inside (page Live, no grant minted; seam `TEST_JOIN_HOLD_AFTER_PAGE`) is COMPLETED by the replay, which mints the owed grant; `ExtentGrant` is idempotent per §5.3.5 — a caller whose unclaimed remainder (an in-process region's RAM grant, a wire joiner's page) already covers the ask is answered it VERBATIM (`manager_verb_replays`), the 50 % refill reaching a fresh carve because a remainder below half the reference is below the derived size; the manager's control entries admit in ring 0's USER class (a verb storm never eats the checkpoint reserve — Issue 15); `appenders_known` (the directory's Live count, wire joiners included) is the cap's appender term (Issue 12). **Round 2's two ring-carve fixes on PR 2's surface (found by the clean-remount grant pin's planted orphan; `sym_appender_tests::a_re_carved_ring_never_replays_its_predecessor_incarnations_entries`)**: a rejoined region's fresh ring took the extents its PREDECESSOR incarnation's ring occupied (lowest-free-first) with that ring's entries still on the device — the replay chain walked past the new head into them (a predecessor `+ref` out-voted an acked release; a predecessor `free` folded into the grant and was RETURNED) — so every carved ring extent is ZEROED before a page names it (`appender::zero_extents`: the rejoin, the wire join, the growth segment); and a fresh ring restarted its position space at 0, so this incarnation's record seqs sat at or below the predecessor's durable ones and per-key LWW resurrected an acked release at replay while the live RAM apply read it gone — the `Free` page now KEEPS the ring's final head as the region's seq-space watermark and the carve CONTINUES from `max(page.head_hint, ring 0's head)` (`JournalRing::new_segments_at`; the §4.1 scan compares the header's low-32-bit lap as the header defines it); the cross-ring case (a lease moving between rings) stays PR 4's globally monotone seq space. **The grant across a CLEAN remount (round 2, Issue 1 — the bug)**: `open_appender_regions` recovers EVERY declared region's RAM grant from tree 0's record (`RegionGrant::recover(record, page.grant)` — a `Free` page names no remainder, so everything the record still names is a live image and lands CLAIMED); before it a non-recovered open built an EMPTY grant against a record naming the region's live images and every later retirement of a pre-remount image was dropped by `free_pending`'s claimed guard — never parked, never returned, invisible to C13 and the closure gauge, one extent leaked per pre-remount image per mount cycle on the ordinary lifecycle; the join's initial grant now keys on `unclaimed() == 0` alone, and the durable-vs-RAM law `record ⊆ claimed ∪ unclaimed ∪ pending ∪ returnable` is pinned after every open (`grant_holds`). A `ReturnExtents` of an in-process region's extent DROPS it from the RAM grant with the return and REFUSES a CLAIMED one (a live image — Issue 11) or a PENDING one (its free is parked on the region's tail; it becomes returnable only through `advance_durable` — round 3, Issue 17; `GrantHeld` names which); the page names the WHOLE unclaimed remainder — a remainder in more runs than `GRANT_RUNS_MAX` moves its smallest runs to the returnable batch (`RegionGrant::trim_to_page_runs`), and a carve is coalesced with the caller's remainder before it is decided, never truncated (Issue 9); a LEASED slot's leaf promises its SMO's extents against the REGION's grant ledger (`NodeDirty::set_promise_ledger` → `RegionGrant::promise_ledger`, `extent_grant_promised`), never `heap_promised` (Issue 10); a FULL heap on a leased slot is the SPACE class — the grant answers `NoSpace`, the flush pass's reactive refill carves in the INTERNAL class (compactions draw to the compaction floor like the manager's own) — for exactly ONE SMO's images (`SMO_IMAGES_MAX` = 4, a split's ≤ 3 parts + a root; the reason on the constant), never the derived grant, so one refill cannot drain the reserve (round 3, Issue 18) — and defers the node on the space class, never `manager_dependency_stalls` (Issue 6). **Round 3's two fixes**: (a) **the clean leave over a final-cycle SMO (Issue 16 — acked-data loss on the dark path, reviewer-reproduced)**: the shutdown fixpoint tested ring 0 alone, so a leased slot tree's SMO in the FINAL flush pass — its records in the REGION's ring, its moved root published to tree 0 only by the next cycle — left the region uncovered while `leave_appender_regions` wrote its page `Free` (roots cleared, tail declared covered, ring released) and the next open routed through the predecessor image; the fixpoint now iterates until ring 0 AND every declared region are covered (`AppenderSet::rings_uncovered`), the final tick's head-stability wait covers EVERY ring — a region window in flight at shutdown is waited for like ring 0's, so the guarantee holds where a slow region write would otherwise burn the bound onto the belt (round 4, Issue 21; pinned with the one-batch schedule: the claim removal handed off first, the region write parked on the device) — and the leave takes ONE coverage verdict at entry (before its own ring-0 control entries) and leaves an uncovered region's page `Live` with its roots and tail, its ring and grant claimed, loud — the next open recovers it as own residue; region 0 is judged by the same law (its page names the manager's guest roots); seam `TEST_SHUTDOWN_FIXPOINT_CYCLES` (total final cycles; `1` = the defective shape) makes the belt observable; pins `a_leased_trees_smo_inside_the_final_cycle_survives_a_clean_leave` (rounds 1..=8 of the shape: every acked base present, nothing pending, C13 empty) + `a_leave_over_an_uncovered_window_keeps_the_page_live_for_the_next_open`. (b) **the return frame's intersection was ∝ frame runs × record extents before its dedup (Issue 2's residual)**: `coalesce_runs` sorts and merges the frame's runs first (bounded by the frame's own run count), `intersect_coalesced_with_record` merge-walks the disjoint runs against the record (strictly ascending output ≤ the record, no dedup step), `already` is counted after the coalesce and adds the inner partition's own count so `cleared + already ≡ named` (Issue 19); `tests/manager_wire_bounds_tests.rs` pins the peak single allocation of a 10K-duplicate × 4,096-extent frame at ≤ 2×frame + 2×record bytes (the product would be 320 MiB) with the `job_wire_bounds_tests` gauge. The mount path's listener wiring is PR 4's first caller (the service + client are contract-proven on their own listener). **KD-SYM-13**: a declared partition or a join on a non-PR substrate REFUSES at open naming `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_ALLOW_NON_PR=1` (bool, default 0 — the LOUD lab opt-in, "detection-grade" announced at every mount using it); a solo forest mount arms nothing and needs no opt-in. **§5.8.1 WERO on the metadata namespace**: with the plane armed on a PR substrate, `SQUEEZEFS_META_PR_WERO` (bool, default 1) makes the manager's hold rtype 3 (`meta_pr_wero` = 1; `decide_meta_fence_posture` at open, `rsv_acquire`/preempt/release rtype-aware) and every joining appender a REGISTRANT (`data_custody::join_wero_as_registrant`, the standing hold one namespace class over); `=0` = the shipped rtype 1, refused on a PR substrate without the opt-in; one configured identity per mount (`SQUEEZEFS_HOSTNQN`/`SQUEEZEFS_HOSTID` pair-or-neither), no fencing groups, no derived identities, no target-ACL probing; co-located appenders sharing one registrant are their own guarantee row (KD-SYM-22: `flock` + `T_self` + the frame screen). **SHIPPED-BUG FIX 1 (KD-SYM-18, every layout)**: `src/meta_backend/reservation.rs` read the Reservation Report into a FIXED 4 KiB buffer, silently truncating at the 63rd extended registrant — the read is now two-step SIZED BY REGCTL (`report_len_for`; header first, then `header + 64 × REGCTL`, re-read once if the count grew) on both namespace classes, `parse_reservation_report` is total; the registrant cap is DECLARED (`SQUEEZEFS_PR_REGISTRANT_CAP`, int 1..=65535, default unset) or LEARNED from the device — only from a REGISTER refused by a device-answered NVMe status in the command-specific or vendor-specific class (`NvmeStatusError` typed on the passthru; never a transport errno such as EIO/ENXIO/ENOTTY, never a generic, transient or path-related status — `nvme_status_is_transient`), and only when the SAME status REPEATS at the same registrant count (one refusal is a candidate); a REGISTER that later succeeds at or past the learned count UNLEARNS it (review round 1, Issue 5; `learn_registrant_cap`, min) — `registrant_cap_gate` refuses a join LOUD at or past it naming the count, the namespace and the remedy; gauges `pr_registrants_per_namespace` / `pr_report_bytes` (per device path), `pr_registrant_cap` (0 = unbounded — nvmet's posture), `pr_registrant_cap_refusals` (0 on nvmet by construction). **SHIPPED-BUG FIX 2 (every layout — found by the C13 crash window on the FLAT leg, binding fact 1)**: a ROOT SWAP journals no pointer record, so a kill between the swap (the D4 compaction nudge, the merge sweep's root collapse, a threshold-maintenance root swap) and the next ledger/page/tree-0 record left the mount replaying through the PREDECESSOR — live again — while Option A's mount gate PARKED the swap's in-window `free(old)`; the bring-up cover's tail then released the LIVE root's extent and the next claim (lowest-free-first) overwrote the INODES root with a fresh node image (the probe read `root at remount: ext 0, pending 0, old_alloc false`). `ExtentAllocator::load` now DEFERS every replayed park (bits set, FIFO untouched) and the mount pushes them once every tree is open and adopted — `park_replayed_frees(&live_roots)`, before the first post-mount SMO — DROPPING a free whose extent a mounted root names (`meta_kv_replay_root_frees_dropped`, WARN per drop; `RegionGrant::unpark_live_roots` is the grant's law); the successor image stays claimed-and-unrouted — reclaimed by C13 on an appender GRANT; the manager's own swaps (tree 0, the native slot tree, every slot it holds) and every flat swap are the bounded leak class until a bitmap-vs-reachability census exists (no detector). The design's §5.3.4 "old image parked (coverage gate)" row is corrected; the two allocator unit contracts + the K4 R3 fallback harness + the wedge-remount pin now run the mount body's second step (the R3 harness and the wedge pin had encoded the defect's own shape). **fsck C13 — orphan image extent** (§5.8.5; `KvMetaBackend::c13_orphan_image_extents` / `c13_return_orphan`; `KvTree::reachable_node_addrs`): every grant-CLAIMED extent no tree root reaches, censused under the SMO mutex AND the forest's mint guard (`SlotTrees::mint_guard`) so an SMO's successor before its route flip and a lazy mint's root before the forest names it — the live in-window image — are structurally never candidates and a retired image parked on its tail is a pending-free, not a claim; its OWN pass beside C8 (unsharded, per volume; the class needs NODE reachability the record census does not read — one paged walk of the interior population, skipped when no grant holds a claim), ONE fresh census per volume in the confirm pass; repair `return-orphan-image-extent` = the appender FREES the orphan in ITS ring gated on its tail (the SMO retirement shape — a direct `ReturnExtents` would leave the in-window `alloc` outside the rewritten record = the `Extent` violation for the next mount), the cadence returns it; `fsck_repair_classC13`. **The fuzz target** `manager_call_frame` (three arms — the raw bytes, the constructive frame, and the SERVICE EDGE over a decoded call's integers: `validate_return_runs` / `coalesce_runs` / `intersect_coalesced_with_record` / `clamp_grant_want` against a small record) + the proptest mirror (`manager_call_decoders_never_panic`, `manager_{request,reply}_frame_round_trips`, `manager_service_edge_is_bounded_by_durable_state`). **Stats**: the Manager family (`manager_lease`, `meta_pr_wero`, `manager_failover_bound_ms`, `manager_dependency_stall_bound_ms`, `manager_dependency_stalls`, `extent_grants`, `extent_grant_extents`, `extent_returns`, `extent_grant_{claimed,returned,unclaimed}`, `manager_vol0_unreachable`, `manager_verbs`, `manager_verbs_per_s`, `manager_load_pct`, `manager_verb_replays`, `manager_verb_refusals`, `manager_verb_rejected`, `appenders_known`, `extent_grant_promised`, `manager_service_ns`), the Fencing family (`pr_registrants_per_namespace`, `pr_report_bytes`, `pr_registrant_cap`, `pr_registrant_cap_refusals`), `meta_kv_replay_root_frees_dropped`, `fsck_repair_classC13` — `vacant` / 0 on every flat mount. **The matrix**: `tests/run_sym_forest_suites.sh` grew `sym_manager_tests` + `sym_fence_tests` + `kv_smo_crash_completeness_tests` (25 suites, flat THEN stamped; the crash-completeness suite's three flat-shaped pins were made layout-blind — the ONE locator, arming by slot, the §2-A law read off the counters). Owed: slot leases + `g` + `slot_tree_extents` (PR 4), the mount-path listener wiring and a globally monotone record-seq space (PR 4), the appender's persisted commit-rate EWMA as the ring-size input, foreign-ring recovery + `Recovering`/`Recovered` + the operator remedy verb (PR 10 — region 0's page must be read BEFORE the fixed-ring replay for §5.8.3), `manager_vol0_unreachable`'s wired arm (PR 8/10), the instrumented fuzz run (nightly), the box bracket (gate 1 / PR 13-14).

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 4, slot leases (2026-09-14, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 4 landed SLOT LEASES** on PR 3's manager (`.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr4-slot-leases.md`; `tests/sym_slot_transfer_tests.rs` + the loom model `slot_lease_models`; design §5.1, §5.3.4 rows 5–8, §5.3.5, §5.4.1, §5.9, §11; KD-SYM-1/2/4/5/11/16/17), dark behind bit 17 AND the registered knob **`SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META`** (bool, default 0 — **`=0` is the shipped posture EXACTLY**: the PR 1–3 forest, every slot the mount's, the shared mint rotor, `dlm_mode` = `solo`, no plane; `=1` on a bit-17-ABSENT volume refuses the writer's open naming `enable-symmetric`; pinned). Under the plane **the slot is the ownership unit** (§5.1.1): the D0 winner IS the manager and leases its native slot plus `M` rotor slots through its own `AcquireSlots` (`M = clamp(W/(2 × writers_known), 1, MINT_SPREAD)`; `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_MINT_SLOTS` 1..=64 explicit-wins), every lease = tree 0 `Leased { appender_id, g, page_addr, root, cursor, slot_tree_extents }` (`slot_state` record v2; `Unleased` gained `last_written`) + a `Live` page entry `(slot, g, slot_tree_extents, root, cursor)` — PR 2's `g = 0` / `slot_tree_extents = 0` made REAL (the `SlotExtentLedger` counts every slot tree's images off the lessee's own alloc/free) — **two durable homes at every instant**; `g` moves once per grant, never at a release or replay. **The core is `src/slot_lease_core.rs`** (`#[path]`-shared into `loom-models/`): `LeaseGate` (dense atomic bitsets of leased / releasing slots — the node cache's THIRD gate state, read by `apply_locked` UNDER the node's write lock: `meta_kv_leaf_lease_refusals` must-stay-0, the §5.4.1 partition law's belt behind the commit DOOR — review round 2, Issue 6: every commit takes a door TOKEN per slot (`LeaseGate::enter`, a `SeqCst`-fenced Dekker pair with `begin_release`) owned by its queue entry to the terminal outcome; a release drains the slot's tokens before its flush; a slot mid-handover PARKS at the door (`slot_door_parks`), a foreign slot refuses the typed `KvError::SlotBusy` = EAGAIN (`slot_door_refusals`, "ship to the holder"), an unleased one is acquired first-touch with a PARKING admission — the belt is reached by no legal schedule, where the round-1 door passed a `Releasing` slot and every commit on the departing holder during a handover failed EINVAL), `SlotLeaseTable` (`Unleased → Leased → Offered → Releasing → Unleased`, one holder per slot, every transition idempotent against tree 0's witness — §5.3.5), `DominanceWindow` (ops over ONE common `T_idle` window, bounded per-requester table), the derivations (`mint_slots_derived`, `affinity_ceiling_bytes`, `n_floor`, `handover_cold_start_ns` — tie-tested); five loom models — 1 and 5 weakening-verified memory-ordering evidence (the gate's verdict INSIDE the node lock, `Releasing` raised BEFORE the flush takes any node lock; the door's fenced Dekker pair), 2–4 serializability pins of the table's law through its one mutex (stated so in the module). **Minting is the bounded parent-slot affinity** (KD-SYM-11/16, `RoutedMetaBackend::pick_mint_slot_for` → `lease_mint_choice`): a child lands in its parent's slot under `A_max(t) = max(used_leaf_bytes/64, node_size)` (soft, `affinity_a_max_bytes`; `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_AFFINITY_MAX_MB` static, floor 1 MiB clamped to `[node_size, heap]`), else the rotor slot with the most headroom (`affinity_ceiling_spills`; SIGNED headroom so over-cap trees equalize), all rotors STRICTLY over the cap grant one more up to `2 × M` (`affinity_ceiling_overflows`; exact equality never does — a solo mount stays at 64), past that the smallest rotor tree; bit-12 ino lanes are retired for metadata under bit 17 — the SLOT's cursor is the mint's and no lane cell exists on an armed mount (pinned). **Handover = flush-then-transfer** (KD-SYM-4, `release_slot_handover`, serialized per volume; the accept runs release + grant under ONE hold of the mutex): gate `Releasing` → the door drained → `checkpoint_now` cycles until the region's `reusable_upto` passes the slot's RECORD FRONTIER (the node cache's per-slot `slot_frontiers`: the pass notes its batch end per slot-stamped union node, an SMO its entry's end, the install the ring head) and the root is published — a post-condition bounded at 64, loud on a stuck tail (Issue 11; the departing ring's window is CLEAR of the slot before any other home says it moved — PR 2's `Lease` detector judges the window by tree 0's lessee at the next open) → the page `Releasing { root, cursor, g }` + barrier → `ReleaseSlot` (tree 0 `Unleased { …, last_written, seq_floor, tails }`, one control entry, the table's verdict consulted BEFORE the write — Issue 3; `tails` complete over the tree — Issue 12) → the page without the slot; the requester's `AcquireSlot` grants at `g + 1` with the live root; `slot_handover_phase_ns` exact-sum. **The record-seq space is monotone across rings (review round 2, Issue 2 — SAFETY)**: every ring stamps `position + seq_offset`; a release records the departing ring's stamp frontier as the slot's `seq_floor` (tree 0 + the wire words); a grant raises the receiving ring's offset above it (`raise_seq_floor`); the offset is durable on the appender page (`seq_offset`, the fixed part 314) and recovered as `max(page, the window's own stamps)`; `dlm_mode` = `slot-homed`; a solo mount has no foreign slot so `dlm_rpcs` stays 0 by construction) and the leave (or the plane's drop) withdraws it — `solo` once the last contributor is gone; **`SlotHolderCache`** (`src/slot_holder_cache.rs`, `ArcSwap<HashMap>`) is tree 0's lessee projection, one entry learnt per grant / forgotten per release — `slot_resolve_rpcs` counts served `ResolveSlot` verbs (0 solo). **The manager's legal answers ride their own gauges** (Issue 14 — `manager_verb_refusals` counts witness contradictions alone): `slot_acquire_refusals` (an explicit ask for a held slot — `SlotBusy`), `slot_rotor_cap_refusals` (`KvError::RotorAtCap`; Found by the round's large-tree fixtures on PR 3/4 surfaces: the departing holder's own flush passes the `Releasing` gate (`apply_locked_structural` — every tree with pending splits at its handover failed the release), and a leased leaf's split wider than the one-SMO constant is refilled to ITS need (`GrantExhausted { needed }`, `SmoContext::check_grant_covers`, the INTERNAL class unclamped by the steady-state cap — the constant ask was re-answered verbatim for ever and every commit parked at the region's full ring escalated through D1.b). **Review round 4**: the SMO task's moves read `LeaseGate::verdict_structural` — a slot mid-handover is the departing holder's flush, an UNLEASED slot's tree is the MANAGER's to maintain (KD-SYM-2/3; `arm_as_manager`), a slot another appender leases refuses (the `foreign` bitset) — and the merge sweep / heap-full recovery / D4 arm / census walk ONE filtered population (`maintainable_trees`, `merge_sweep_foreign_skips`; the lap keyed by tree identity); `PageRead::ForeignLayout` — a valid-checksum page of another layout refuses the four-slot read and the OPEN (`reformat required`), never reads as absent; `slot_tails()` grows its `Vec` per bounds-checked run; the door's pre-admission is an RAII `HeldAdmission`; found by the pins: `dead_bset_census` resolves a leaf's tree by id AND slot (every slot tree's id is 0), and `JoinAppender` never reuses a Free page whose id a released in-process region still holds. The release's cold-tree I/O floor (≈ 65 k extent reads at the operating point) is priced in design §5.8.2 with the header tail hint owed. **Review round 5 (Issue 28 — the structural door)**: a grant to any appender but the manager takes the per-volume SMO mutex BEFORE `manager_verbs` (the checkpoint task's own order — the `foreign` bit joined the maintainer filter only AFTER a wire grant's durable write, and `maintainable_trees` is a per-pass snapshot, so a grant racing the manager's sweep on an UNLEASED tree either landed the SMO on a slot tree 0 now leased elsewhere — records in ring 0 judged by tree 0's FINAL lessee at the next open, the `Lease` violation; a root published nowhere — or reached the belt on a legal schedule); the pass holds the mutex for its whole pass, so no SMO on the slot is in flight when the lease moves and no pass spans the grant (the re-check-before-apply alternative leaves the SMO built before the grant in the window); no lock-free token, so no loom model owed; pin ×10 stamped. **Review round 6 (Issues 29/30 — the wire words' screen)**: every slot word a wire `ReleaseSlot` carries is validated against DURABLE / derived state BEFORE any RAM or durable effect (PR 3's bounded-execution law for the slot words — round 5 had made `seq_floor` act on ring 0's seq space immediately, so one bad frame near `u64::MAX` would have saturated every later stamp and made the next replay skip every window record): `appender::screen_release_words` (pure, fuzzed by `manager_call_frame`'s poisoned-frame arm + the proptest mirror) — `seq_floor` strictly above the grant's floor and ≤ `release_seq_floor_bound` (the appender's page `seq_offset` or the highest floor this manager granted it + 1, + `head_hint` + 2 × its ring length, capped at `SEQ_FRONTIER_SANE_MAX` = `u64::MAX / 2`), `cursor` ≥ the grant's and ≤ `GUEST_NS_BASE`, `slot_tree_extents` ≤ the volume, `root` the grant's verbatim or a node-aligned heap address inside the appender's `extent_grant` record whose node (`node::load_node`, one extent read — round 7 widened the 4 KiB header read to the node so its RECORDS name the released slot, Issue 32) carries the word's `node_seq`;
