the service classifies EVERY verb's `Rejected` as `STATUS_REJECTED` (before, `ReturnExtents` alone). Issue 30: the clearing loop's bound is a retryable `KvError::GrantDeferred` (EAGAIN, `slot_grant_deferrals`) when the tail moved and `Corrupt` only when it did not; the four 64-cycle loops share `checkpoint::COVER_CYCLES_MAX` = 8 × `PENDING_FREE_FORCE_CYCLES` (tie-tested); the manager's own release raises nothing on ring 0. **Review round 7 (Issues 31–33)**: the derived `seq_floor` bound presumes the appender's OWN checkpoint writes its page (`head_hint` ≥ its head, `seq_offset` in force, `ckpt_seq` ≥ 1 — PR 12's obligation, stated in its row); a page no appender checkpoint ever wrote (`ckpt_seq == 0`, every PR-4 wire appender's) bounds the floor by `SEQ_FRONTIER_SANE_MAX` alone, so a stale join-time hint never refuses a legitimate release (pinned: a floor 64 ring lengths past the hint lands); the root witness reads the node's first routable record for the SLOT (`node_records_slot` — a holder of A and B releasing A with B's root is rejected); `GrantDeferred` is TYPED on the wire (`ManagerReply::Deferred`, `STATUS_DEFERRED` = 50; the client's `acquire_slots` maps it to EAGAIN, `acquire_slot` passes it through); and the Issue-25 pin's witness moved from a process-global `META_KV_NODE_MERGES` delta (a legal earlier cadence pass merged the pair first — ~8 % red on the matrix's stamped leg) to the TREE under a parked cadence — the sweep's own `report.merges`, slot 4's node count and underfull census before/after — ×20 stamped green; the Issue-22 pin's cycle-count delta runs under a parked cadence for the same reason.
### Symmetric metadata program — PR 11, the real bit-17 stamp (2026-09-14, DARK; incompat bit 17)

**PR 11 landed the two PRODUCT arms that stamp bit 17** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr11-convert.md`; `tests/sym_convert_tests.rs` — 36 contracts (+ the `#[ignore]`d §5b handover), in `run_sym_forest_suites.sh`'s list and layout-blind by construction: its source volumes format FLAT with the seam cleared, the verb is what stamps; design §6.2, §7.1–§7.3, §5.1.8), beside the test seam — so the §7.1 sentence "the ONLY way a volume carries bit 17 is the seam" is FALSE since this rung: **the seam, `format --symmetric`, or `squeezefs volume enable-symmetric`, each an explicit act, all dark until PR 14.** `format --symmetric` (`ImageBuilder::set_symmetric`, `format_v3_stamped_symmetric`) is the seam-stamped builder path made the flag's second caller — ONE code path, so the flag and the seam build byte-identical images (pinned whole-image); it is the multi-writer class plus the forest and CONFLICTS with `--single-writer` (clap, pinned through the binary). A default `format` stamps no bit and names no directory, and the flat `ImageBuilder` image of a fixed description digests to the pre-PR-11 builder's value (pinned, both format classes). **`enable-symmetric` is an OFFLINE conversion driven by DURABLE STATE, never a progress cursor** (`config_ops::enable_symmetric_with`): the design row's "whole-tx per batch" is honoured at its atomic points, not as a journaled key-range move — a journal entry is interpreted by the volume's LAYOUT BIT, so one transaction cannot both delete a flat record and insert its forest form; instead every volume gets a **per-volume `sym_upgrade:` marker** (ino 1, `SYM_UPGRADE_MARKER_XATTR`, the mw marker's shape; read by ONE `getxattr` in `KvMetaBackend::open` BEFORE the claim gate — at most one extra leaf read per writer open, since the claim gate reads the same ino-1 xattr tree next; a refused writable open writes nothing, no claim tx, no checkpoint, which is exactly what keeps every window resumable; `open_for_sym_upgrade` is the verb's one tolerant door; readers/probes/co-writers proceed), **Everything that can refuse runs BEFORE the first marker, and the marker is the verb's first write** (review round 2, Issue 1 — the bug: a volume roughly half full took its marker and failed `NoSpace` mid-build, `--resume` failing identically with no abort — writer-unmountable until `format --force`): the live-client gate, the discovery, **the verb's own D0 flock on every volume** (`VerbFlocks` — held for the verb's duration, released only around its guarded opens; a concurrent invocation or a mount that began after the gate refuses at Layer A, and two invocations started together never double-build — pinned), then ONE write-free inspection per volume through a probe: the layout bit and the marker, the stamp, the bit-8 record, the replay window (a flat volume not cleanly unmounted refuses naming the mount that fixes it — reachable, nothing is marked yet), intents, jobs, the record collection (the codec's slot-range refusals), the census, and the **capacity preflight**: the forest is written into FRESH extents while the shared trees stay claimed until the end, so the build's peak claim is `needed = Σ_slot nodes(slot records) + nodes(tree 0) + 1 (directory)` — `TreeWriter::planned_nodes`, the writer's OWN chunking level by level, the marker planned in as the native slot's record — against `available = free + reclaimable orphans − compaction_floor(reserve)` (the formula on the line; the tie `extents_needed ≡ extents_written` pinned exactly); a short volume refuses naming the volume, the numbers and the remedy, `--dry-run` reports the shortfall in its row. **`--abort`** undoes a crashed run on every volume still FLAT under its marker (the flat trees verified by the digest walk AND the kinds-6/7 set oracle, the aborted build's extents reclaimed, ONE ledger record restating the flat roots with the reclaimed bitmap generation and a node-seq watermark raised above every stamp the reclaimed residue carries — Issue 4's law on the abort path, pinned: a later flat mount's new nodes all sit above it — the marker removed; the pre-verb digest, `claimed ≡ reachable`, writer-mountable; pinned at the three pre-stamp seams) and refuses the WHOLE set, undoing nothing, when any volume is past its flip (the forest is its truth; `--resume` is the exit) OR when a volume's DUR-5 backup copy already carries bit 17 under a flat sector 0 (`superblock::read_backup_superblock` — the kill between the stamp's two sector writes; an abort would leave the stale stamped copy for a later sector-0 failure to fall back onto, `--resume` rewrites both; pinned by planting the stamped copy). Then per volume in canonical order: **quiesce** under the D0 ladder (guarded open → `checkpoint_now` → clean shutdown ⇒ an EMPTY window, ASSERTED by a recovery scan — a live foreign writer is refused by the ladder, never converted under), **read** every live record of every kind FOLDED through a probe (the digest oracle's own walk, so the forest digests EQUAL by construction over the USER kinds — `digest_backend` walks kinds 1/2/3; kinds 6 and 7 are proven by the order-independent per-kind oracle `builder::digest_backend_kind_set` — `(count, Σ xxh3(record))`, a SET because the forest's kind-routed walk is slot-major — pinned equal across the relayout beside it), **build** the forest into FRESH extents at seq 0 (checkpoint-covered by construction; the ring's SEQ SPACE is KEPT — see the hybrid ledger) — one mixed-kind slot tree per slot the records name and one EMPTY slot tree per hosted slot whose stamp carries a cursor but whose inos were all deleted (Issue 5: tree 0 is the cursor's durable home without the stamp, so PR 4's `max(...)` floor sees it — pinned `a_slot_emptied_before_conversion_never_remints_after_it`), tree 0 with `Unleased { root, cursor: max(stamp cursor, max live local ino + 1), g: 0, tails: [] }` per guest slot (§5.1.8: a cursor is never lowered), the appender directory extent + header, the fixed journal extent ZEROED and appender 0's `Free` page in its first slot, the bitmap at a new generation — then **ONE ledger write** naming the flat roots AND tree 0 + the native root (the QUIESCED TAIL `T` kept — found by the `--abort` contracts: a tail of 0 restarted the ring's seq space below the flat trees' records, so a flat write in the window before the stamp — `--resume`'s quiesce, `--abort`'s marker removal — lost every per-key LWW fold: the marker's Delete survived as the marker, an interior child-pointer update as a routing hole; both layouts now resume their ring at `T` over the zeros, the PR-3 re-carve law; a flat open still finds its trees and reads the zeroed ring as empty — the appender page in page 0 is not a journal page), **ONE sector write** stamping bit 17 + `appender_dir` (`superblock::set_symmetric_forest` — the layout flips onto a ledger that already describes it; the marker rode the build into the native slot tree, so the forest open finds it), the **old trees freed** (reachable from the flat roots the hybrid ledger still names, walked through a bare `NodeCache`, `release_unpublished` per extent, the bitmap written, a ledger record naming the forest roots ALONE folding them out — repeatable until that record lands), and the **marker removed** through the forest's own guarded open (its join, checkpoint, leave). The resume reads each volume's state off the bit, the ledger's roots and the marker: flat + marker ⇒ rebuild (the pre-build **bitmap-vs-reachability census** reclaims the crashed build's extents first — pinned: a resumed volume has EXACTLY the free extents of a straight conversion — and floors the writer's node seqs above every stamp those extents' residue carries, `node::residue_seq_ceiling`: a census-reclaimed extent was never freed under a ledger record, so the `node_seq_watermark` law does not cover it and a fresh node minted at a colliding seq would adopt a dead frame as its own tail — Issue 4, pinned: the orphaned images sit ABOVE the mounted watermark and every node the resumed volume reaches is stamped above them); stamped + old roots ⇒ free; stamped + marker only ⇒ drop it. Refusals FIRST (already symmetric; a bit-8 NON-SOLO partition record → "mount solo once"; open `xv_*` intents; in-flight `job:` records; live clients; a marker without `--resume` / `--abort` — a crash must be acknowledged; `--resume` / `--abort` with nothing to resume / abort); `--dry-run` plans and writes nothing (pinned: the fixed region byte-identical); the bit-8 remedy is pinned true (a solo mount re-checkpoints the ledger in solo form, the verb then converts); `set-owners` assignments and solo bit-8 suffixes are reported DROPPED (§7.3). Sets: every volume in one invocation (the 4-volume set is the 46-volume shape); a half-converted set refuses writable mounts naming the first volume still under its marker. **SHIPPED-BUG FIX (every layout; found by the census, pinned red-first as `a_clean_flat_unmount_leaves_no_claimed_extent_its_roots_do_not_reach`)**: the census found claimed-but-unreachable extents on every cleanly unmounted flat volume, and idle mount/unmount cycles GREW the count — `after_durable_barrier` → `advance_durable` releases the pending frees a cycle's tail covers AFTER that cycle wrote its bitmap pages, dirtying them for the NEXT cycle, and the shutdown fixpoint converged on ring coverage (`head == reusable_upto`) alone, so the final cycle's releases never landed and the retired images read CLAIMED for ever (proportional to the final flush pass's SMOs: 6 extents on a 60-file volume, 43 on a 50 k-file one, +1 per idle mount cycle, cumulative over a volume's life; the class AGENTS.md called "the bounded flat leak … until a bitmap-vs-reachability census exists" had a second, clean-unmount face). The fixpoint now also converges on `ExtentAllocator::has_dirty_pages()` (`checkpoint.rs`, inside the existing bound and the `TEST_SHUTDOWN_FIXPOINT_CYCLES` seam; with nothing dirty the extra cycle journals no SMO, so it converges in one) — a clean unmount of either layout leaves `claimed ≡ reachable` (+ the directory extent on a forest), and the conversion's `orphans_reclaimed` reads 0 on a volume this binary unmounted. **FINDING, NOT FIXED (outside the surface; every layout)**: the live-FUSE leg's first storm — `write(file)` then an immediate `rename(file)` — panicked the conveyor pass on the same-key co-queue exclusion (`Delta`/`Put` on one inode key from two batch members) on flat, fresh-`--symmetric` and converted volumes alike: the production path `RoutedMetaBackend::rename` (`src/meta_backend/mod.rs`, the `lock_many` over the two parents and the two names) → `KvMetaBackend::routed_rename_local` (`backend.rs`, the `stage_delta(TREE_INODES, inode_key(src), ctime)` and its `dest_local` twin) locks the two parents and the two names but **never `I{moved ino}`** (the trait-impl `rename` shares the hole), while it stages the moved inode's ctime `Delta` — and the file's staged-writeback publish holds `I{ino}` and stages a `Put` of the same inode; a debug binary fails the batch (EIO), a release binary can lose the rename's ctime to a later-applied `Put`. Fix direction (lookup → one `lock_many` incl. `I{moved}` → revalidate) is in the note's §5b, and the in-process repro is the `#[ignore]`d contract `rename_and_a_layout_publish_of_one_ino_never_co_queue_a_delta_and_a_put_unguarded` (un-ignore with the fix); the suite's storm fsyncs before it renames and names the finding. `tests/sym_convert_fuse_tests.rs` is the live-FUSE leg (mount-class, in the require-mount gate): flat format → populate through a mount (staged + striped files, link, rename, unlink) → clean unmount → `--dry-run` + the verb through the binary → forest mount reads every byte back → storm → `fsck --json` clean. Memory: one volume's live records are held in RAM during its relayout (the streaming form is owed). The design's §6.2 row and PR-plan row 11 carry the as-built deviations; `docs/operations.md` has the operator procedure ("Converting an existing set to the symmetric forest").

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 7, pack per slot, the refcount probe, the shared-block index, gather mode (2026-09-15, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 7 landed the DATA plane's answer to the forest** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr7-refs.md`; `tests/sym_pack_tests.rs` (8) + `tests/sym_shared_refs_tests.rs` (15) + the loom model `shared_ref_core`; design §5.4.3 laws 1–2, §5.4.4, §5.7.5, §5.8.5 C16, §11; KD-SYM-8, risk R6), dark behind bit 17 AND PR 4's knob — **an unarmed forest and every flat mount take the shipped clone, free and pack paths verbatim** (pinned: the flat ledger keys the GLOBAL owner byte-identically, PK2's one pack scope per data volume, the plain clone with every gauge 0, the gather xattr ignored). **A PR-1 defect, fixed on every forest volume (armed or not)**: the forest codec routes a block reference by the slot bits of the owner ino at offset 17 (the `(s+1) << 40 | local` LOCAL KEY form, the convention every PR 2–4 contract drove the backend with), but every product site keyed the owner's GLOBAL ino (the rung-19 `refs_owner` law), whose top bits are zero for any realistic ino — so through the routed layer every reference landed in the manager's NATIVE slot tree whatever slot its owner lived in: complete as a census (the union over every tree), wrong for the plane (a publish from another appender named the manager's slot — the cross-region refusal) and fatal to the pack law's probe (pinned red on `6f8d44e6` by `a_layout_publish_through_the_routed_layer_lands_its_reference_in_the_owners_slot_tree`). `RoutedMetaBackend::forest_ref_ops` — the layer that owns `route_ino` — rewrites a forest volume's reference owners to their local key form at every ref-op entry point (the recompute paths' `refs_owner` too), `enable-symmetric` re-keys the references it converts under the stamp's width + native slot (`shared_refs::local_key_owner`), and the kind-6 set oracle (`digest_backend_kind_set`) folds a forest's owners back to the global form (`global_owner`) so the relayout contracts stay layout-blind; a flat volume takes the ops verbatim. **Pack per `(writer, slot, data volume)`** (law 1): `Packer` keys its open packs by `(data volume, scope)`, scope = the tenant's `(meta volume, forest slot)` on an armed set (`routing::pack_scope_key` / `DataRouter::pack_scope_of` — the native forest slot is `0` on EVERY meta volume, so the slot alone pooled two volumes' natives on a converted set; the compaction mover scopes its re-pack by the tenant) and `0` everywhere else — a pack block's tenants are inos of ONE slot, so its references live in ONE slot tree and the one-slot probe is exact there (pinned across a handover and a crash: `a_pack_block_whose_slot_is_handed_over_keeps_its_whole_population_in_one_tree`); open packs per data volume ≤ leased slots being written (≤ 64 rotor + affinity on a solo mount, ≤ 108 with inherited slots), the seal residue on `pack_blocks_sealed_dismount` / `pack_open_scopes`. **The on-demand refcount probe** (law 2): `KvMetaBackend::block_ref_probe(vol_tag, block_idx, slot)` = one `06 ‖ vol ‖ blk` range in ONE slot tree (`SlotTrees::refs_probe`), `block_ref_probes` the gauge — **the free list stays the ONE by-block scan's derivation at mount (§5.5.1's allocation bitmap is PR 8's — landed, and PR 8's arm SEEDS it from this scan's population; the mount-time by-block scan seeds the RAM refcount map on every writer mount on BOTH layouts today, and the bitmap replacing it as the terminal-free engine — "a forest mount's open performs zero by-block census reads" — is owed to PR 12/14)**; the RAM refcount map stays the free-list / cursor / pin engine and the two decisions read the durable truth beside it — **the W1 sole-owner patch's durable clause** (`DataRouter::sole_owner_durably`, ONE call at the patch site after `begin_patch_sole_owner`): on an armed set the RAM verdict is confirmed by one probe of the patching ino's slot tree run BEFORE the incarnation retire — exactly one reference and NONE of them SHARED (`RefProbe`; the durable flag is the authority, the RAM mark an accelerator a mark that landed elsewhere never set) — so a reference a PREDECESSOR lessee committed into a handed-over tree, which this mount's RAM map never saw, or a sole reference under a durable SHARED bit, refuses the patch into the CoW fallback (`patch_ineligible_shared`), unarmed `true` without a read (pinned both ways: `the_w1_predicate_confirms_sole_ownership_in_the_inos_slot_tree_on_an_armed_set`). An UNSHARED block's terminal free stays the RAM count's verdict (`free_block` carries no ino; PR 8's bitmap is that decision's input). **The shared-block index + the clone protocol** (§5.4.4; `src/meta_backend/kv/shared_refs.rs`, a child module of `backend` re-exported at `kv::shared_refs` so its executors reach the tx/guard/control-entry primitives without widening them): a block referenced from two slots arises only through clone/reflink; on an armed set `DataRouter::clone_file` runs the protocol over the entries the dest map SHARES with the source (never over a staged source's spill copy — a fresh block under a full ring, nothing to share; round-1 Issue 2 aborted every such clone `ENOENT`) and only across two slot trees (different slots, or a source reference already SHARED — a clone of a clone; a same-slot clone of an unshared block is the shipped clone verbatim): `MarkShared` at the source's holder through the routed door (`RoutedMetaBackend::mark_block_refs_shared` — the cutover / delegation / S9 gates, then `mark_block_refs_shared` — the SHARED bit, **bit 1 of the block-ref value** (`BLOCK_REF_FLAG_SHARED`; `BlockRefOp.shared`, `taken_shared`), set on every source record in ONE tx under F's 4a guard; `Gone` ⇒ the clone aborts `ENOENT`-class with every pin undone), `ShareBlock` at the **index home** (`share_block` — tree-0 `shared_ref:` records keyed `prefix ‖ vol_tag ‖ block_idx ‖ GLOBAL owner ‖ block_index` for BOTH inos, ONE control entry through `write_control_entry`; idempotent), then publishes the dest's references WITH the bit (a clone of a clone inherits it) — the ordering law. **The home is resolved behind ONE function** (`shared_refs::index_home_volume()` = volume 0's manager in PR 7 — the design's kind `0x09` in the allocation holder's slot tree is PR 8's re-point; `TREE_SHARED_INDEX = 9` stays reserved and unwritten); the three verbs `ManagerCall::{MarkShared, ShareBlock, ReleaseShared}` ride the manager wire as the PR-7 block at the END of the enum (the design's 0x70–0x7F range names the BLOCK — bincode carries the variant index; `MANAGER_SCHEMA` unchanged) with `ManagerClient::{mark_shared, share_block, release_shared}` for PR 12's non-manager; the wire service takes no GC verdict (it sees one volume's ledger), the routed executor (`DataRouter::release_shared_at`) is the GC arm. **A terminal free of a SHARED block is never decided locally**: `BlockAllocator` carries the per-block SHARED mark (`shared_ref_core`'s word — `mark_shared` / `clear_shared` / `is_shared` / `shared_blocks`), seeded from the home's index at the mount's arm (`DataRouter::arm_shared_refs`, after `set_meta_backend` in the mount path; a no-op unarmed) and set by `MarkShared`; `BackendRouter::free_block_verdict` sends a marked block's release to the home (`ReleaseShared`, keyed on the releasing `(ino, block_index)`, with the GC arm — an entry whose ino holds no reference is dropped, the "cloner died after step 2" window) — `Held` releases ONE RAM reference never to 0 (`release_shared_held`; a holder the RAM map never seeded may stand behind the count), `Freed` / `NotShared` clear the mark and fall through to the ordinary verdict; both index writers admit their control entry PARKING before `manager_verbs` (PR 4's door law — round-1 Issue 3: `Try` failed clones and leaked references on a busy ring 0), and the home's journal-failure class KEEPS the reference (`shared_release_failures`, must-stay-0), never a discarded free. A surviving sole owner stays SHARED (no un-share until PR 12's wire protocol — priced in the note). **A re-description of an existing reference writes NO op** (`block_refs::cancel_same_reference_pairs` at both translators — the passthrough truncate-shrink's `bk:off:len → bk:off:len'` re-Put the record from scratch and stripped the durable bit, tripping C16 on a legal op: round-1 Issue 4). The served wire `ShareBlock` confirms every word against durable state (exists ∧ SHARED — `share_block_screened`, `manager_verb_rejected`) before the index moves; the served `MarkShared` sets the serving mount's RAM mark through the routed hooks (`shared_refs::install_routed`). **The W1 predicate reads the mark after the §5.1 fence** (`begin_patch_sole_owner`: `refcount == 1 && !is_shared` — the mark protects the block between step 1 and step 3, when the count still reads 1; `patch_ineligible_shared` counts it at the callers); the loom model `shared_ref_core_composed_never_patches_or_frees_a_marked_block` (a SERIALIZABILITY pin of the DESIGN's three-word protocol — the product's clone pins before it marks and its free reads the mark before releasing, so the PR-7 composition rests on the two-word law plus pin-before-publish; stated in the model) shows the two-word §5.1 law holds with the third word — weakening the PATCHER's fence fails it; the cloner's two fences fail only together (its post-mark fence stands in for its post-pin one; in the product the two acts are separated by `MarkShared`'s durability-lane ack). **fsck C16** (report-only, `fsck_shared_index_drift`; `shared_refs::shared_index_drift` — the SHARED flags of every meta volume resolved to the routed identity against the home's index, both sides, confirmed by a second census, riding C8's unsharded pass since PR 1's ONE-walk census is still per kind): a cloner dying after step 1 leaves a flag C16 reports and the source's release frees the block; dying after step 2 leaves an entry C16 reports and the GC arm drops — both pinned. **Gather mode** (§5.7.5, KD-SYM-17): `setfattr -n user.squeezefs.gather -v 1 <dir>` (`crate::GATHER_XATTR` — the ONE user-settable name inside the reserved `user.squeezefs.` family, admitted by NAME in `xattr_name_allowed`; values by the knob law `gather_xattr_opts_in`) makes a child mint into the directory's slot whatever the affinity ceiling says (`RoutedMetaBackend::gather_mint_slot` — ONE arm ahead of PR 4's policy in `pick_mint_slot_for`, one node-cache probe of the parent's xattrs per create on an ARMED volume, nowhere else; the native slot excluded like §5.1.2's), counted `dir_gather_mints` so **`affinity_mints + rotor_mints + dir_gather_mints ≡ mints`** (pinned); never automatic — a plain directory spills past the cap exactly as before, an unarmed mount never reads the opt-in. Stats (appended after PR 4's block): `dir_gather_mints`, `block_ref_probes`, `share_block_calls`, `release_shared_calls`, `mark_shared_calls`, `shared_blocks` (per data volume), `pack_open_scopes`, `shared_release_failures`, `fsck_shared_index_drift`. The matrix `tests/run_sym_forest_suites.sh` grew both suites (30). **Deviations stated in the note**: the index in tree 0 of volume 0 (not kind `0x09` in a slot tree — bit 17's kind set unchanged), the free-list seam, the wire GC arm, the in-process "ship" (every actor this mount), `open_room_bytes` reading the roomiest scope. **Owed**: PR 8's bitmap + zero-scan open + the home's re-point, PR 12's wire path for the verbs, the box rows (`ls -l` with/without gather, the pack ratio — dev-box scoping only here).
### Symmetric metadata program — PR 6, cross-owner transactions (2026-09-15, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 6 landed CROSS-OWNER TRANSACTIONS** on PR 4's slot leases (`.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr6-cross-owner.md`; `tests/sym_cross_owner_tests.rs` — 27 contracts + the `#[ignore]`d scoping instrument, in `run_sym_forest_suites.sh`'s list; design §5.6 — ruling **D18 REVERSED** — §5.6.4, §5.3.5, §11 the Cross-owner family; KD-SYM-14), dark behind bit 17 AND the armed plane — **`=0` and every bit-17-absent mount are the S3.5 paths EXACTLY** (intents at ino 0, no ship, no lock, every Cross-owner gauge 0 — pinned on both layouts). Under the plane a namespace mutation whose objects live in slots OTHER appenders lease (`create`/`mkdir` under a foreign directory — the D1 shared-output shape — `unlink`/`rmdir` of its entries, `link` into it, `rename`/`RENAME_EXCHANGE` across holders) is an ordinary POSIX op: ONE S3.5 intent whose foreign steps ship as the S8 verb `MetaCall::XvStep` (`MetaVerb::XvStep = 0x60`, the PR-6 block; `LookupExact = 0x61` the exact read verb; `XvGuards = 0x62` / `XvRelease = 0x63` the travelling guard's pair) to the slot's holder — resolved through tree 0's lessee (`SlotLeaseTable::resolve`, compared with `KvMetaBackend::own_appender_id()` = region 0's) and the appender→endpoint table (`SlotHolderCache::{set_endpoint,endpoint}` — the join ladder's binding, PR 12's; the contracts bind it directly) — and **`xv_apply_step` stays the ONE applier**: the served side (`RoutedMetaBackend::xv_serve_step`, `meta_ship/service.rs`'s arm) is the same function under the holder's guards, lease and ring, idempotent under the recorded `(pre, post)` witness, its reply after its durability lane, its outcome counted on the S3.5 step ledger AT THE HOLDER. The ladder as built (`crossvol_tx::execute`): plan under the op's 4a guards — **foreign-home guards TRAVEL** (§5.6 line 1; `crossvol_tx::acquire_guards_leased`, the arms' ONE acquisition: every key whose lock lives in this process's table in the ONE stripe-canonical `lock_many`; a foreign holder's keys as one `XvGuards` parked at the holder under the op's scope (`mint_guard_scope`), tables in ASCENDING appender-id order — the hierarchical argument makes the wait-for graph over tables acyclic; a served step under a scope applies WITHOUT taking a guard (`serve_guards_for` → `Covered`; a roll-forward that travelled none takes the step's keys for the apply), the release rides the drop of the op's guard set as `DlmGuard::external` — the terminal-outcome law by construction — and a dead initiator's parked scope expires with its lease at the cadence's sweep, `xv_cross_owner_guard_expiries` must-stay-0) → `tx0` = STEP 0's records + the intent when step 0 is local, ONE entry in ONE region (the intent keys on local 0 of that step's slot namespace — `intent_ino_for_slot`; guest cursors mint from 2, so the ino-0 argument holds per slot); when step 0 is another appender's the intent is written ALONE first into the mount's first rotor slot (`xv_write_intent` — its own entry + barrier) → the initiator's ring barriered → steps in plan order through `apply_or_ship_step` (`StepHome::{Local, Foreign, Unreachable}`) → the local participants barriered → retire (`xv_retire_intent_at`, idempotent against the record). A create in a foreign directory (`create_in_foreign_directory`, `XvOp::Create`, the new step kind 6 `CreateInode { …, size, ts_ns }`) mints the child in the CREATOR's rotor (`lease_parent_slot` filters a foreign parent — affinity never follows it) and answers a holder's live witness refusal as the op's errno (`EEXIST` / `ENOENT` / `ESTALE`) with the minted child destroyed before the retirement (`xv_destroy_unnamed`). **A ship that fails leaves the intent OPEN, never fail-stops**: the S3.5 lattice latch protects the witnesses' premise against a local mid-plan device error, which a holder that is down does not violate — the op errors, and the set's **roll-forward cadence** (`spawn_roll_forward_cadence` from `open_routed_meta_set` on an armed set, at the checkpoint landing ceiling — adoption by the intent register's STATE (`InFlight` = a live op of this process, its own however long it runs; `Abandoned` = left open by an op whose ship failed, or found with no live owner — the cadence's), never by elapsed time, and `recover_one` RE-READS the intent under the guards it acquires and applies that image (a record gone meanwhile applies nothing, leaves no ghost); `roll_forward_open_intents` its body, `recover_open_intents` the mount's) completes it once the holder serves; an open intent no holder serves past the grace window (`stuck_grace_ms` = `CLIENT_STALE_TTL_SECS`, seam `TEST_XV_STUCK_AFTER_MS`) is **`xv_cross_owner_intents_stuck`, must-stay-0** (PR 10's recovery of the dead holder's slots is the remedy). **Every `rename` whose SOURCE is a directory takes the set-wide `dir_rename` lease** (a tree-0 record on volume 0, `slot_state::DirRenameRecord`; `ManagerCall::DirRenameLock/DirRenameUnlock`, idempotent — `already` / `busy { holder }`; `KvMetaBackend::{manager_dir_rename_lock,manager_dir_rename_unlock,manager_dir_rename_release_dead,dir_rename_lock_held}` in the child module `kv/backend/crossvol_arms.rs`), taken OUTERMOST — `Metadata::rename` is now a wrapper deciding the lock on an unguarded read of the source's type and `rename_body` re-decides under its guards and hands back `Ok(false)` when the source became a directory (taken after a 4a guard it would cycle with a peer's shipped step) — and checks ancestry on EXACT data WITHOUT 4a guards (`refuse_rename_into_own_subtree`: each hop O(1) off the routed backend's directory-parent memo `dir_parents` — a hint, confirmed at its holder through the guard-free `lookup_dentry_exact_unguarded` / `LookupExact`; a miss is the reverse scan `dir_rename_parent_scans`; a denied or absent link re-read three publication ceilings then `EAGAIN`; a chain reaching the moved directory `EINVAL` — review round 1, Issue 2: a shared `D{}` guard on the walk parked the initiator behind its own exclusive guards on a stripe collision, holding the lease); the wire words of `DirRenameLock/Unlock` are SCREENED (volume 0, a `Live` non-own id, the holder for an unlock — `screen_dir_rename_words`, fuzzed), two directory renames of one process are TICKETS under one record, the lease is RAII — same-slot directory renames too; file renames never take it; the expiry law is the holder's membership lease, the release-dead arm PR 10's driver. **Found by the scoping row and fixed before landing**: the first build took the step's guards AT THE HOLDER while the initiator held its own across the ship ("no foreign guard travels") — the 27th cross-holder directory rename found the holder's `insert_dentry` parked behind the initiator (a stripe collision in the shared table; across two nodes the two-mutual-initiator cycle), so the served step waited out the wire's two call timeouts; the travelling guard above is the design's own line, pinned by `a_served_step_never_parks_behind_the_initiators_guards_even_on_a_stripe_collision` (the collision forced by NAME) + the wire pair's contracts. **As-built deviations, stated**: (0) **the D19/D20 arms (`cross_owner_error`, M1/M2/M3) are NOT retired here** — the flat per-volume-owner recipe's, live product code its PV suites exercise, unreachable under the symmetric plane by construction (its ownership is the slot lease, not the S8 `OwnerMap`), so `meta_ship.cross_owner_refusals` reads 0 on every symmetric mount; D18 is REVERSED (cross-owner ops are ordinary ops), the arms retire with the recipe in PR 12/14; (1) the `dlm_rpcs += 1` face of a travelling guard is its own gauge `xv_cross_owner_guard_rpcs` (the S4 word lives in `dlm_slot.rs`, PR 5's file — the one-line fold is the rebase's); a same-process holder (the seam's declared regions) costs no round trip, so `dlm_rpcs` stays 0 in the contracts; (2) a live witness refusal at a shipped step STOPS the plan and undoes every applied half in reverse under the op's guards (a link's count, a rename's removed source name, a create's mint), the retirement riding the last local inverse's own entry — the window that remains (a kill before that inverse, or before the separate retirement when it is foreign) completes the plan FORWARD at recovery, the C9/C10 census classes; (3) the plan's foreign READS (the EEXIST probe, the `(pre, post)` witnesses) are exact in one process and the S5 projection on the wire until PR 5's tokens. **The multi-holder model** is PR 2–4's declared partition made two holders: appender 0 (the manager, the mount's own identity) and the declared regions (`SQUEEZEFS_TEST_SYM_APPENDER_SLOTS`), each with its own ring, lease set and tree-0 record; a declared region's slot is FOREIGN to appender 0 for every cross-owner decision and ships over a real `cluster_wire` session to the S8 owner service over the same backend (`AsyncVerbRouter::with_meta` + `with_manager` on one listener), which applies it under region 1's lease and ring. Seams: `TEST_XV_GUARDS_FORCE_REMOTE` (an in-process holder's guards over the wire — the initiator's remote arm), `TEST_XV_SERVE_MISDELIVER_ONCE` (a holder dying after its commit and before its reply — the reply's correlation id poisoned, the client's same-id resend), `TEST_XV_SERVE_REFUSE` (a holder down before its commit), `TEST_XV_STUCK_AFTER_MS`, `TEST_XV_SEAM_AFTER_STEPS` (S3.5's, now over shipped steps). Gauges: the Cross-owner family `xv_cross_owner_intents_{minted,retired,open}` (closure `minted ≡ retired + open` — an adopted intent counts at discovery), `xv_cross_owner_steps_{shipped,served,rejected}`, `xv_cross_owner_intents_stuck`, `xv_cross_owner_phase_ns` (`guard_rtt / plan / intent_barrier / ship_rtt / local_steps / retire / total`), `xv_cross_owner_guard_{rpcs,expiries}` / `xv_cross_owner_guards_parked`, `dir_rename_lock_{acquires,wait_ns}`, `dir_rename_parent_scans` (`crossvol_tx::cross_owner_stats_json`, appended at the end of the symmetric stats block). Fuzz: `cluster_wire_frame` gained the step-body arm (one step through the S8 body AND the intent record, both codecs agreeing; the guard pair beside it), `manager_call_frame` the lock verbs. Owed: tokens over the plan's foreign reads (PR 5), the endpoint binding + the wire initiator's lock take (PR 12), the death ledger driving `manager_dir_rename_release_dead` and the dead holder's slot recovery (PR 10), the striping steps (PR 7b — `InsertDentry` / `RemoveDentry` take the KEY ino), the `tar -x` / shared-directory rows (PR 13).
### Symmetric metadata program — PR 8, the allocation lease (2026-09-15, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 8 landed the ARMED plane's DATA ALLOCATION and the home shard's PARK** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr8-alloc-lease.md`; `tests/sym_block_grant_tests.rs` — 36 contracts after review round 4, in `run_sym_forest_suites.sh`'s list, the crash rows on 1- AND 2-volume sets; design §5.5 / §5.5.1 / §5.5.2 / §5.5.3 / §5.9 / §6.3 / §11; KD-SYM-9/15), dark behind bit 17 AND the knob — **`=0` and a bit-17-absent volume are the shipped posture exactly** (no holding, no park posture, the S9 lane path byte-identical; pinned). **Per DATA volume a floating allocation lease** (`src/meta_backend/kv/alloc_lease.rs`): `alloc_lease:{vol_tag} → { holder, holder_appender_id, home_vol, control_ino, blocks, term, bitmap: [(home_vol, ExtentRef)] }` in tree 0 of the set's VOLUME 0 (KD-SYM-2's bound), first-come at term 1, a LIVE holder's refused to everyone else (`Busy`), a DEAD holder (`dead_member:` present) succeeded ONLY once `recovered:{holder, home_vol}` exists — before it the ask is **`LeaseDeferred`** (EAGAIN, the wire's `STATUS_DEFERRED`) — the §5.5.1 ordering law; the successor reads the predecessor's pages through the record's refs, COPIES them into extents of its own grant on its home volume and publishes the new refs (`AllocLeaseBitmap`) at `term + 1`. **Ranged block grants** (`src/block_grant.rs`, KD-SYM-9): `BlockGrant { start, len }`, `G = clamp(2 × ewma × T_renewal, 64, cap/(2 × writers))` (floor 64 = one write-pipeline BDP window of 4 MiB blocks; `T_renewal` = the membership beat, `membership::renewal_beat_ms`; tie-tested), the holder's `BlockGrantLedger` (carve = the lowest clear run at or above the grant frontier, bits SET at the carve, `Already` verbatim for a covered remainder — §5.3.5, `ReturnBlocks` clears, `revoke_dead` → the S7 quarantine), the writer's `GrantWindow` (lowest-first mint, the 50 % top-up ask — proactive off the write path through `kick_block_grant_topup`, the exhausted mint's inline ask the belt, retried after a proactive ask in flight lands — at the volume's last grant the two asks race and the holder answers one `Full`; on an armed allocator the free-grace valve's supply terms read the window's remainder + the holder's clear population (`grant_supply_blocks` — round 4, Issue 28: the drained list + a zero tail read as a false trough); `BlockAllocator::install_block_grant_arm` makes `next_fresh_block` mint from the window — the unarmed loop verbatim — and on a grant-armed allocator **the bitmap IS the free list**: the local list is drained into the bitmap at the arm (`drain_free_list_into_grants`), `try_allocate_block`'s free-list-first pass is gated behind the arm (the window is the ONE mint source — round 3, Issue 23's second face: the mount-time gap fill was still handed out), `publish_free_list` never inserts into the local list, a freed block returns only through a carve that SETS its bit again); **the PRODUCTION arm is `alloc_lease::arm_symmetric_allocation`** (the mount path after the mw arm — review round 2, Issue 7): per data volume the derived block count and the allocator registered, the lease acquired first-come through volume 0's manager, held (**the FIRST hold SEEDED from the allocator's derived truth** — `derived_allocation_snapshot`, 1 bit per block: every block below the dense cursor not on the free list reads SET, the free list's blocks CLEAR — ∪ the set's durable reference ledger for the volume (`block_ref_scan`'s union, every slot tree on a forest — PR 7 kept the mount-time by-block scan as the free list's derivation, so the derived state is populated on both layouts), written + barriered before the record names the pages; an allocator whose cursor reads 0 while that ledger names blocks is UN-SEEDED and refuses the arm loud; every hold checked against `derived ∪ durable` in the LOSS direction, the arm refusing loud on `data_alloc_bitmap_drift`; the snapshot re-taken after the hold and a torn one refused — review rounds 3/4, Issues 23/27/29: an unseeded first hold re-granted a populated volume's LIVE blocks; `rehold_alloc_lease` for this identity's own record after a clean remount or a crash — the own-residue recovery folds the kept window onto the pages; a same-node predecessor of another mount slot is dead by the D0 flock's proof: `record_death`, its home recovered, `manager_record_recovered`, then succeeded; a live FOREIGN holder refuses the arm loud naming PR 12), the refs published, the grant arm installed with `holder_block_grant_sink`; `wire_block_grant_sink` / `install_wire_free_target` are the wire writer's halves (PR 12's second daemon is their production caller); **liveness is the death ledger, never a TTL**. **The data allocation bitmap** (`src/data_alloc_bitmap.rs`): one bit per block per data volume, A/B page pairs exactly `alloc_ext.rs`'s (magic `KVDA`, the `vol_tag` in the 32-byte header, xxh3, newest-valid-wins; 32 KiB per TiB; a page pair with NO valid slot is fresh only when both slots are all-zero — a non-zero undecodable pair REFUSES the load, never "all free"), pages in extents the holder claims from the manager's bitmap on its HOME volume (`hold_alloc_lease`; a `Weak` to the home backend binds the holding — `write_data_alloc_pages` acts only on the volume it is homed on), **kind-4 `vol_tag`-prefixed deltas in the holder's ring** (a 16-byte key — the length discriminates them from the heap's 8-byte extent deltas; `alloc_ext::load_inner` and the declared regions' replay SKIP them through `is_data_alloc_delta_key`; the 9-byte VALUE carries the holder TERM and a recovery folds only its term's deltas), under **the ordering law** (review round 2, Issues 1/2): every bit mutation — a carve's SET, a return's CLEAR, `finish_free`'s CLEAR — moves the bit AND queues its delta under ONE per-holding lock (`AllocHolding::mutations`), one drainer at a time journals the queue head-first into the home ring (`drain_deltas`), so record-seq order IS RAM order and the replay's LWW fold reads it; a carve drains and BARRIERS before it answers, a `finish_free` (synchronous) queues and kicks the single-flight journaler — no checkpoint in its path, and the checkpoint's page step never drains (review round 3, Issue 22 — §4.4 pt 5: no USER-class admission decides the cycle; a non-empty queue is re-kicked OFF the cycle, a refused window counted on `data_alloc_bitmap_deltas_deferred`); and **the pages are written where the meta bitmap's are** — before barrier #1 and the ledger record whose tail passes their deltas (`checkpoint.rs`, the same position; `TEST_CHECKPOINT_HALT_AFTER_LEDGER` is the kill-after-the-record seam); the recovery arm `replay_data_alloc_deltas(bitmap, term)` folds per-key LWW over the ring window plus the window's deltas the mount's own replay KEPT for exactly that (data volume, holder term, home device) (`data_alloc_bitmap::KeptKey` — bounded to one open's window, evicted by a newer term, consumed by the re-hold, dropped at the leave); the C6/C8 census is the ORACLE (`DataAllocBitmap::drift` — LEAK released, LOSS report-only on **`data_alloc_bitmap_drift`, must-stay-0**). **The wire is screened** (Issue 6 — PR 3/4's bounded-execution law): `screen_alloc_lease_acquire` (`blocks` against the data volume's registered derived block count, `home_vol` against the set's width) and `screen_alloc_lease_bitmap` / the pure `screen_bitmap_refs` (inside the home volume's heap, node-aligned, covering exactly the region) refuse `Rejected` on `manager_verb_rejected` before any effect; `AllocLeaseAcquire`'s `already` answers the record's pages and blocks (crash row 8). **Frees re-homed**: a co-writer's shipped frees go to THAT data volume's holder when `block_grant::free_target_for` names one (`execute_shipped_frees` verbatim; the authority otherwise); one grace ring per data volume — `GraceRing` carries per-ring `deferrals` / `releases` / `timeout_deferrals` (`free_grace_deferrals ≡ releases + offsets` PER VOLUME, `free_grace_timeout_deferrals` = frees released past the routine bound, the ring's surviving role under PR 5's recall-driven qualification). **The death ledger's RECORDS** (§5.5.2): `dead_member:{node_token, mount_slot}` / `recovered:{…, vol}` with `record_death` (the in-process writer — **PR 10's recovery driver is the production writer**, the allocation arm's same-node takeover its first caller; the wire's `RecordDeath` is RESERVED, refused naming PR 10; **the record's arm on the holder**: every held lease `revoke_dead`s the dead writer's grants and QUARANTINES them on the registered allocator under a dead epoch — S7) and `manager_record_recovered`; gauges `dead_members_recorded` / `_acted` / `recovered_records` / `dead_member_propagation_ms`; **the vol-0 rule wired**: `note_vol0_ledger_probe` releases the manager role (`manager_lease` → `vacant`, `manager_vol0_unreachable`) past `T_owner` unreachable — the D0 claim stays, the successor ladder is PR 10's. **Park-and-reclaim** (`src/park_core.rs` `#[path]`-shared into loom + `src/park_gate.rs`; KD-SYM-15 extended): a symmetric appender's `T_self` action is PARK — **classified by OBJECT** (review round 2, Issues 4/5): `MemberSession::self_fence` is the SHIPPED terminal fence for every object (the S9 custody client, the lane/publish-era refusals, the not-custody answer POISON), only `self_fence_as(FenceClass::SymmetricAppender)` parks and the membership renewal tick's deadline arm is its one caller (the session unfenced, `SelfFence.parked`); the tick answers `RenewalTick::Parked` — RETRYABLE — and the loop keeps ticking at the renewal BEAT (never a 1 ms spin), reclaiming against the successor the home volume's ledger observation names (`membership::note_successor_observed` / `successor_endpoint` — PR 10's `writer_claim` poll is the production writer) until admitted (`park_gate::release` — held acks in journal order, admission resumed) or `T_park_max` (the shipped poison, `appender_park_expiries`); "not custody" → `expire_now` + the terminal fence; the gate is a **PRE-admission state** ahead of `admit_user_budget`'s ring park (never `note_journal_failure`) whose `AdmissionPass` is owned by the batch's `QueuedTx`s to fan-out (the parker's in-flight count = the commits whose acks the park holds — the modeled Dekker pair is relied on), the durability lane HOLDS acks at `hold_acks` before `fan_out`, a shutdown over a standing park is REFUSED loud and SET-WIDE (`close_at_leave` + the process latch `leave_refused` every volume's `shutdown` consults — round 3, Issue 24: the parked committers fail `EIO`, the landed acks release, no expiry, every page stays `Live` — PR 10 inherits the boundary), the checkpoint task runs (the flush ceiling holds), the FUSE watchdog labels PARKED only an op at or before its publish and no older than the park + threshold, the barrier's bounded timer is suspended for the park's bound, `T_park_max = manager_failover_bound_ms + LeaseClocks::grace` (derived; `park_gate::t_park_max_for`), expiry = the shipped poison (`appender_park_expiries`, must-stay-0); **`data_custody::poison` SPLITS** by `FenceClass` (`RemoteCustody` poisons, `SymmetricAppender` parks); the roles arm at the routed open (`alloc_lease::arm_symmetric_roles` — the slot-0 volume's manager = the coordinator home, home volume 0 until PR 12's join ladder) and disarm at the leave. **Membership shards** (KD-SYM-15): a symmetric appender's rendezvous is its HOME volume's record, `membership_shards` = the shards an owner SERVES (one plane — the owner-role fusion, volume `v`'s manager as the S6 owner of the mounts homed on `v`, is PR 12's), `membership_sim::run_sharded` is the SIM-1 shape with an API-level park leg (the production arm is driven over the wire by `a_parked_member_reclaims_against_the_successor_the_ledger_names`). **The PR 7 seam** (round 4): the shared-block index's home is resolved through `shared_refs::index_home_volume_for(vol_tag)` — the data volume's allocation-lease holder's home volume off this process's holding, PR 7's volume-0 default unheld (fsck C16, the router's index arm, `clone_file`'s step 2 per data volume, `release_shared`); a wire writer's projection of an unheld lease is PR 12's. **Coordinator = volume 0's manager** (`symmetric_coordinator_refusal`, consulted first by `jobs::maintenance_coordinator_refusal`); **lessee shards**: `inode_plane_owns_slot` (leased, or unleased on the manager — `LeaseGate::verdict_structural`) gates C9/C10's candidates (`fsck_inode_plane_foreign_slot_scoped`), `inode_plane_slot_coverage` publishes **`fsck_inode_plane_slots_covered` ≡ leased ∪ unleased**. **Wire**: `BlockGrant` / `ReturnBlocks` / `AllocLeaseAcquire` / `AllocLeaseBitmap` / `AllocLeaseRelease` / `RecordRecovered` / `RecordDeath` appended to `ManagerCall` (documented codes `0x80..=0x86`; `CLUSTER_WIRE_SCHEMA` stays 5, `MANAGER_SCHEMA` 1). Loom: `park_gate_models` (the `try_enter`/`park` Dekker pair, release-vs-expiry exclusive) + `grant_table_core` re-run; fuzz `data_alloc_bitmap_page` + the PR-8 arm of `manager_call_frame` + the proptest mirror. Stats: the Allocation-lease family (`alloc_lease`, `block_grants`, `block_grant_blocks`, `block_grant_returned`, `block_grant_revoked`, `data_alloc_bitmap_{set,clear}_bits`, `data_alloc_bitmap_population`, `data_alloc_bitmap_deltas_journaled`, `data_alloc_bitmap_deltas_deferred` (drains the ring's full user window refused — the deltas stayed queued for the next drainer, the checkpoint cycle untouched), `data_alloc_bitmap_drift`, `free_grace_timeout_deferrals`, `free_grace_per_volume`, `dead_members_recorded`, `dead_members_acted`, `recovered_records`, `dead_member_propagation_ms`), the park family (`appender_parked`, `appender_parks`, `appender_park_ns`, `appender_park_expiries`, `appender_park_acks_held`, `slot_lease_reclaims`, `t_park_max_ms`), `membership_shards`. **Deviations, stated**: `alloc_lane_grant.rs` is NOT renamed (60 references across files other PRs edit — the rename rides PR 12/14's retirement sweep; `block_grant.rs` is the armed plane's module); the grant top-up is asked by the writer's window (in-process sink / the wire verb), not yet carried on the renewal grant; the holder's ring in-process is ring 0 of its home volume (the manager's own — a non-manager holder's ring is PR 12's); a foreign home volume's page read and the `dead_member:` driver are PR 10's. Owed: PR 10's driver (+ `replay_data_alloc_deltas` on a foreign home volume, the `writer_claim` poll behind `note_successor_observed`), PR 12's home volume + wire top-up carriage + the owner-role fusion + C13's exemption of the `alloc_lease:`-named page extents once the pages come from a grant + the wire writer's index-home projection, **the forest open with ZERO by-block census reads** (PR 7's stated seam for PR 8 — needs the bitmap to replace the RAM refcount map as the terminal-free engine; PR 12/14), PR 5's recall-gated free arm, the fleet legs of gates 3/4, the box bracket.

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 7b, directory striping (2026-09-16, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 7b landed DIRECTORY STRIPING** on PR 4's slot leases, PR 5's tokens and PR 6's intents (`.benchmarks/2026-09-16-sym-pr7b-dir-stripes.md`; `tests/sym_dir_stripe_tests.rs` — 25 contracts after review round 2, in `run_sym_forest_suites.sh`'s list; design §5.6.5 as built, §5.8.5 C17, §7.1 amended, §11 the Striping family; KD-SYM-20, owner ruling R-SYM-5 — the Lustre DNE-2 shape), dark behind bit 17 AND the knob — **`=0`, a bit-17-absent volume and `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_DIR_STRIPES=1` are the shipped posture EXACTLY** (no flip, no map, `readdir` the volume's page verbatim, `user.squeezefs.stripes` a reserved name for the VAL-2 screen, every Striping gauge 0 — pinned). **A hot shared directory `D` spreads its dentries over `K` STRIPES** (`src/meta_backend/dir_stripe.rs`, the ONE module, called from the existing arms of `meta_backend/mod.rs`): `K` stripe inos `D_0 … D_{K-1}` — ordinary `S_IFDIR` records with no name, each minted in a DIFFERENT creator's slot **on `D`'s volume** — with `stripe(name) = hash54(name) % K` over the volume's SEEDED dentry hash (one seed ⇒ one order across stripes, so the K-way `readdir` merge is the shipped `(hash54, coll)` cookie order and the shipped cookie is its exact continuation) and stripe `i`'s dentries keyed under `D_i` in `D_i`'s slot tree: stripe ownership IS slot ownership, and a create into `D` is PR 6's shipped `InsertDentry` with the stripe as its key parent (`dir_stripe_ships`) — the wave rows: 7 declared holders, K = 14, 2,000 creates ⇒ every foreign create one ship, per-stripe population 113–170 vs a mean of 143 (max ≤ 1.19 × mean), and 31 holders, K = 62 ⇒ 20–47 vs 32.3 (1.46 ×), `slot_handovers == 0` on both. **The stripe map is `K + 2` RESERVED-NAME dentries in `D`'s own tree** — `\0sqz.stripe:NN → D_i`, `\0sqz.migrating → D_0`, `\0sqz.striped → D_0` (the COMMIT marker; the map is live iff it exists AND names stripe 0 — **no marker names `D` itself**: a self-reference was the reverse dentry scan's FIRST hit whenever `D`'s key sorted below its parent's, and `..` of `D` answered `D` on the reconnect path — review round 1, Issue 3) — names whose leading NUL byte no user op can pass (the VFS refuses it), filtered at the ONE routed pager; the marker codec is CANONICAL (exactly the encoder's two digits decode — Issue 7): the map rides `D`'s token verbatim (PR 5 pages a directory's dentries — no token-API change), the flip is `K + 2` existing `InsertDentry` steps of ONE PR 6 intent with the commit marker LAST (no new step kind, no new applier; `XV_MAX_STEPS` = `2 × MINT_SPREAD + 8`, the one-intent rmdir of a K = 64 directory; a kill at any step rolls forward to the WHOLE map at the slot's next lessee — pinned at every window), and C9's dentry pass marks a stripe REFERENCED by its map entry (an orphan stripe is C9's class, as §5.6.5 says). **No slot-tree KEY kind is added** — bit 17's kind set stays PR 1 + PR 7's; what this rung adds to the bit's meaning before the flip is the reserved NUL name space inside kind `0x02` (§7.1 amended). **A stripe NEVER carries a map** (Issue 1 — at the design's own operating point, ≥ 2 creators into one striped directory, the first build's trigger flipped every STRIPE and its migration stranded the stripe's names in nested stripes nobody routed to): the census skips a known stripe (learnt from every map read and mint), a marker insert and an already-striped parent; the spawned flip runs the belt (`is_stripe` — the reverse scan, once per stripe) and the explicit flip refuses "a stripe cannot carry a map". **The flip** (`stripe_dir` / `flip_dir`): the holder re-checks the commit marker guard-free right after its single-flight slot (a concurrent flip that won is found with nothing asked and nothing minted), asks the `K` heaviest foreign creators (`SupplyStripeIno`, `dir_stripe_supply_rpcs`; a creator that declines or is gone is replaced by the next — another appender's own transaction, asked while the flip holds NO guard), allocates the remainder's inos (no guard), then takes its 4a guards in **ONE canonical `lock_many_leased`** — `I{D}` + every remainder `I{D_i}` + the `K + 2` marker `D{}` keys — mints the remainder under that one set and writes the one intent (fix round 2026-09-16, `fix/sym-dir-stripe-flip-lock-many`, PR 10 review Issue 28: the landed build held `I{D}` + the marker keys across `K` single-key mints on the SAME striped table — a stripe-collision self-deadlock, ≈ 0.8 % per 64-stripe flip at the shipped width, DETERMINISTIC under `SQUEEZEFS_DLM_STRIPES=1` — parking the directory for ever; pinned by `a_flip_takes_its_4a_guards_in_one_acquisition_under_the_width_one_crucible`, a child-process pin because the table width is a process-lifetime resolution; a plan that fails names every mint LOUD as C9's class); the `-o stripe_dirs` flip runs after the mkdir's own guards, gate and permit have dropped (still awaited — striped at creation); names re-home LAZILY (`migrate_dir`: insert into the stripe FIRST, remove from `D` second, each pair one intent — `migrate_one`, the ONE move; `dir_stripe_migrated_names`; a migration the holder died inside resumes at the directory's next ACCESS by its slot's lessee — the marker is the durable cursor) and the flag clear RE-VERIFIES `D`'s own tree empty of user names under the exclusive guard (a name that landed behind the scan is moved first; Issue 4c). **Where a name IS** (Issue 4): `D`'s own tree is the PERMANENT secondary home for every READ (`stripe_locate`: the stripe wins where both hold a name — the LWW rule; the `readdir` merge streams `D`'s tree as one more stream), and every MUTATION keys the STRIPE — `unlink`, `rename` (both sides), `link` re-home a name still in `D`'s tree first (`stripe_mutation_parent` → `migrate_one`, under the same guards as the background migration, so the two serialize and the second finds the work done; `dir_stripe_rehomed_on_touch`) and revalidate under their guards against one home — the first build's unrevalidated pre-read gave spurious `ENOENT`s racing the migration and stranded a rename's destination; a shadowed FILE loser (the same name in `D`'s tree and the stripe with two children) is unlinked with its count, a directory loser is left and named (C17). **Triggers**: automatic at the holder when the foreign creates it served into `D` over one `T_idle` window exceed `N_floor` from MORE THAN ONE creator (the creator read off the shipped child's slot holder — PR 6 mints the child in the creator's rotor; one creator is a handover candidate, never a striping one; `note_served_insert` in `xv_serve_step` — one map increment + two O(1) reads per served insert, the heaviest-first sort once when due (Issue 11); `flip_due` the pure law), explicit `setfattr -n user.squeezefs.stripes -v K <dir>` (a COMMAND intercepted at the FUSE boundary ahead of the VAL-2 screen on an ARMED mount only; `getfattr -n` answers `K`, `getfattr -d` omits it; **holder-local until PR 12** — from a non-holder it answers `EAGAIN` naming the holder, Issue 19), or `mkdir` under `-o stripe_dirs`; `K` = `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_DIR_STRIPES` (int 1..=64, derived `MINT_SPREAD`; `1` = OFF), never re-striped; a PRESET (delegated) create into a striped parent refuses loud (Issue 20a). **Every op routes** (`stripe_route` / `stripe_locate`): `lookup`/`create`/`unlink`/`link`/`rename` take the stripe as the key parent (a rename within `D` across two stripes = the two-parent rename; the `..` reconnect scan hops a stripe to its directory; the dentry graph keeps a stripe a CHILD of `D`, so PR 6's ancestry walk stays exact); `readdir` is the STREAMED K-way merge (`readdir_striped_page`, `dir_stripe_readdir_merges` — each stream refilled in pages of `max/(K+1)` floored at 8 only when its head is consumed, so a merged page reads `≤ max + (K + 1) × page` raw entries whatever the stripes hold, never `K × max` — Issue 10); **`stat D` is the FOLD** `nlink = D.nlink + Σ (D_i.nlink − 2)`, times = max (a stripe's record IS the exact, idempotent delta its inserts wrote in the same transaction as the dentry — nothing ships per insert, nothing can be lost or double-applied; `dir_stripe_time_batches` = the fold's persist onto `D`'s record ONCE PER CHECKPOINT CADENCE per directory (`checkpoint_landing_ceiling_derived`; a `stat` never writes at the stat rate — the write recalls every reader's token on `D`; Issue 8) — the design's "Δtime batched per stripe per cadence", as built). **`rmdir D`** (R26; §5.6.5 "K `DestroyStripe` ships + `D`'s destroy in ONE intent" — Issue 2): `rmdir_striped` holds ONE guard set for the whole act — `I{parent}` + `D{parent, D}`, `I{D}`, `I{D_i}` EXCLUSIVE on every stripe (travelling to a foreign holder), `D{D, marker}` — probes `D`'s own tree (no USER name — the marker-filtered page of one, Issue 5: an 8-entry read called a directory carrying 66 markers empty), every stripe's count (`nlink 2`) and every stripe `IsEmpty` (exact under the exclusive guards — a foreign stripe's holder reads under the travelling scope, `Covered`; a stripe served here is read under the guard the caller HOLDS, never a scope-word inference — Issue 9), refuses `ENOTEMPTY` having written NOTHING, then writes ONE intent — the dentry out of its parent, the markers (commit marker FIRST), `SetNlink 2 → 0` on every stripe (the DYING mark) and on `D` — that a kill at any step rolls FORWARD whole (pinned at every window: no `nlink 0` stripe under a live map for the corpse sweep, the first build's crash window); an insert that parked on a stripe's guard behind the rmdir resumes to a parent whose record reads `nlink 0` or is gone and refuses `ENOENT` (`refuse_dying_parent` under the insert's guards at EVERY insert path — create, link, rename's destination, the served step after the `Take` arm's guards, answered there as a WITNESS refusal so a roll-forward retires the intent instead of erroring at every cadence (Issue 13) and the initiator corrects the plan's `EEXIST` to `ENOENT` off the record; `dir_stripe_dying_refusals`; Issue 6), then the stripes' records are destroyed as hygiene (`DestroyStripe`; an unreached stripe is an `nlink 0` corpse named by no map — the sweep's; `dir_stripe_rmdirs`); two concurrent rmdirs: the loser's revalidation finds the name gone (no revive exists — Issue 15). **Wire**: `MetaVerb::{SupplyStripeIno, IsEmpty, DestroyStripe}` = 0x90–0x92 on the S8 `MetaCall` (every word judged against durable state at the served side — a supply's `dir` a directory record, its `index` inside the stripe namespace, its `supplier` an appender the served mount serves; a destroy refused on a live or non-empty record); `IsEmpty` reads under the rmdir's travelling scope where it covers the stripe (never re-taking a parked guard). **fsck C17** (report-only, `fsck_stripe_findings` must-stay-0): the marker census rides C9/C10's ONE dentry walk (`RefPass.markers`), the striped population alone is re-read — a stripe named by two maps, a map entry naming a missing stripe (also C10's dangling-dentry face), an incomplete map under a commit marker (fewer than two entries, a gap, or a commit marker naming anything but stripe 0), a misrouted name, a user name in `D`'s own tree after `migrating` cleared (a stale-route insert's trace — served, re-homed at its next mutation); verify-before-report judges every C17 suspect against ONE fresh census per confirm pass (Issue 14). **Deviations, stated**: dentries for the map (not a record field), stripes on `D`'s volume, the fold for `nlink`/times (no per-insert ship), the one-intent rmdir under exclusive stripe guards (no mark-then-probe), the explicit flip holder-local, the seam-shaped in-process holders (PR 6's declared partition — the seam caps in-process holders at 7 per 64 KiB directory extent, 31 at 256 KiB; N daemons as stripe holders and the wire reader's per-stripe token merge are PR 12's). Seams: `TEST_STRIPE_HOLD_MIGRATION` (hold the flip's background migration), `TEST_STRIPE_RMDIR_HOLD_MS` / `TEST_STRIPE_RMDIR_GUARDS_HELD` (hold a striped rmdir between its probes and its intent — the R26 pin's window), PR 6's `TEST_XV_SEAM_AFTER_STEPS` (the flip's, a move's and the rmdir's kill windows). Stats: the Striping family (`dir_striped_dirs`, `dir_stripe_flips`, `dir_stripe_supply_rpcs`, `dir_stripe_ships`, `dir_stripe_readdir_merges`, `dir_stripe_migrated_names`, `dir_stripe_time_batches`, `dir_stripe_dying_refusals`, `dir_stripe_rmdirs`, `dir_stripe_rehomed_on_touch`) after the Cross-owner block + `fsck_stripe_findings` in the fsck family; `squeezefs::invariant_tripwire_count(site)` is the per-site census behind `invariant_tripwires` (a contract allow-lists ONE routed tripwire by name — PR 6's `xv_local_step_unguarded` fires on every cross-owner create, routed to its owner). **Found and fixed on the way (review round 2)**: **the flush pass's reactive extent refill was refused "volume is shutting down"** (`manager_extent_grant_class`, `kv/backend.rs` — the INTERNAL class now takes the leave's gate: a burst that drained a floor-sized grant inside the final cycles deferred every SMO, the fixpoint never converged and the leave kept the region's page `Live` over an UNCOVERED ring — acked records a probe could not see, the wave's 2-in-9 red; pinned red-first: `appender_self_recoveries == 0` at the next open), and **a PROBE's `shutdown` WROTE a checkpoint — a SHIPPED-BUG FIX on every layout** (`open_probe` opens `Writable` with no checkpoint task, so `run_offline`'s teardown took the `checkpoint_now` arm and wrote a checkpoint from a "read-only probe" on every offline `squeezefs fsck`: content-equivalent on a flat volume — the probe's replay is complete there — and on a forest volume whose writer died with unpublished slot trees the ledger advanced past the window records the probe's own replay SKIPPED and the writer's next open replayed nothing — 50 inodes lost after ONE offline fsck of a crashed writer, fsck C10 dangling dentries; `KvMetaBackend.probe` — set by `open_probe` alone, never `non_writer`, which the §4.11-degraded writer shares while keeping its whole teardown ladder — joins the no-write teardown arm; pinned flat + forest (`an_offline_probes_shutdown_writes_nothing`, the image hashed before/after) and the degraded writer's ladder (`a_degraded_writers_shutdown_runs_its_whole_ladder`) — both `kv/backend.rs` edits stated for the rebase onto PR 10); fsck's inode plane records NO verdict on a read-only probe over a `Live` appender page (an uncovered window is not a census — `inode_plane_volumes_covered` 0), and the contracts' oracle asserts the leave COVERED before the census. Owed: N daemons as stripe holders + the wire reader's per-stripe token grants + the shipped explicit flip + the striped rmdir's probe under the S8 owner-execute context (a remote holder's `IsEmpty { scope: 0 }` is answered under its own guard, not the initiator's exclusion — the ship-client context must travel the stripes' guards or the rmdir must refuse under it) (PR 12), re-striping, the dead holder's stripes / a stuck `StripeDir` intent under the death ledger (PR 10), the box rows (PR 13/14).

`Recovering` refuses), the eviction + grace-deadline writers (8 members, 7 reclaim, ONE record), three volumes (one record, 3 acts), eight simultaneous deaths (7 declared + 1 wire joiner, one projection), the dead wire lessee's slots/grant/`dir_rename`, the vol-0 manager failover + the stale `dir_rename`, the released manager's silent heartbeat, `appender clear`'s four refusals + the attestation, C14 reported (probe) / refused (mount) / cleared (the verb) + C15's plan and offline refusal, the dead holder's open intent rolled forward, the successor observation, the non-PR zombie. **The fleet legs**: `tests/mw_fleet.sh create --symmetric` (format `--symmetric`, the manager armed; ### Symmetric metadata program — PR 12, the mount posture (2026-09-16, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 12 landed the JOIN LADDER — every RW mount of an ARMED set is a WRITER** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-16-sym-pr12-mount-posture.md`; `tests/sym_mount_posture_tests.rs` — 15 contracts after review round 1, in `run_sym_forest_suites.sh`'s list (38 suites); `SQUEEZEFS_MW_BIND` stays: every writer serves, the bind is where, `off` refused at rung 7); **2 bits** — `REQUIRED_INCOMPAT | bit 17` on every volume, the refusal naming the volume, the bit and the stamping act; **3 membership** — the shard armed at `auto` when no bind is declared (explicit wins verbatim, explicit `off` refused — the death ledger's writer is the S6 eviction; `membership::arm_mount_membership_at`); **4 registrant** — S7's data-namespace WERO joined (the metadata half is PR 3's `meta_wero` at the open), DETECTION-GRADE under `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_ALLOW_NON_PR=1` where the substrate has no reservations (announced in the report, refused without the opt-in); **5–6** the open's `JoinAppender` / `AcquireSlots`; **7 the planes** — `multi_writer::arm_authority_planes` (the declared arm's tail made shared: the custody owner on EVERY writer — PR 9's deviation 5 — the publish / meta / manager / token services on ONE listener, the all-local ownership map so `dlm_rpcs == 0` by construction) and the writer's listener PUBLISHED into its own claim-set entry on every volume (`publish_symmetric_endpoint`) — **the durable binding** `sym_join::resolve_holder_endpoint(vol, appender_id)` reads on any mount (the page's identity → `node_member_id_of` → the entry's endpoint; no knob, no wire). `mount_posture` reads `writer`; **there are no roles** — `manager_lease` / `alloc_lease` / `slot_leases_held` / `membership_mode` say what the writer holds, and `symmetric_join` (`{rungs, data_namespaces_registered, detection_grade, endpoint}`, `null` unarmed) publishes the ladder — `rungs` is the REQUIRED set in the design's numbering, a checklist not a trace (rungs 5–6 run inside the open, before 3 and before 2 / 4 / 7). `volume set-owners` REFUSES on a bit-17 set naming the lease (D19 reversed; `get-owners` / `locate` still read). **The `-o ro` reader binds PER SLOT HOLDER** (`KvMetaBackend::token_reader_for(object)` at every divert — `find_dentry` / `getattr` / `readdir_page` / `getxattr` / `listxattr`): the lessee off the reader's own tree 0 (`slot_state` — a projection refreshed at the epoch step), the manager's plane for an unleased slot or the manager's own (KD-SYM-2/3), a plane dialed to the holder's endpoint for a slot another appender leases — **one plane per LISTENER** (`reader_holder_planes` keyed by endpoint: two appenders one daemon serves share one plane and one recall channel, or a recall would reach the plane that does not hold the object), created at the first resolve, released at the leave; an UNBOUND holder REFUSES loud (`EAGAIN`, `dlm_token_reader_unbound_holders`) — never the projection (KD-SYM-19) — and its cost is bounded: the resolve (an appender-directory read + the claim set's `getxattr`) runs ONCE per holder per epoch step (`reader_holder_unresolved`, the negative cache `forest_reader_resync` clears; `dlm_token_reader_holder_resolves` is the cost face, `dlm_token_reader_holder_planes` the per-volume plane count); `bind_reader_holder_endpoint` is the explicit binding, the durable resolver the production one; `SQUEEZEFS_MW_AUTHORITY` is read nowhere on the reader. **`plane_gate` is `&self` and keys on the held ALLOCATION LEASE** on a grant-armed allocator (`alloc_lease::holding(vol_tag)` ∨ the served-free scope; `fuse_client::lease_refusal` names the lease and the volume on `cowriter_accounting_refusals`) and `alloc_plane_gate` passes on the grant window — W1, the recovery walk, reclaim and quarantine run on every mount for what it holds (pinned: the holder frees and patches; a non-holder's terminal free refuses, its fresh mint passes); every unarmed allocator takes the posture-word arms verbatim. **PR 4 round 6 Issue 31 discharged**: the appender's own checkpoint refreshes `head_hint` / `seq_offset` / `ckpt_seq` (PR 2's `write_appender_pages` — verified), the manager's slot-only rewrite of a wire joiner's page STOPS once `ckpt_seq ≥ 1` (`write_wire_joiner_page_slots` — one writer per page; a rewrite racing the owner's checkpoint on one A/B pair would land an older root at a newer generation), and a > 2-lap release lands under the DERIVED `release_seq_floor_bound` where the join-time hint refused it (pinned on a declared region past two laps). **PR 7's cross-process patch-under-clone window CLOSED**: the served `MarkShared` (`meta_ship/manager.rs`) runs under `BLOCK_FLUSH_LOCKS(global owner ino, block index)` — lock-order rung 3, the guard the local W1 patch site holds from its predicate through its DMA — so the mark lands wholly before a patch's fenced mark load or wholly after its DMA; `RoutedSharedRefs::global_ino` answers the FUSE key of the frame's local owner; `served_mark_shared_under_patch` stays as the must-stay-0 belt (pinned red-first: a served mark waits out a held guard). PR 9's holder JOIN carries the registrant key rung 4 registered (`live_wero_key`). **Two base defects fixed**: the A7 block-map admission keyed on the leaf's TREE ID, which every slot-tree node carries as 0 — no forest leaf ever entered probation or counted as a leaf read (`kvmap_read_tests`' two tree-7 contracts stamped-RED; the admission keys on the leaf's KIND — `leaf_carries_block_map_records` — and the suite joins the matrix); PR 6's `xv_local_step_unguarded` fired on every cross-owner create and whiteout rename — a fresh mint's `I{ino}` has no guard to be covered by (the local create path holds none; `XvLocalStep::is_fresh_mint` exempts the class; 7b's allow-list removed, every contract asserts `invariant_tripwires == 0`). PR 5's recall pin waits for the reader's own ack word. **NOT in this rung — PR 12b (design PR-plan row 12b, serial after 12; the orchestrator's adjudication at review round 1, the owner's wording at round 2; LANDED 2026-09-17 — the next paragraph)**: the MANY-writer posture — N RW daemons on one set, **N unbounded by design** (every non-manager RW daemon joins as a full writer with its own ring, page writes and checkpoint task, tree 0 as a projection, the third gate state, the wire `AcquireSlots` / `ReleaseSlot` holder-side acts; nothing bounds N — per-writer pages + rings with no ledger bound, per-slot leases over `W = 2^16` × 64 rotors, membership sharded by home volume at 12.5 k writers per shard by the design's arithmetic; 15 k is the target) — "the second daemon" is only the FIRST PIN; so the ladder's rungs 5–6 over the wire still meet the D0 gate's `FreshForeign` refusal, which NAMES the plane and PR 12b when the plane is requested ("a second or later RW mount joins as a WRITER once PR 12b — the many-writer posture, N unbounded by design — lands"; unarmed: the shipped text); with it stay appender homes ≠ volume 0 / two shards on a 2-volume set, the wire holder's flush-then-transfer, the `shared_ref:` migration at a home change, the bitmap replacing the by-block scan as the terminal-free engine, the writer's read divert to its per-holder planes, 7b's remote `IsEmpty`, PR 9's per-object epoch capture and owner-side grace, PR 10's cross-shard rejoin retirement, PR 2's drain-then-grow, PR 7's un-share of a surviving sole owner (the share-in-flight witness across owner and home). The matrix is 40 suites. **Review round 6 (2026-09-18, Issues 30–32)**: a peer's declared block-grant window is SCREENED against the volume's durable block count before any walk (`block_grant::screen_window_decl` — non-empty, inside the volume without wrap, ascending and disjoint; one bad range refuses the renewal's whole declaration on `data_alloc_bitmap_decls_rejected`, must-stay-0, nothing adopted; the adoption walks the pending set intersected with each range — PR 3's bounded-execution law on the new wire word; fuzz `cluster_wire_frame` arm 3d + the proptest mirror), the two S6/free-grace shipped-behaviour changes carry their 1.3.0 bullets, and the PR 8 supply-terms pin witnesses the top-up EVENT at rest (`BlockAllocator::block_grant_topup_inflight`) + conservation, never the window's instantaneous remainder. (27) the fleet collect loop's floor is the job wire's dial deadline (`fsck::fleet_collect_progress_floor ≡ job_wire::ENROLL_DIAL_TIMEOUT`, tie-tested); `RENEW_RETRY_FLOOR_MS` keeps 100 ms with its bound on the line; what a GREEN fleet fsck row proves while joiners live is stated (the block plane + C1 minus the live lessees' projected trees, NO C9/C10 verdict). **Standing finding, not this rung's**: the fleet worker's coordinator discovery picks a heartbeat-fresh `client:` record, and a dead manager incarnation's stays fresh for `CLIENT_STALE_TTL` (45 s), so a member's census shard re-enrolls at a successor up to 45 s + the retry grain after a failover (the leg waits it out).
