**until it lands the armed plane REFUSES the class LOUD** (fix rounds 1–2, Issues 12/22 — `RoutedMetaBackend::refuse_foreign_slot_file_mutation` → the typed `SqueezefsError::ForeignSlotFileMutation`, **`EREMOTE`** ("Object is remote" — the record lives at another appender) naming PR 13b — **never `EOPNOTSUPP` (§4.4ai, fix round 2, found by the fidelity leg's real-mount contract: `chmod(1)` EXITED 0 on a refused `fchmodat` because coreutils ≥ 9.6 `chmod`/`chown` treat `ENOTSUP` from the mode/owner syscalls as "not applied" — the errno class round 1 chose was the one class the tools that reach this face are entitled to swallow; the in-process pin `a_chmods_setattr_shape_on_a_foreign_slot_file_refuses_with_an_errno_no_tool_swallows` asserts the FUSE layer's shapes (mode + ctime, uid/gid + ctime, the `>>` open) refuse outside gnulib's `is_ENOTSUP` set with the record untouched at both daemons; a red-first pin whose premise is what the SYSCALL's caller does with the word, not the word's value)**: at the FUSE `open` for WRITE INTENT (`meta_backend::OPEN_WRITE_INTENT` = `O_WRONLY | O_RDWR | O_TRUNC | O_APPEND | O_CREAT`, the S5 reader gate's mask — `refuse_foreign_slot_open`; so a shell `>>` / `dd` / `truncate` fails AT THE OPEN and no write is ever acked), at the routed `setattr`/`setxattr`/`removexattr` entries before any read, at both layout publish entries, and as a BELT in the FUSE `write` handler for an fd opened before the slot moved or the il shim's ring path — where the honest statement is that the DEFAULT mount's writeback cache acks `write(2)` into the kernel's page cache and the refusal reaches the application at `fsync`/`close` through the kernel's errseq (POSIX-16's class); only a `--no-writeback` / `O_DIRECT` / `O_SYNC` path is refused at `write(2)` itself, and a shell redirection that ignores `close`'s status still reports success; never `ENOENT` for a file that exists; the kernel's ctime-only times echo is ABSORBED (`foreign_file_times_echo_absorbed`, `4523f25e`), an own-region slot exempt (`b8c4c92a`), a `-o ro` reader untouched; two transient shapes — a slot mid-handover TO this mount, a joiner's lagging lease projection — answer `EREMOTE` where `EAGAIN` would be honest, PR 13b's; gauge `foreign_file_mutation_refusals`; pinned in-process on the two-backend fixture and LIVE on the fidelity tier's `sym-join-ladder` leg (joiner 2's `>>` into joiner 3's file fails at the open on real nvmet, its `chmod` exits nonzero and moves nothing at any of the three daemons); PR 13b's contract stands `#[ignore]`d RED with its three faces — `setattr` + `setxattr`, the layout publish, the data write + `fsync` reading back at the manager: `a_joiners_setattr_of_the_managers_file_lands_at_the_holder`). **FOUND, NOT FIXED — the flip's SECOND blocker (§4.4af, fix round 1's storm): an acked-writes LOSS on the death path** — `sym-storm --victims=7 --cross-owner --striped`, round 4 from zero on `7c62428b`: 25 of 17,376 fsynced files lost across a seven-victim kill, every one a moved file of ONE writer at a 128-name stride, at neither its source nor its striped cross-owner destination after the manager recovered all 14 regions — 10 of them renames whose `mv` had RETURNED (an acked rename PR 6's law says was durable at its holder — the P0 signal) and 5 whose `mv` had not (the S3.5 lattice's designed transient while the intent is open; fix round 2's oracle settles on `xv_cross_owner_intents_open == 0` and judges those against the intent gauges, Issue 25); UNATTRIBUTED (the recipe in §4.4af; the candidates: the recovery's replay of the stripe holder's ring dropping shipped inserts, the roll-forward applying `RemoveDentry` without the insert with the initiator AND the insert's holder dead, or an acked `mv` whose insert never reached durability); a default cannot flip on an unattributed acked loss — **PR 13b's first item; the storm ×10 count restarts from zero on its fix**. **FOUND, NOT FIXED (§4.4ag, PR 12b / PR 5): a joiner's user read answers `EIO` for one beat inside a manager FAILOVER** — the successor's token plane refuses a member whose reclaim has not landed and the read does not wait for the retryable class; found by the widened deleted-stays-deleted arm (only `ENOENT` is "deleted", Issue 8); a gate-4 PRECONDITION ("refusals 0" on the death path) PR 13b clears beside defect 15's follow. **FIXED test-side (§4.4ah, the hang hunt `48fdf7d5`): defect 21's pin deadlocked ITSELF intermittently** — it named the single-flight winner by SPAWN ORDER and awaited that handle unbounded while the seam held it (≈ 35 % alone, more under load; attributed from the parked stacks + the seam's statics, no product change); the harness law: **a seam-driven pin names its actors by what the SEAM observed and bounds every await** (`tokio::select!` at 20 s — a genuine product park now fails loud). **The storm count as it stands**: `sym-crash` 10/10 GREEN on nine consecutive from-zero runs (attempts 7–15, attempt → binary in §3.8b), then 1/1 and 0/1 on the widened arm (§4.4ag); `sym-storm` ×10 NOT REACHED — 7 + 3 GREEN rounds from zero under `--venue=laptop`, each batch stopped on a product finding (the flip walk, then §4.4af); both counts restart from zero on PR 13b's binary. Harness laws learnt: never edit `run_mw_matrix.sh` while a batch runs it (bash reads the running script incrementally — a leg died `r: command not found`); a leg's `die` kills the harness's children (a died storm left its movers writing for an hour); a handover's accounting lands on three daemons at three instants — settle before the next phase's snapshot; the `--cross-owner` mover re-homes acked files concurrently (a pre-kill resolve accepts either home); the storm's acked-writes oracle settles on the intent gauges before it judges (PR 10's roll-forward runs AFTER the recoveries that move `appender_recoveries`); the fleet's rows dir is copied before a recreate; a `sym_stat_deleted` arm reads only `ENOENT` as deleted and dies on any other errno (which is what found §4.4ag). Standing (not fixed here): `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns` +1–2 at the N = 8 create storm's tail on the throttling laptop (the landing ceiling's fixed 2-tick margin, §4.5 — venue-attributed under the ruling; the box row decides; a PR 14 item if it trips there); the `SupplyStripeIno` on-demand binding. Docs: `docs/operations.md` "Symmetric metadata program — acceptance (PR 13, DARK)"; design §5.1.4 / §5.6.5 / §5.7.2 as-built at PR 13.

### Symmetric metadata program — PR 13b, the record-level metanode ship (2026-09-21, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 13b closes the flip's three blockers PR 13 left open** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-19-sym-acceptance.md` §4.4z / §4.4af / §4.4ag / §9; `tests/sym_n_daemon_tests.rs` + `tests/sym_coherence_tests.rs` pins; the fleet leg `sym-foreign-file`; design §5.10 status BUILT, §5.1.4 amended), dark behind bit 17 AND the knob — **`=0` and every bit-17-absent mount take the shipped paths verbatim**, every gauge below 0 / absent there. **(1) Defect 32 — the record-level metanode ship** (`src/meta_backend/record_ship.rs`, the ONE resolver + ship + ledger): a FOREIGN-slot file's `setattr` / `setxattr` / `removexattr` and its layout publish (`set_layout_and_size` / `merge_layout_and_size` / the block-reference commits / `migrate_block_map` / `park_write_times`) from a mount that does not lease its slot resolve the holder through PR 6's `step_home_bound` (tree 0's lessee → the appender→endpoint table; `bind_holder_endpoint_on_demand` for a late joiner) and SHIP over the existing S8 verbs (`MetaCall::{Setattr, Setxattr, Removexattr}` via `ship_meta_call`) and S9 publish frames (`publish::PublishTarget` — the S8 owner where one is declared, else the slot holder with the per-holder custody lease PR 9's grant established through `data_grant::slot_holder_client`; a publish whose presented lease epoch is not live custody at the holder is refused with the S9 status the writer's ladder retries (`check_publish_era` — the served gate is keyed on the LEASE EPOCH alone; the grant on the ino is the writer's acquire ladder's, which the FUSE write path always runs first, never the served gate's — S9 parity with the set-authority path)); the SERVED side is the S8 `MetaShipService` on the holder's `RoutedMetaBackend` under `SHIP_CLIENT` — its door, its lease, the conveyor pass's token recall (the writer's own token included — it re-fetches at its next resolve, exact), `record_ship::note_served` feeding `record_served` and the holder's dominance window (`note_slot_ship`, the requester's op), idempotent under `(lease_epoch, request_id)`; a slot moved between resolve and ship is re-resolved ONCE at the manager (`reresolve_slot_holder`, `record_ship_redirects`); a holder with no bound endpoint answers the RETRYABLE class `RefusalClass::HolderUnreachable { holder }` (EAGAIN, wire 3) — PR 13's interim `SqueezefsError::ForeignSlotFileMutation` / `EREMOTE`, its `write` belt and `foreign_file_mutation_refusals` are DELETED; `refuse_foreign_slot_open` (the FUSE write-intent gate, async now) passes for a reachable holder and refuses only the unreachable class; the ctime-only times echo stays absorbed (`foreign_file_times_echo_absorbed`); the NAMESPACE verbs keep PR 6's intent arm (the S8 router's `create` would bypass the creator's-rotor mint). Pins: `a_joiners_setattr_of_the_managers_file_lands_at_the_holder` (un-ignored; RED on the base with `EREMOTE`), `a_managers_setattr_of_a_joiners_file_lands_at_the_joiner`, `a_foreign_slot_verb_whose_holder_is_unreachable_refuses_retryable_then_lands_once_bound` (three daemons); LIVE on the fidelity tier's `sym-join-ladder` leg (its contract flipped from the refusal to the landing law: a joiner's `>>` into another joiner's file lands and reads back byte-exact at a third daemon, `chmod` exits 0 and the mode reads 600 everywhere, `record_ships` at the shipper ≡ `record_served` at the holder). Gauges (the `meta_ship` object): `record_ships`, `record_served`, `record_refusals` (**must stay 0**), `record_ship_redirects`, `record_unreachable`, `foreign_publish_ships` (the layout publishes that LANDED at a slot holder — counted at the terminal reply, once per logical publish, review round 1 Issue 4: a slot-moved redirect counts once and a refused publish never, so `ships ≡ served` at rest), `foreign_publish_served`, `foreign_publish_refusals` (a shipped publish's terminal failure — the writer's retry class); `record_ship_phase_ns` {`resolve`, `rtt`, `total`} top-level; the §5.10 row's 3.6 verbs/MiB is the box bracket's. **(2) §4.4af — the acked-writes loss on the death path, ATTRIBUTED and FIXED (PR 4/12b's page-budget overflow law)**: the 25 misses were 15 files of ONE writer at a 128-name stride = every inode the rotor round-robin minted into ONE slot (with two metadata volumes a joiner's files alternate volumes and the volume where the parent stripe does not live mints by the rotor — `rotor_mints = [940, 65]`; 64 rotor slots ⇒ stride 128), slot 3549, which the manager's recovery found with "NO page entry — the grant-time root 0x0 stands" and an EMPTY window; 18 of that volume's 19 overflow slots had shipped their roots (`slot_roots_shipped`), this one never had — the 5 misses whose `mv` had not run prove a CREATE-record loss, not the rename's. The hole: a LEASED slot's root rides its lessee's page (KD-SYM-3) and the page names the 108 LOWEST slots by page slot; the cut is DYNAMIC — a later first touch of a LOWER slot (the storm's `rm -rf`, the stripes, slot 35 at 00:04:28) pushed a PAGE-published slot into the overflow with its `published` mark intact, so `roots_to_publish` excluded it, `PublishRoots` never shipped it, the page no longer named it and the tail had passed its records: a root named nowhere durable. Fixed in the forest's publication bookkeeping shared by both appender kinds: a publication remembers its HOME (`forest::PublicationHome::{Tree0, Page}` — `note_page_published` marks the page's, every tree-0 writer marks tree 0's); `KvMetaBackend::page_plan` is ONE order for every page writer (review round 1, Issue 2 made it the ONE page writer's INVARIANT, not a property of the two cycle sites): the rank is by what a slot LOSES if the page drops it — the entry mid-handover first (the caller's `releasing`, or the table's `Releasing` state, so the cadence's own page write names it during the flush cycles), then every slot whose tree only a PAGE names (`SlotTrees::page_homed` — a page-homed publication at ANY root: at the live root the page is the root's only home, at an OLDER root it is the only home of the records flushed under that root, which the live root's floor no longer covers — review round 2, Issue 12: the round-1 predicate required `root == live`, so a slot whose root SPLIT in the same cycle's flush pass ranked with the never-published and a lower first touch cut it off the page with its `R1` records below the tail; pin `a_root_split_in_the_parked_cycles_flush_pass_never_drops_the_slots_page_home`, RED on that predicate), then every UNPUBLISHED slot whose last home is tree 0 (its floor keeps every record since tree 0's root in the window), then the slots tree 0 names at their current root and the native slot (the ledger's root), each in page-slot order, the budget cutting the tail (a slot with no tree has nothing to name) — and **every page write of a leased region takes its entries from `prepare_page_entries`** (the checkpoint cycle's `write_appender_pages`, the handover's steps 3 and 5, the leave), which publishes a page-homed slot the plan still cuts into tree 0 BEFORE the page drops it (the manager: one control entry rewriting its `Leased` record with the current root in the checkpoint class inside a cycle, the user class from a handover; a joined appender: `PublishRoots` awaited — `wire_publish_roots`, the cycle's overflow publication's own function) — a publication that cannot land fails the page write and the previous image, which named the slot, stands; `SlotTrees::demote_page_publications` — an off-page slot loses a page-homed publication (it reads unpublished, its floor re-armed at the region ring's `reusable_upto`), run by the cycle's bulk publication (`publish_forest_roots` / `joined_publish_overflow_roots`, BEFORE the tail computation and the page write) and by the page writer's publish-before-drop. The first fix's rank put the unpublished ahead of the page-homed and enforced the order at the two cycle sites alone, so a first touch landing between the cycle's publication and its page write, a handover's `Releasing` entry promoted to the page's head on a region holding the budget in page-homed roots, or the leave's all-`Releasing` page could still evict a page-homed slot un-demoted for a cadence. Gauge `meta_kv_forest_page_publications_demoted`. Pins (each RED on the first build at the durable level and at the acked-writes level): `a_page_published_root_pushed_off_the_page_by_a_lower_first_touch_rides_tree_zero_before_the_lessee_dies` (twenty lower unpublished first touches evict nothing; the lower roots ship to tree 0), `releasing_an_overflow_slot_of_a_page_full_region_publishes_the_slot_it_pushes_off_first` (the handover shape: the 109th `Releasing` entry pushes the rotor — every file's inode — off; the joiner ships its root before the page drops it; the holder dies after step 3 (`TEST_HANDOVER_HOLD_AFTER_PAGE`), the recovery loses nothing), `a_first_touch_between_the_cycles_publication_and_its_page_write_evicts_no_page_homed_root` (the manager's cycle parked before its page write — `TEST_CHECKPOINT_PARK_BEFORE_PAGES` — a first touch + lazy mint of a lower slot lands under it, the manager dies after the page write, the successor opens every file); `sym_slot_transfer_tests`' page-budget pin follows the selection law. The fleet's `sym-storm` ×10 count restarts from zero on this binary. **(3) §4.4ag — a joiner's read inside a manager failover, FIXED**: the successor's token plane's membership screen answers the TYPED `TokenReply::NotMember` (`STATUS_REFUSED`), which `call_on` mints as `RefusalClass::MembershipPending` (EAGAIN, wire 4; the screen's reason in the text); a token FETCH (`call_parking`) and the serve gate over a channel whose last round was the screen's (`serve_gate_parking`, `channel_membership_pending`) park on the member's grant adoption (`membership::await_grant_adopted` — `note_grant_adopted` is the one writer of `GRANT_GENERATION`, a Notify beside it) bounded by `reassertion_wait_bound()` = 2 × `renewal_beat_ms()` and retry; past the bound the retryable class surfaces, never `EIO`; ONLY while this process holds a member session (`installed_member_session()` — a ghost keeps the shipped fail-closed word at once); the recall channel keeps its loop (it re-polls on the next adopted grant instead of a dead holder's backoff), the arm's probe / acks / releases never park; a `CustodyGrant` answered `NotMember` surfaces the typed class for the acquire ladder's own retry. Gauge `dlm_token_membership_waits` (reader side, per volume). Pin: `sym_coherence_tests::a_read_refused_not_a_member_parks_on_the_members_reclaim_and_never_answers_eio` (a manual lease oracle: the read parks, the reclaim lands, the read completes exact; no reclaim inside the bound → `MembershipPending`/EAGAIN, then serves again). **Rounds 3–4 (the `sym-crash` legs' two shapes, each red-first — `a595c35a`, `30ed26dc`)**: the park's bound starts at the FIRST refusal, never at entry (the `call` ahead of it spent the whole bound re-dialing the dead manager's listener, so the class reached `stat(2)` at once — `deadline.get_or_insert`; a one-shot `TokenReaderPlane::test_delay_next_call_ms` seam stands in for the dead dial), and a fresh per-holder plane's ARM PROBE rides `call_parking` too (`data_grant::foreign_read_plane` dials a fresh plane for a joiner's first read of a holder after the failover and probed it with a plain call — the class surfaced through the probe; a ghost with no member session keeps the fail-closed word); legs 3 and 4 of the same contract. `sym-crash --rounds=3` GREEN 3/3 from zero on `30ed26dc`. **The fleet leg** `tests/run_mw_matrix.sh sym-foreign-file [--ff-files=F]`: N joined writers `chmod` / `touch` / `setfattr` / append+fsync / truncate / `rm` a COLLEAGUE's fsynced files under the acked-writes + deleted-stays-deleted oracle, read back at the holder and at a third mount; laws `Σ record_ships ≡ Σ record_served`, `record_refusals == 0`, `record_unreachable == 0`, `Σ foreign_publish_ships ≡ foreign_publish_served`, `dlm_custody_via_slot_holder ≥ 1` per writer, fsck clean; the storm's `LOST` lines carry each stat's errno (§4.4af's attribution recipe). **The leg found two defects on the ship's own face, each fixed red-first** (GREEN ×3 from zero after): (a) **an ACKED-WRITE LOSS — a token recall dropped the WRITER's dirty layout** (`85e42408`): under PR 12b's divert a writer is a token client of the holders it writes to, and `MountRecallSink::purge_scoped` ran `discard_layout_cache` UNCONDITIONALLY — PR 5's `-o ro` reader law, where an entry is never dirty; the kernel's post-write times echo shipped as a `Setattr`, the holder's commit recalled the writer's own token, the purge dropped the inline append's `layout_dirty` entry (its `data_key` the ONLY copy of the acked bytes — "RAM only until fsync/release"), and the `fsync` found nothing to save and returned 0 with the bytes in no KV (m62 `meta_kv_journal_entries 0`, `meta_ship_publish.shipped 1` = the `ParkWriteTimes` sibling); the sink keeps a DIRTY entry (`fetch_metadata`'s law: a dirty layout is the LOCAL AUTHORITY, cleaned only under `INODE_META_LOCKS` by the paths that persist it), decided under the ino's (3.5) guard in its NON-PARKING form (`meta_lock_try_acquire` — a held stripe is the write or the persist whose shipped publish caused the very recall the holder is waiting on; parking would hold its pass to the recall deadline) with a held stripe SKIPPING the discard, safe because the recall's epoch step already expires every pre-step clean entry (`layout_entry_pre_step`); gauges `dlm_token_recall_{dirty_kept,discard_skipped}`; pin `sym_coherence_tests::a_recall_never_drops_the_writers_dirty_layout_entry`. (b) **the HOLDER read a served publish's OLD bytes for the inode's life** (`d0872ace`; `drop_caches` cured it): the served commit lands at the holder's KV BELOW its own caches — the router's RAM entry, the attr cache — and below the KERNEL's inode, which under `FUSE_WRITEBACK_CACHE` owns a cached regular inode's size / mtime / ctime for its life (`fuse_get_cache_mask`: every attr reply's size is replaced by `i_size_read`; `fuse_reverse_inval_inode` never moves `i_size`); `record_ship::note_served_mutation` (the served record verbs + the S9 served layout publish, group path included) runs the mount's sink (`install_served_mutation_sink`, the armed set's mount arm only): the attr-cache entry dropped whole, the router entry discarded through the own-mint reclaim funnel, then `make_served_mutation_kernel_hook` — `FUSE_NOTIFY_INVAL_INODE` (attrs-only for a record verb, attrs + pages for a publish) THEN `FUSE_NOTIFY_PRUNE` (uapi 7.45 — the fork gained `FUSE_NOTIFY_PRUNE = 9`, `fuse_notify_prune_out`, `prune_frame`, `Notify::{prune,prune_detached}`) when the kernel speaks it AND the writeback cache was negotiated: `d_prune_aliases` drops the unreferenced dentries, `generic_delete_inode` evicts the inode, its next lookup re-instantiates it from the daemon's attrs — the ONE act that makes such a kernel adopt a peer's change; both frames detached over the classical sideband (the W1 `notify_inval_inode` venue law); an inode a process holds OPEN at the holder is the stated residual (pages dropped, size / times the kernel's until its last close — every FUSE writeback-cache filesystem's foreign-writer law); a kernel below 7.45 is announced once and gets the invalidation alone; gauges `meta_ship.served_mutation_{invals,prunes}`; pins `tests/served_mutation_kernel_tests.rs` (the frame order and forms, the incapable-kernel arm, the venue, and the FLAT/unarmed law — a whole unarmed writer session never reaches the sink). **The holder's sink obeys the SAME dirty law as the recall's (review round 1, Issue 1 — the holder-side mirror of `85e42408`)**: the first build ran `discard_layout_cache` UNCONDITIONALLY, so a colleague's grant-less `chmod` / `setxattr` dropped the holder's OWN dirty layout entry (an inline append's `data_key`, a staged write's pending merge + C8 delta) and the holder's `fsync` landed nothing; both sinks now share ONE discard, `DataRouter::discard_clean_layout_entry` — decided under the ino's (3.5) stripe in its non-parking form (`meta_lock_try_acquire`), a DIRTY entry KEPT (`meta_ship.served_mutation_dirty_kept`), a CLEAN entry dropped, a HELD stripe skipping the discard (`served_mutation_discard_skipped`; no epoch step runs at a holder, the entry's 1 s `cached_at` TTL expires it) — and a served LAYOUT PUBLISH beside a dirty entry is two writers under one custody, reported on `invariant_tripwires` as `served_publish_over_dirty_entry` with the dirty entry kept; pin `sym_n_daemon_tests::a_served_chmod_never_drops_the_holders_dirty_layout_entry` (a `SqueezefsFilesystem` in front of the HOLDER with the real sink: the holder's unfsynced inline append, the colleague's shipped `chmod`, the holder's `fsync` lands the bytes and every mount reads them, the served mode beside them; RED on the first build at the dropped entry). `foreign_publish_ships` counts the LAYOUT class the holder counts (`494ba885` — the count moved from `publish_target` to the four witnessed ship sites; the `ParkWriteTimes` sibling belongs to neither side). **The storm ×10's round 4 found PR 6's belt `xv_local_step_unguarded` firing on the MANAGER (`b806b9a5`)**: defect 29's re-dispatch one step further — a shipped step's holder answered `SlotBusy { holder: the initiator }` (the dominance rule / PR 10's recovery handed the slot to the initiator mid-plan) and the re-dispatch applied the step LOCALLY under guards that had TRAVELLED to the old holder, so nothing in the initiator's table covered the key; the belt's "cannot happen" was false (a handover is a legal schedule) and the apply ran unguarded. The local re-dispatch now takes the step's own guards in the NON-PARKING canonical form — `DlmLockManager::try_lock_many`, `lock_many`'s deduped ascending plan with the `try_*` fast path, all-or-nothing, because a parking acquire while the op holds its other guards could cycle with a peer's canonical set — holds them across the apply (`xv_cross_owner_step_late_guards`), answers a contended stripe with the slot-moved retryable class (`xv_cross_owner_step_late_guard_refusals`: the bounded re-dispatch, past it the intent stays open for the cadence — the ship-failure posture), and a key the op's OWN scope has parked in this table (the one-process holder model) takes nothing (`own_parked_scope_covers`); the belt is retired; pins `a_step_whose_slot_moved_to_the_initiator_mid_plan_applies_under_its_own_guard` (RED with the fleet's tripwire) + the round-2 belt pin flipped to the new law. **The recall's third-mount face** (`743ee197`, found by the fidelity leg's real kernel): a token this mount holds on a FOREIGN object is recalled by its holder's commit, and the recall now invalidates this mount's attr cache and kernel view of the object like a served mutation (`record_ship::install_recalled_object_sink`, run from `MountRecallSink::purge_scoped`; the router's layout entry stays the dirty law's) — the manager, having read a joiner's file before a colleague appended to and `chmod`ed it, had kept the old bytes and mode for the inode's life; fidelity `quick` PASS=130 FAIL=0 on the final binary, the `sym-join-ladder` leg 87/87. **The fleet counts, each named to the sha it ran on (laptop — "it works" only)**: `sym-foreign-file` GREEN ×3 from zero on `b806b9a5`, `sym-crash --rounds=3` GREEN 3/3 on `b806b9a5`, `sym-storm --rounds=3 --victims=7 --cross-owner --striped` GREEN 3/3 on `30ed26dc`; `743ee197` (the recall-side kernel invalidation) after them ran the fidelity `quick` tier only, and review fix round 1 re-ran them on ITS binary `13ecfea5` (Issues 1 and 2 change product on the armed plane): `sym-foreign-file` GREEN ×3 from zero (`record_ships=768 ≡ record_served=768`, `foreign_publish_ships=192 ≡ served=192`, `foreign_publish_refusals=0`, `invariant_tripwires 0` on every writer) and `sym-crash --rounds=3` GREEN 3/3 from zero; **the storm ×10 is NOT MET** — 8/10 (metadata ENOSPC on the 1 GiB rig namespace, below), 2/10 (a harness precondition, fixed), 9/10 (a joiner's tree-0 PROJECTION routing loop `[0, 0, 0, 0, 256]` — PR 13 defect 34's class recurring under seven rejoins' tree-0 churn, the refresh finding no new ledger seq; owed with its recipe), LOST 0 in every one of the 19 seven-victim rounds — §4.4af's loss class did not recur. **Found by the storm ×10, stated, not built (PR 14): every rejoin mints a FRESH rotor (64 slot trees × 256 KiB per volume) and an emptied unleased slot tree keeps its root leaf for ever** — the seven-victim storm eats ≈ 400 metadata extents per round per volume (fsck C13 clean: reachable empty roots), the rig's 1 GiB namespace met the §4.7 heap-full refusal at round 9 (metadata `ENOSPC` at a flip, refused loud, nothing lost); remedy = free an EMPTY unleased slot tree's root at the LRU release / the cadence, or re-acquire the predecessor's recovered rotor at the rejoin; the ×10 count ran on `SQZ_DEVSUB_MDS_GB=4`. **Stated, not built — PR 7's un-share of a surviving sole owner stays PR 14's**: not a record-level ship of an existing verb but a two-party protocol (the index home decides `remaining == 1`, the owner's slot holder clears the durable SHARED bit CONDITIONALLY, the home retires its `shared_ref:` entries) whose safety needs a share-in-flight witness across owner and home (a clone's `MarkShared` racing the clear would let a W1 patch rewrite a block a clone is about to reference) — a new verb pair + witness + fuzz/loom evidence; the deferral's cost is W1's CoW fallback on a once-cloned block (`patch_ineligible_shared`), never a correctness loss. ### Symmetric metadata program — PR 13c, the box campaign (2026-09-22, DARK; incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`)

**PR 13c is the FIX rung for what the box brackets found** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-19-sym-acceptance.md` §3.9 — F-B1 / F-B2 / F-B3, the gate-1 flat-path regression, gate 3's N = 8 term; §3.9.3 the gate-3 attribution; design §8 rows 3 / 3c / 5 / 7 carry "fix landed PR 13c, re-run owed"), every fix red-first, no box row run (the orchestrator re-runs gates 1 / 3 / 3c / 5 / 7@N=32 from zero on this binary — the counted-run law). **F-B3 — the cluster-wire LISTENER cap** (`cluster_wire::max_connections_from(raw_cpus, nofile_soft)` = `(raw × 16).clamp(64, nofile / 8)`): the RAW affinity mask is the root — `crate::cpu::raw_parallelism`, the **fleet-width exemption class** beside `possible_cpus` (a listener's load is the whole fleet's width, which N co-located daemons each serve; the fleet-share-divided form derived 64 on a 32-member fleet on 32 cores and refused the 14th member at accept), 16 parked connection threads per core (the thread-per-connection posture), the shipped 64 the floor, the fd budget the ceiling (`CONNECTION_FDS` = 2 — the socket + its shutdown-nudge clone — × `LISTENER_FD_SHARE` = 4, the `uring_fs::fd_cache_cap` law); **the daemon raises its soft `RLIMIT_NOFILE` to the hard limit at startup** (`raise_nofile_soft_limit`, `main.rs`); `SQUEEZEFS_CLUSTER_WIRE_MAX_CONNS` (64..=65536) explicit-wins railed by the fd ceiling; `member_session_demand_from(cpus, volumes)` = `volumes × (publish_ship_depth + 1) + publish_ship_depth + 4` is the tie-tested per-member demand (32 × 12 = 384 ≤ 512 at share 32 on 32 CPUs; the consumer census of `raw_parallelism(` is pinned in `derivation_sweep_tests`); the dial side RETRIES a listener that accepts and closes before its challenge (a doubling backoff `DIAL_TIMEOUT/500 → /20` inside the dial bound — `RpcClient::connect_sync` / `connect_once`) then surfaces the TYPED `RefusalClass::ListenerRefused` (wire word 5, EAGAIN) — never the first-EOF `InvalidOperation` the box's 14th member died on; the accept-side refusal names the count and the lever. **The second F-B3 defect**: the kernel's `default_permissions` walk GETATTRs the mount ROOT before `/.stats`, so a token reader's root fetch surfaced the wire error as EINVAL on the operator's instrument — a `-o ro` token reader whose plane answers a TRANSIENT class (`KvMetaBackend::is_transient_wire_error`: the transport failure, `ListenerRefused` / `MembershipPending` / `HolderUnreachable`, `ManagerUnreachable`) serves the ROOT's attrs from its local projection (`dlm_token_root_projection_serves`) — R-SYM-4's ONE named exception, the root alone; every child resolve stays fail-closed; the class is STRUCTURAL — the plane's LAW refusals (`fail_closed`: past `T_self`, a holder's decoded refusal) are typed `Refused { EIO }` and never read as the wire's (the matrix's R20 contract caught the first build serving a fenced reader's root), a recall channel with no fresh session wears `Io(NotConnected)` (EIO, the shipped errno — the wire's word). Pins: `derivation_sweep_tests` (×3 + the census), `cluster_wire_tests` (the retry lands when a slot frees / fails typed inside the bound), `posix_errno_tests`, `sym_coherence_tests::a_readers_root_attr_survives_the_holders_connection_cap_so_stats_never_fail` + `a_reader_past_t_self_serves_nothing_from_cache`. **F-B1 — the flush-ceiling audit's margin is ONE law over the SMO mutex's structural holds** (defect 33's recovery extension generalized): `epoch_core::StructuralHolds` on `NodeEnv` keeps a monotone Σ of hold time per class (`HoldClass::{Recovery, Service}`; nested guards bracket one hold; the one mutex serializes them so the Σ never double counts), a forest-slot leaf stamps both Σs at its clean → dirty transition beside `dirty_since_ns` (`CachedNode::dirty_since_held_ns`; a flat volume's nodes read nothing), and `note_flush_ceiling` (`DirtyLeafAge`) excludes `Σ(now) − Σ(stamp)` from the leaf's age — a recovery's overlap up to the published `appender_recovery_bound_ms`, a service hold's measured whole (the service is priced on `manager_service_ns`) — counting an excused leaf on `appender_flush_ceiling_{recovery,service}_extensions`; the pass's own wall past the ceiling stays the must-stay-0 overrun. Service holds are registered AFTER the mutex (a wait behind the pass is the pass's time) at the manager's wire slot grant (`manager_acquire_slots`) and release (`manager_release_slot_wire`), the region release, the wire lessee's transfer adoption (`install_wire_grant`), the projection refresh, the joiner's leave, the recovered-region release (recovery class), and around the joiner's reactive wire refill INSIDE its flush pass; `StructuralHold` replaces `RecoveryHold` (the RAM words `recovery_holds` / `recovery_hold_last_end_ns` retired); `test_hold_smo_as_service` is the seam; pin `sym_appender_tests::a_leaf_that_aged_under_a_service_hold_of_the_smo_mutex_is_an_extension_not_an_overrun` (the parked-device overrun still counts — the exclusion is exact). **F-B2 — the dominance rule's `ops_h` counts the holder's work on the slot's SUBTREE** (design §5.1.4 as built): the commit door (`ensure_leases_for_tx`) notes every dentry-bearing commit on the tx's own slots AND on the parent directory's ANCESTOR slots through `KvMetaBackend::install_liveness_ancestors` (`LivenessAncestors`, installed by `RoutedMetaBackend::install_liveness_ancestry` in the two armed opens — a directory's LOCAL ino → its ancestors' forest slots on that volume, walked up the `dir_parents` memo to the root, bounded by `LIVENESS_CHAIN_MAX` = `PATH_MAX / 2`; a memo miss credits nothing, an ancestor on ANOTHER metadata volume is credited on THAT volume's plane as the walk passes it (`KvMetaBackend::note_subtree_holder_op` — `pick_mint_volume` mints a directory's children round-robin over the volumes, so a job's storm directory routinely lives on the other volume from the directory a requester touches: the fleet's two-volume `sym-foreign-touch` PAUSED phase found the one-volume first build), slots this mount does not lease are skipped, a served step still counts for the requester alone), so a job live anywhere below a directory keeps that directory's slot while the IDLE arm, the trickle-holder reclaim and the crowd law are unchanged; the box's shape — a storm under `job-wA/live/r*` (spilled to the rotor) beside 64-touch bursts into `job-wA`, `N_floor` 2 — is pinned RED-first at ship 2 (`OfferDominated`) by `sym_slot_transfer_tests::a_holder_live_below_a_directory_is_never_recalled_by_a_burst_into_it` + `…_on_another_volume_keeps_the_directorys_slot` (the fleet's two-volume shape, RED at ship 2 on the one-volume build). **Gate 3's N = 8 term is the co-located VENUE's** (§3.9.3, decided from the row's own `.stats`: 8 daemons at 8.4 of 32 cores, no product wall — conveyor ρ ≈ 0.3, door parks 0, guard waits ≈ 0 — every RAM-only phase +40–58 % per op uniformly, ≈ 600 of the ≈ 700 µs per-create latency growth OUTSIDE the daemon's meta op); design §8's gate-3 row is judged on creates per daemon-CPU-second on a co-located venue (the `sym-scale` table's `C/CPU-S` column — the create phase alone, a snapshot between the create and the ingest) beside the wall multiple, and `MGR_CPU` divides by the row's whole wall (the first build's 1,322 % was 9.8 CPU-s ÷ the 0.74 s ingest wall). **The gate-1 flat-path regression**, attributed on the laptop (the in-process routed+KV A-B-B-A microbench `tests/meta_flat_path_microbench.rs` against `3228fcb8`: create / mkdir / unlink par, rename +0.4–1.6 µs, lookup / getattr +30–80 ns; the mount A-B-B-A with `SQUEEZEFS_OP_PROFILE=1`: `dlm_guard_hold` +1 per rename = the PR-4 rename lock-set fix's shape, the box's +100 k) and by the ONE approved `perf record` leg per arm on the box (`fuse3-tpc*` samples +2.4 %: `memmove` +280 — half the delta, callers unnamed past glibc's frame — the `RouteTable` arc-swap loads +78, moka + jemalloc +143, `lock_stripe` +32, `token_serve` +30): the unarmed entry points now pay `Option` probes — `stripe_map` / `stripe_unlink_prelude` answer an unarmed set (`stripes_armed_any`) BEFORE any route-table load, the directory-parent memo is fed on an ARMED set only (`dir_parents_armed` — every reader runs behind the plane; pin `an_unarmed_mount_feeds_the_directory_parent_memo_nothing`), and `token_serve` returns in two probes on a flat volume; the rename lock-set fix's +1 guard and one lookup per rename (≈ +2.6 µs on `rename.backend`) is the priced cost that stays, and the memmove term (a per-op state-size growth of the routed mutation futures) is what the gate-1 bracket on this binary re-reads. Harness: `run_mw_matrix.sh sym-scale`'s `C/CPU-S` column + the `MGR_CPU` fix. Docs: `docs/operations.md` "Symmetric metadata program — the box campaign (PR 13c, DARK)"; design §5.1.4 / §8.

### Metadata-throughput program (Implemented 2026-07)

The 4.4× FUSE-layer metadata multiple was closed by the M1–M12 program — design + landed-SHA record `docs/design-metadata-throughput.md`, gate adjudication `.benchmarks/2026-07-15-metadata-throughput-closing.md`, beta-blocker fix `.benchmarks/2026-07-15-find-m11a-fix.md`. What is now load-bearing machinery (do not regress):

* **Single-writer mount guard (D0)** — every write mount claims each meta volume: a **dedicated daemon-lifetime `flock`** (same-host, kernel-arbitrated, instant crash reclaim), **NVMe Persistent Reservations** Write-Exclusive where `RESCAP` advertises support (cross-host *enforcement* — a fenced holder fail-stops at its first post-fence journal barrier), and a **`writer_claim`** heartbeat record (identity + detection everywhere). **No bypass knob**; automatic cross-host takeover is disabled on non-PR substrates — recovery is dead-pid proof → PR preempt → TTL → the attested **`squeezefs claim clear <sqmeta-uri>`** verb, in that order of automation. The guarantee-class table ships in docs/operations.md verbatim; the barrier-failure escalation governs journal-durability barriers only (the data-path writeback ladder stays retry-forever — see below).
* **Commit conveyor (group commit v2, D5)** — all user commits on a volume enqueue `{staged records, Arc<[DlmGuard]>, oneshot}`; a detached, panic-guarded per-volume **pass task** drains a batch and runs one admission → union-leaf-lock pass → **N ordinary checksummed entries** in one contiguous reservation → one write → one barrier. **One tx = one checksummed journal entry is unchanged** (zero on-disk format change; whole-tx atomicity + torn-write immunity transfer verbatim). Queue entries co-own their tx's DLM guards until terminal outcome — a dropped committer future cannot strand isolation. **Two stages since D-2 (e2e perf audit DLM board #2, 2026-09-03, `.benchmarks/2026-09-03-d2-two-stage-conveyor.md`)**: the pass is the APPLY stage only — it drops its leaf locks and **submits** the batch's entries (one `uring_fs` push, no wait), and a per-volume **durability lane** (the same loom-modeled `ConveyorCore` handoff protocol) awaits the writes in handoff order and takes each group (the head window + every successor already landed) through ONE completed-prefix wait, the §4.4 pt 4 hole checkpoints, ONE strict barrier and the fan-out in journal order — so the device's completion latency never sits inside the serialized server (the D-1b authority ran its pass at ρ 0.97 with 650 of 752 µs being the ring write's round trip). N windows sit between the stages, bounded by the ring's admissible capacity (every window holds a registered reservation; admission parks before any node lock) — never a constant. Acks stay in journal order and only after the entry landed / the barrier completed; guards release at the STAGE-B terminal outcome; the fail-stop lattice fires from stage B; the lane takes leaf locks in exactly one arm — the §4.4 pt 4 rollback of a FAILED write (the 4b argument is `backend.rs`'s module doc + `stripe_locks.rs`). Engagement `meta_conveyor_windows_inflight[_hwm]` (1 = serialized, ≥ 2 = overlapped) / `meta_conveyor_durability_passes`; phases `meta_txpass_phase_ns.{window_lane_wait,window_total}` (`pass_total` is now the apply stage's service time — ρ = Σ ÷ wall reads the serialized server), `lock_phase_ns.leaf_lock_hold` (the "never across device I/O" tripwire). Test seam `uring_fs::arm_device_latency` (a SLOW device — writes/barriers complete no earlier than the armed latency; the stalls are PARKED devices). **Both stages run on the volume's own journal lane since C-2 (DLM board #3, 2026-09-03, `.benchmarks/2026-09-03-c2-uring-fs-completion-hop.md`)**: one OS thread per writable volume (`sqz-jrnl{N}`, derived — never a constant; readers / co-writers / peer-owned volumes commit nothing and get none) that OWNS the volume's journal io_uring and parks IN it (`sqz_exec::LanePark` + `uring_fs::OwnedReactor`: a task wake and a device CQE are one `io_uring_enter` wait; the lane reaps its own CQEs, so a completion never crosses a thread to reach the durability task and never waits behind the `sqz-meta` lanes' serve work — the same-binary fleet bracket measured that shared-lane `wake_hop` at 59–62 % of the journal write's round trip: `uring_fs_write_phase_ns.{queue_hop,device,wake_hop,total}`, always-on, exact-sum). `SQUEEZEFS_JOURNAL_LANE=0` is the A/B control (the D-2 shape on the shared pool); engagement `journal_ring_lane_writes` ≈ passes (`journal_ring_pool_writes` carries the rest), `journal_lanes_{spawned,ringless}`, CPU class `sqz-jrnl`. The residue is the io-wq punt a buffered block-device write takes (`blkdev_write_iter` refuses NOWAIT buffered writes — no inline completion at `submit()` on any venue), two kernel wakes per window. **Group commit since D-1c (e2e perf audit §5.3 row 1, 2026-09-04, `.benchmarks/2026-09-04-d1c-conveyor-group-per-frame.md`)**: `ConveyorCore::enqueue_many` pushes a SET of staged txs under ONE queue-lock acquisition (drain takes the same lock, so no drain ever sees a partial group — loom `conveyor_group_never_drains_partially`), `KvMetaBackend::commit_tx_group` runs the pipeline over the set (every member built and admitted first — an inadmissible member fails ALONE and never enqueues; one enqueue, one election, fan-outs awaited in order), and the group's 4a guards are ONE canonical `lock_many` (distinct inos may share a DLM stripe — per-member guards taken while siblings hold theirs would self-deadlock). Queue-side only: one tx = one checksummed journal entry unchanged, the drain caps still govern (the byte cap may split an over-cap group, progress-first), replay identical to single commits (pinned). Gauges `meta_conveyor_group_{commits,txs}` (txs ÷ commits = the live group size; 0 on a solo mount by construction).
* **Journal-entry economy (D4)** — rename is one whole-tx entry (incl. parent Δtimes); the kernel's SETATTR ctime/mtime echo is absorbed, not committed; unlink destroys ride batched `destroy_inodes`. Measured **≈ 1.0 entries/op rename/unlink** (gate G4 ≤ 1.05) — regressions show in `meta_kv_journal_entries` per-op ratios.
* **Fold overlay + snapshot memo (D7)** — record folds serve from fold-forward overlay heads and immutable-snapshot memos (fold algebra untouched, property-tested equivalence; replay rides the same fold). `InodeDelta::decode` fell off the profile (26.3 % → < 0.5 % of daemon CPU); memo/overlay bytes are charged to the node-cache budget.
* **Watchdog, not per-op timeouts (D1.b)** — handlers no longer wrap in `tokio::time::timeout` (that expiry *dropped* futures mid-`commit_tx` — the ring-budget/reservation-wedge vector). A deadline watchdog logs overdue ops loudly (`fuse_op_watchdog_overdue`); ring-admission parking past the threshold escalates via the `disabled_volumes` fail-stop lattice; barrier waits keep bounded errors. Do not reintroduce per-op timeout wrappers.
* **Round-trip + transport economy (D2/D3)** — clean-handle FLUSH/RELEASE fast paths, `FOPEN_NOFLUSH`, kernel-side negative-entry caching, refresh-instead-of-invalidate parent attrs (fuse_ops/create 5.18 → ~4.0); over-uring COMMIT_AND_FETCH submits batch per drain (`io_uring_enter`/create 31 → ~9). SQPOLL on the queue rings exists as a knob and measured **not recommended** (M10; README posture note).
* **Supersession-aware never-lossy writeback (FIND-M11-A fix)** — flush units re-validate staged ownership per attempt and present the ino's *current* DLM generation; fencing-stale units resolve as contractual no-ops, verified-NotFound (reclaimed-ino) units discard their orphans, and everything genuinely transient still retries forever. "Stale fencing tokens discard staged work" is the **remount** contract, not a license to drop live acked custody.
* **Env/mount knobs added:** `SQUEEZEFS_META_COMMIT_BATCH_TXS` (default derived `max(64, cpus × 2)`) / `SQUEEZEFS_META_COMMIT_BATCH_BYTES` (default derived `max(256 KiB, ring/16)`, clamped to the ring's admissible capacity) — conveyor batch caps (floors = the shipped M7 posture, derivation sweep 2026-08-04); `SQUEEZEFS_OP_PROFILE=1` — per-op phase histograms + the under-`i_rwsem` estimator (zero cost off); per-class kernel TTLs `-o attr_timeout/entry_timeout/dir_entry_timeout/negative_timeout` / `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_{ATTR,ENTRY,DIR_ENTRY,NEGATIVE}_TTL_MS` (default 1 s each); `mount --daemon --supervise` external watchdog (+ `SQUEEZEFS_SUPERVISE_{INTERVAL,UNRESPONSIVE}_SECS`) with FUSE-connection abort.
* **Stats families added (stats inode):** `writer_guard_{mode,fenced,pr_reacquires}`; `meta_commit_group_{size,bytes}`, `meta_conveyor_{leader_passes,pass_panics}`; `meta_kv_fold_{head_serves,memo_hits,memo_misses,memo_bytes}`; `meta_kv_times_echo_{absorbed,pending,drained,drain_commits}`; `fuse_op_phase_ns` / `fuse_create_under_lock_ns` / `fuse_op_watchdog_overdue`; `fuse_{flush,release}_clean_fastpath`, `fuse_lookup_negative_replies`, `fuse_attr_cache_refreshes`; `transport_commit_batch*`; `meta_reclaim_gather_*` + `meta_reclaim_inflight_waits` (finding 50, 2026-09-03: a reclaim batch that finds an ino claimed by a concurrent batch never double-drives AND never returns before that owner's outcome — `reclaim_orphaned_batch(inos).await` ⇒ every ino reached its terminal outcome; the gauge counts the parks, growth = the release-enqueued pool racing a FORGET/explicit reclaim); `writeback_{superseded_noops,stale_token_retries,orphan_discards}`.

### Random-small-write program (Implemented 2026-07)

The ~2,500× whole-block RMW amplification on small random writes (the vs-JuiceFS scoreboard's only genuine product loss: 354–397 IOPS) was closed by the RW1–RW5 program — design + landed-SHA record `docs/design-random-small-writes.md`, closing adjudication `.benchmarks/2026-07-17-rand-write-program-closing.md`. Load-bearing machinery (do not regress):

* **Sole-owner extent patch (W1)** — isolated LBA-aligned small overwrites of exclusively-owned, passthrough, whole-block-mapped striped blocks are **one in-place sub-block DMA** (zero reads / meta / staging; 61–67 k IOPS, W all scoreboard regimes). The predicate lives in ONE place (`is_whole_block_mapping()` + the 6-clause decision ledger); the clone/patch race is closed by the §5.1 `fence(SeqCst)` protocol on both sides (composed two-word loom model — the fence is load-bearing, verified by weakening). v1 is aligned-only **by contract**: only app-written sectors are ever rewritten (crash blast radius), and `patch_edge_rmw_reads` must stay 0.
* **Coverage-union write-through trigger (RW3b / FIND-L1-A fix)** — block completeness is the ACCUMULATED written-coverage union (`record_write`'s completion transition), never one write's end offset; kernel-split out-of-order O_DIRECT WRITE segments are the normal case (`FOPEN_PARALLEL_DIRECT_WRITES` stays on). Inline write-path seed fetches are deleted; `write_path_seed_read_bytes` is a must-stay-0 tripwire; a fully-covered buffer can never pay a flush-seed read (`seed_deferred ⇒ union partial`, structural).
* **Extent overlay + staged extent records + batched fold (W2)** — patch-ineligible shapes park extents (byte-budgeted, R5-gauged), spill as versioned+checksummed `active_block_ext:` records (**no seed read at spill, ever**), fold seed-once with measured amortization (`fold_fill` median ≥ 16; compressed rand-4k ~2,500× → 15–26×). Recovery: generation-bound + fencing-stamped like staged blocks; future-version records/dirs refuse loud; clean unmount drains records to fold (a clean staging dir carries none); below-RW4 downgrade is declared-unsupported with loud orphan forward-detection.
* **Incompressible store-raw escape (FIND-RW4-A fix)** — compression is best-effort per block (frame bit 31 = raw, below the AEAD layer); transformed volumes reserve chunk headroom at format and mounts refuse geometries that cannot hold `max_stored_image_len(block_size)`; every transformed upload site refuses oversize images loud (never overflow, never truncate).
* **The device overlay's length floor (finding 47, 2026-09-02 — `overlay_length_eligible`, `src/fuse_client.rs`; `tests/overlay_length_floor_tests.rs`; design-overlay-overwrite §5.1)** — the W1 cap is ALSO the B4 device overlay's minimum segment length: overlay-eligible by length ⇔ `len > patch_max_bytes()` (the predicate-5 oversize verdict — the two fast paths tile the sub-block population exactly; `SQUEEZEFS_PATCH_MAX_BYTES=0` empties both the W1 class and the sub-cap class). Before it, a sub-cap write the patch declined at STATE time (hole / clone-shared / decorated / co-writer-refused / stream-adjacent) rode the overlay: a whole CoW dest minted and held per touched block, a whole-block old-image read + complement seed at every settle (`.benchmarks/2026-08-17-mw-shipped-free-c8-fix.md`: six refused 4 KiB patches ⇒ `overlay_gap_seed_old_bytes` = 6 × 4 MiB − 24,576), and small-bs sequential segments stored per op (`wareq-sz` collapsing to bs) instead of accumulating into one whole-block write-through. `overlay_ineligible_sub_cap` is the floor's ledger (BOTH shapes).
* **The device overlay rides the write-pipeline depth governor (W-3, e2e perf audit write board #3, 2026-09-02 — `.benchmarks/2026-09-02-w3-overlay-depth-governor.md`)**: the ACK-early store used to issue OPEN-LOOP (no admission against the BDP target, in-flight bytes on no R5 component, CQEs never feeding the lane — the 0.68–0.80× device-bound-venue loss the overlay notes' own bracket rows recorded). Now `try_device_overlay_store` takes a write-pipeline permit (`WritePipeline::admit_segment` — segment bytes counted against the target at the volume block-size scale) after the registry join and before the claim, and the continuation releases it at the CQE BEFORE the coverage publish (a parked admitter holding the block guard can never wait on a permit whose release needs that guard), feeding `record_completion_sized` on the destination lane so the BDP estimate and the probe-up governor learn from overlay completions too. ONE gauge (`write_pipeline_inflight_bytes`), ONE target, ONE backpressure gate for both write vehicles; ACK-early semantics unchanged — only ADMISSION waits. Lever `SQUEEZEFS_OVERLAY_DEPTH_GOVERNOR` (default on; `0` = the open-loop control); engagement `overlay_governed_stores` (≡ overlay stores on a governed mount); census word `ov_admit`. Field `w_rewrite` A-B-B-A: par within noise, p99.9 −17 %/−33 %; the tcp-devsub adjudication is owed.
* **The WRITE handler's per-op economy (W-6, e2e perf audit write board #7/#10, 2026-09-08, `.benchmarks/2026-09-08-w6-write-handler-economy.md`)**: the warm W1 patch write went **14.6 → 3.06 allocs/op** (the stage-1b write-phase census map kept a derived minimum capacity instead of re-allocating its scc bucket array per write, stack keys for the per-block `active_block:`/`inode_{ino}`/`active_block_ext:` values, the single-block future awaited inline, the patch prelude borrow-only) with the budget pinned at 4/op in `tests/kernel_op_economy_tests.rs` and the three left named as the device layer's; its 17 shared-line METRICS RMWs per write record into per-thread stripes (`ShardedLatencyHistogram` / `ShardedQueueDepthHistogram` / `ShardedAtomic` — every stats key folds on read, meaning unchanged); the zc held-slot mint is ONE `Arc` clone (`ZcWriteSlot::from_connection`) instead of four boxed closures; and the overlay settle's gap seed reads exactly the uncovered span (`SQUEEZEFS_GAP_SEED_RANGED`, default on, `0` = the whole-image A/B; `overlay_gap_seed_ranged_bytes` ⊆ `overlay_gap_seed_old_bytes`, `overlay_gap_seed_read_bytes` the device-byte face; `tests/overlay_gap_seed_ranged_tests.rs`) — transformed volumes and decorated bindings keep the whole read by construction. The field `rw_4k` CPU/op row on squeeze-test is the parent's.
* **The rewrite epoch's supply-coupled close on co-writer lanes (2026-09-07, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-rewrite-epoch-supply-close.md`; design-rewrite-program §5.3 + the KD-1.7 amendment)**: a rewrite epoch parks each displaced A key until a close trigger fires, and on a CO-WRITER the freed key returns only through the recycle loop (ship → grace ring → lane list → harvest RPC) — so KD-1.7's close-on-`StorageFull`-then-retry-once is structurally late there, and on a shared-file rewrite no routine trigger fires during an iteration (the s11 fleet's 9–31 s supply gaps, finding 15's next binding term). `SQUEEZEFS_REWRITE_SUPPLY_CLOSE` (default on; `0` = the shipped KD-1.6/1.7 triggers, the A/B lever) makes the laned co-writer's ahead-refill tick close the mount's open epochs when `lane_reachable_blocks < watermark` (the ahead-harvest's own `rate × horizon` threshold, capped at share/4), largest epoch first until the yield covers the deficit `watermark − reachable` — which is also the per-tick bound (never more closes than blocks short); the close is `close_rewrite_epoch` verbatim, so every §5.6 crash window holds and the write hot path gains nothing; installs only on a harvesting lane (`DataRouter::arm_rewrite_supply_close`), so single-writer / authority mounts are byte-identical. Gauges `rewrite_shadow_supply_closes` / `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_blocks` + the ledger `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_declined_{covered,no_parked}` / `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_bounded`; contracts `tests/rewrite_shadow_supply_close_tests.rs` (incl. the s11 per-volume closed-loop model: parked residence 2.3 → 0.85 s, starvation −54…−59 % where the parking binds). The fleet row is owed.
* **Instrument-alignment lesson (standing)**: elbencho page-aligns its O_DIRECT buffers; `squeezefs bench` (tokio-copied) does not — an unaligned 1 MiB buffer spans max_pages+1 and the kernel **splits it into 2 concurrent out-of-order FUSE WRITEs**. FIND-L1-A hid from one instrument and reproduced under the other for three sessions. **Every measurement must state its instrument**, and write-path changes must stay correct under split/reordered WRITEs (that is now pinned by `tests/write_through_coverage_tests.rs`).
* **Env knobs added:** `SQUEEZEFS_PATCH_MAX_BYTES` (default derived block_size/8 — 512 KiB on the shipped 4 MiB block; `0` = the acceptance A/B lever, not an operational escape), `SQUEEZEFS_FOLD_MAX_EXTENTS` / `SQUEEZEFS_FOLD_MAX_BYTES` (fold triggers, default 64 / block_size/4 — the byte trigger derives, the extent count stays the measured amortization constant).
* **Stats families added (stats inode):** `patch_{writes,write_bytes,dma_errors}`, `patch_edge_rmw_reads` (**0 by definition in v1**), `patch_ineligible_{unmapped,decorated,unaligned,overlay,shared,transform,adjacent,oversize}` (the decision ledger — growth on a shape that should patch = predicate rot); `active_block_ooo_runs`, `write_path_seed_read_bytes` (must stay 0), `write_through_blocks`, `overwrite_seed_{materialized,skipped}`; `parked_extent_bytes` / `parked_full_buffer_bytes` (R5 components), `extent_{parks,escalations,implicit_escalations,spills,spill_bytes,record_absorbs}`, `fold_{passes,seed_reads,extents_folded,fill}`, `staged_rider_{extent_writes,folds}`, `extent_records_{recovered,stale_discarded,torn_discarded,future_refused}`; `compress_stored_raw`.

### Small-file packing (Implemented 2026-09-10; ON by default since PK7)

The 4 KiB–4 MiB population's answer (design `docs/design-small-file-packing.md`, Rev 3.1 — five review rounds; acceptance `.benchmarks/2026-09-10-packing-rows-squeeze-test.md`, local scoping `.benchmarks/2026-09-10-packing-rows-local.md`): a **staged-layout** file (the local-ring write-absorption vehicle stays) PROMOTES INTO the volume's shared **open pack block** — one `LBA_GRAIN`-aligned slot reserved by one `fetch_add`, one DMA of the image at `base + off`, one layout commit publishing the size-carrying `bk:off:len` mapping the read path already decoded, its C8 reference in the SAME tx — instead of taking one whole 4 MiB block per file (the 64× space law the rejected fsync-promotion A/B measured; `.benchmarks/2026-09-09-fsync-promote-staged-ab.md`). Every client reads a packed tenant through the ordinary striped path (cross-client visibility for free); N tenants of one block are N C8 references and the block frees terminally at population 0 through the ordinary ladder — arithmetic that already existed, so **no new incompat bit**. Load-bearing machinery (do not regress):

* **The lever** `SQUEEZEFS_SMALL_FILE_PACKING` (default **ON** since the 2026-09-10 squeeze-test A-B-B-A: files/s +0.5 %, fsync −3.7 %, CPU/file −2.2 %, device/user bytes 1.00× on BOTH arms; 96,000 fsync-promoted 16 KiB files = **375 blocks instead of 96,000**, dismount 20,000 → 148, the legacy volume 20,000 → 147 with one `defrag --pack`) gates the PROMOTION arm only; `0` = the A/B control, the one-block-per-file block arm byte-identical, and a volume packed under `1` reads byte-exact under `0` (rollback = the lever). `SQUEEZEFS_PACK_MAX_SLOT_BYTES` (derived `CHUNK_SIZE/2`, tie-tested) is the ONE law with two faces: a slot above it takes its own block (`pack_own_block_promotions`), and a pack block whose live bytes fall at or below it is a compaction victim.
* **PK1 — the routed read funnel** (`DataRouter::read_mapping_window`, live and lever-independent): every decorated-mapping read is ONE ranged read on the mapping's OWN backend — **FIND-PK-0** (promoted files placed on a non-default data volume read as ZEROS from every other client — the old arm read the default device) and FIND-PK-1 (a 4 MiB prefix read for a tail tenant) fixed; `parse_block_mapping` refuses four malformed shapes at the single choke point (`packed_mapping_refusals`, must-stay-0 — incl. the `unwrap_or(0)` that resolved a bad offset to tenant 0's bytes); a clone of a promoted file pins the shared block (FIND-PK-3). `tests/packed_mapping_wire_tests.rs`.
* **PK2 — the open pack** (`src/pack.rs`: `OpenPack`/`Packer`, RAM-only — the crash contract is the ledger's two durable states, no durable pin record; pin = tenants + 1 the way `move_one` pins a source; per-tenant inflight guards; seal FULL / DISMOUNT / DRAIN / BATCH; the OQ-1 stop latch — on `StorageFull` the pack arm stops for the batch, files stay resident-and-counted, one aggregated WARN), the packed arm via `prepare_promotion`/`commit_promotion` (PK3's landing helpers `land_own_block`/`land_stored_image`/`land_packed_image`/`land_tenant` are the primitives), `BlockAllocator::release_pack_reference`, the pack-open fsck ledger (`fsck_pack_ledger_exempted`). Found and fixed on the way: **FIND-PK-2** (P0 — the promotion published its mapping with NO durable reference; on a ledger-seeded remount every promoted block recovered FREE and the next striped write overwrote promoted files; `bfcf1e57`, `.benchmarks/2026-09-09-promotion-durable-ref-hole.md`), a real fsck C8 two-epoch false positive (one block is every promotion's landing site), **FIND-PK-4** (a FAILED save re-noted its own `+ref` → `retract_block_ref_ops`), **FIND-PK-5** (the staged RMW + inline write arms released a superseded copy's RAM ref without its durable `−ref` → `note_superseded_map_release`).
* **PK3 — tenant ops**: passthrough truncate-shrink of a decorated mapping is a pure mapping re-description (zero device writes, population unchanged — lever-independent: strictly fewer DMAs); transformed shrink lands a NEW tenant; both spill escalations dispatch through the packed arm; clone of a promoted tenant shares the window (nested clone+clip too — the two LEGAL same-`off` share classes); `move_one` defers an OPEN pack and SEALS it when a drain targets the volume (`pack_mover_{open,resident}_defers`, `pack_blocks_sealed_drain`). `tests/pack_tenant_ops_tests.rs`.
* **PK4 — the co-writer batch pack** (`prepare × N → commit_group → seal`, partitioned per `(owner endpoint, home meta volume)` as ONE atomically-enqueued conveyor group; the `pack_group` frame flag, `PUBLISH_SCHEMA` 17, with the owner's `PUBLISH_PACK_GROUP_UNAVAILABLE` 0x57 / post-gate `PUBLISH_PACK_GROUP_SPLIT` 0x58 refusals → abandon + re-`prepare` once → one-block-per-file; **"at most ONE `pack_group` frame ever names a pack block"**; the typed `SqueezefsError::PublishFailure` outcome class preserved end-to-end — no string-parsed outcomes, errno EIO; the authority's served-publish free-block screen 0x59; `Grant::pack_group_available`, `CLUSTER_WIRE_SCHEMA` 4). `tests/mw_cowriter_pack_tests.rs`.
* **PK5 — fsck class C12** (report-only, the C8 posture): Overlap = two live windows on one `(vol, offset)` intersecting at DIFFERENT `off`; Overrun = past the chunk / misaligned / UNDECODABLE decoration (its own tolerant decoder); zero-FP by settle + a fresh re-read under both inos' 4a leases + the open-pack exemption; `fsck_tenant_overlap_findings` must-stay-0. `tests/fsck_c12_tests.rs`.
* **PK6 — compaction on the defrag D1 axis** (`JobType::DefragPack`, `squeezefs defrag --pack` / `--report-only`): occupancy off the census's C8 walk (same-`off` windows counted once; `frag_d1_pack_*`, `pack_reclaimable_bytes`, `pack_blocks_below_half`), the mover with `move_one`'s discipline (pin → per-tenant re-pack through the landing primitives → republish with the C8 delta → release nonterminal → pin dropped LAST = the terminal free via the ladder); the report is BOUNDED on the admin lane (`DefragReport::to_bounded_json`, worst-occupancy prefix + `rows_elided` — **FIND-PK-6**: the legacy volume's one-row-per-block report refused as "reply too large"). The FIRST customer is the legacy one-block-per-file population the shipped dismount pass minted. `tests/pack_compaction_tests.rs`.
* **Found beside the program, on the shipped default path (P0, fixed `d914b673`, `.benchmarks/2026-09-10-overlay-vs-growth-merge-data-loss.md`)**: a buffered file just over one block lost a kernel-split 1 MiB segment (ZEROS after a successful fsync) — `write_striped`, the router's own GUARD-LESS striped RMW, reached when a segment's handler classification went stale across a sibling's promotion, seeded from the device and published a `Merge` holding no block guard, and the one-authority screen superseded the open overlay record's acked bytes. **`write_striped` is deleted**; `write_file` answers `WriteFileOutcome::LayoutStriped` and every caller re-dispatches through the ONE guarded striped path (`dispatch_striped_write`). `overlay_foreign_merge` is a must-stay-0 `invariant_tripwires` label (the `.stats` key is `invariant_tripwires`; the label names the class in the log line) with teeth again. Deterministic repro `tests/overlay_growth_merge_tests.rs`.
* **Stats families:** `layout_promoted_packed`, `pack_own_block_promotions`, `pack_blocks_{opened,sealed_full,sealed_dismount,sealed_batch,sealed_drain,abandoned}`, `pack_slots_abandoned`, `pack_partial_frees`, `pack_release_untracked_noops`, `pack_open_block_{age_ms,occupancy}`, `pack_mapping_clips`, `pack_mover_{open,resident}_defers`, `pack_cowriter_{batches,frames,group_refusals,group_splits}`, `pack_compaction_{passes,victims,tenants_moved,bytes_moved,blocks_freed}`, `frag_d1_pack_*`, `packed_reads`/`packed_read_bytes`/`packed_mapping_refusals`, `fsck_pack_ledger_exempted`, `fsck_tenant_overlap_findings`, `fsck_repair_classC12`, `small_file_packing` (the posture word). A shim client is unaffected by construction: the interposer carries no layout logic, and the direct-drive prelude refuses decorated mappings to the handler path (the same branch clones always took).

### LD_PRELOAD interception data plane (Implemented 2026-07)

The L4 program (`docs/design-preload-interception.md`; closing record `.benchmarks/2026-07-19-l4-interception-closing.md`) shipped the DAOS-libioil-class client bypass: `-o interception` arms a per-mount session host (abstract AF_UNIX + sealed-memfd shm sessions; the §5.2 daemon fd screen is THE security boundary), and `LD_PRELOAD=libsqueezefs_il.so` (built ONLY via `--profile preload-release --features interposers` — the Issue-4 `panic="abort"` compile guard makes wrong-profile builds unrepresentable) routes data ops on bound fds over a lock-free ring. Load-bearing machinery (do not regress): pinned single-consumer service threads (default ceiling = the drain-LANE derivation `squeezefs_ipc::sizing::il_drain_lanes_default` = `clamp(3×cpus/8, 2, 64)` — the counted 2026-08-06 field width sweep, `.benchmarks/2026-08-06-dd-width-slope.md`: one lane = svc submitter + direct-drive reaper = 2 OS threads, sized so the lane-pair population takes ¾ of the core budget, and the SAME function widths the direct-drive shards so the pair cannot drift; it DOMINATES the shim's per-mount session default `il_sessions_default` = `clamp(cpus/4, 2, SESSION_REGISTRY_SLOTS/2)` = `clamp(cpus/4, 2, 16)` at every machine size, so every default session still owns a drain thread — the flat `clamp(cpus/4,2,8)` was the DEFAULTS-MISMATCH constant the 2026-07-28 ingest-economy campaign ran to delete; warm serves execute ON them), the §5.5.1 sync fast path (per-inode `try_read()` + the drop-guard-before-enqueue demote rule + the sync tier serve mirroring the handler's staging/hot/read-cache legs), sever-at-dequeue write custody (§5.5.2), KD-7 build-commit equality (dev override forgives `-dirty` degeneracy, never inequality), KD-11 forced write-through (~3× tax on unintercepted buffered small writes, priced in the closing report), the fork-child `close(2)`-not-`shutdown(2)` poison law, idle reap, and the W1 `notify_inval_inode` handoff riding the classical sideband post-arm. Client levers: `SQUEEZEFS_IL_SESSIONS` (fd-sharded sessions — override lever only since the 2026-07-28 ingest-economy campaign: the DEFAULT is the shared derivation `squeezefs_ipc::sizing::il_sessions_default` = `clamp(cpus/4, 2, 16)`, which the daemon's drain-lane ceiling dominates at every machine size since the 2026-08-06 width re-grade, with paired tie tests making drift a red test), adaptive spin (`SQUEEZEFS_IL_SPINS` pins), `SQUEEZEFS_IL_OP_TIMEOUT_MS`. The 2026-07-28 **ingest-economy campaign** (`.benchmarks/2026-07-28-ingest-economy.md`, design Rev 13) closed the field's large-sequential-ingest wall (7.5 of 16.6 GB/s on a 32-CPU 2×200GbE client — half the drain threads idle from the defaults mismatch): service threads now **spawn on session admission** up to the derived ceiling (`ensure_service_threads` — a session-less host, i.e. every default mount, owns ZERO service threads; `ipc_service_threads` gauges the SPAWNED count), and ring-write severs recycle through the **severed-buffer pool** (`SeveredPool`, `ipc_severed_pool_{hits,misses,bytes}`; retention rides R5 as the non-sheddable `ipc_severed_buffers` component — deletes the per-op slab-alloc engine: 1.76 M minor faults/s across saturated svc threads at 12.7 GB/s, ~70 % %system → ~1.5 %; the §5.5.2 severance law's ONE arena read + single memcpy unchanged). fuse3 TPC lanes are now named `fuse3-tpcN` (they inherited `sqz-ipc-svc0`'s comm before — pidstat/perf attribution on interception mounts was polluted). The same campaign root-caused and fixed a BLOCKING latent dev wedge: the §5.4 transport-lease watchdog's `debug_assert` panicked write-handler tasks whose invocations legitimately exceeded 1 s under writeback backpressure — losing the FUSE reply (fsync D-state forever, umount joins) and the ent re-arm; it is now the loud-never-fatal `transport_lease_overlong` tripwire (stats inode), deterministically pinned by `tests/transport_lease_overlong_tests.rs` via the `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_WRITE_STALL_MS` seam (load selects such schedules; it never causes them — load-dependent hangs are first-class product bugs). The 2026-07-26 reap-economy program (`.benchmarks/2026-07-26-ipc-reap-economy.md`) made the libaio reap **event-driven in the sparse regime** (qd > 24 per ctx batches on a 50 µs bounded sleep — the deep-regime economy), made the service-thread drain pass registry-mutex-free (epoch-cached owned-session snapshots), and added the daemon lever `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_SPIN_US` (service-thread empty-pass spin window before parking; **default 0** — every nonzero setting buys libaio-fleet IOPS by taxing sync-lane CPU, so it is an explicit fleet lever, never an ambient default) plus the `ipc_service_parks` counter (park-economy gauge). The 2026-07-26 handoff-economy fix (`.benchmarks/2026-07-26-ipc-handoff-economy.md`, design Rev 10) closed the reap note's §7 residual: **ring handoffs (read misses AND writes) ride the fuse3 per-core handler lanes** (`handoff_spawn` → `tpc_spawn` — the venue kernel-lane handlers run on), never a `Handle::spawn` from the foreign service thread onto the multi-thread runtime's global inject queue (the measured ~130 µs/op cold-firehose queueing term; venue pins in `ipc_service.rs`, weakening-verified). Cold ddt il rows moved 0.87×/1.05× → **1.07×/1.13× kernel** (t16qd16/t32qd32, fabric-latency rig, medians of 3, engagement exact) with the 16-process fio fleet at 1.04× and the sync lane +13.6 %; do not reintroduce a runtime-handle handoff. G-L4-2 adjudicated (devsub, elbencho sync drivers, medians of 3): **warm 1,017,548 IOPS** (kernel-FUSE warm floor 644,726; target 1.0 M met at median) and **device-true 622,112** (floor 600 k; beats the kernel-FUSE 604,313 reference) — engagement exact on every run. The scoreboard's `SQUEEZEFS_SB_MODES=il` pass emits separately-labeled, never-W/L-gating rows whose engagement check exits nonzero on any silent-passthrough cell; its instrument must be a DYNAMIC elbencho (the pinned static one cannot load the shim). OQ-1 (libaio interposers) resolved: v1.1 economics item, no floor depends on it. stat/del storms and buffered-il semantics are N/S by design. The 2026-07-28 **ipc op-economy campaign** (`.benchmarks/2026-07-28-ipc-op-economy.md`) made the warm §5.5.1 serve prelude **allocation-free** (12 → ≈0 allocs/op: single metadata get + `Arc<str>`/CompactString `CachedMetadata` fields, stack-formatted keys, serve-into-arena via `crate::PayloadSink`, zero-mint staging probes, allocation-free service parks — pinned by `tests/ipc_op_economy_tests.rs`, whose `SQZ_ALLOC_TRACE=1` mode is the standing alloc-site profiler) and replaced the per-ticket completion wakes with the **per-session completion doorbell** (`squeezefs-ipc` `cqe_core::CqeDoorbell`, header line 2, IPC_ABI 2): the daemon pays a completion `FUTEX_WAKE` only toward a registered parked reaper (Dekker `fence(SeqCst)` pair is load-bearing; `ipc_cqe_parked_reaper_never_stranded` loom model, weakening-verified ×3), and the libaio sparse-regime reap parks on ≤ IL_SESSIONS session words (register→snapshot→re-scan) instead of a `futex_waitv` array per pending ticket. Phase B (counted A-B-B-A on the 235 µs fabric-latency venue, medians of 3, engagement exact) adjudicated: warm il rows **+22–29 % IOPS / −18–23 % daemon CPU/op** (lever 1); the doorbell's shared-session wake herd at qd ≤ old-threshold was found by the bracket (−21 % t16qd16) and closed by the counted retune **`REAP_EVENT_PARK_MAX` 24 → 2** (qd ≤ 2 keeps the event-driven wake — qd1 RTT clat preserved at 250–251 µs; deeper batches — t16qd16 +4–6 % over pre-doorbell, fleet −10 % daemon CPU; `SQUEEZEFS_IL_REAP_PARK_MAX` is the measurement lever).

The 2026-08-07 **shim-iops campaign** (`.benchmarks/2026-08-07-shim-iops.md`) wrote the rand-4k direct-drive per-op cost ledger and landed its instrument + levers: **`ipc_direct_phase_ns`** (stats inode, always-on — admit / inflight / finish / total; the direct-drive path has no handler, so `read_serve_phase_ns` structurally never sees the shim's hot path, and `fio clat − total` is the client+ingress subtraction the ledger keys on), **reaper/drain fusion** (`ipc_direct_inline_reaps` engagement gauge; the owning service thread's flush pass drains its lane's CQ in userspace behind a try-locked per-shard `cq_gate`, the reaper stays the blocking backstop on an EXT_ARG-bounded 100 ms wait — pre-5.11 kernels degrade loudly with fusion auto-disarmed; `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_INLINE_REAP=0` is the A/B lever — measured WASH on the local venue at 10.7 % engagement with 20 %-CPU reapers, target venue = the field's 80–85 %-CPU saturated reapers, the "local hides it" class), and two registered measurement levers at the shipped defaults: `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_EAGER_FLUSH` (mid-sweep dd enter at K unflushed SQEs; +2.3 % counted at 32×8, below the bar) and `SQUEEZEFS_IL_REAP_QUANTUM_US` (the client's deep-regime batch-reap sleep, default the shipped 50 µs — the 1×8 row priced the blind sleep at 4.6×, the fleet shape reads it as a wash). Local sustained rows: 32×32 = **1.03–1.04 M IOPS flat** (the 1 M class at depth), 32×8 = 741.8 k flat = 0.61× same-shape raw; the 594 k-field-row residual is named and split (client reap quantization / daemon inflight burst tails / device-at-load) with the field A/B spec filed in the note. **Round 2 — the reap-fanin campaign (2026-08-08, `.benchmarks/2026-08-08-shim-reap-fanin.md`)** built the shaped follow-ons: the v5 **slot ingress stamp** (`IpcSlot::stamp_ingress`, IPC_ABI 5, `mono_core.rs` #[path]-shared clock) turns the clat−total subtraction into the MEASURED **`ipc_ingress_ns`** term (row-validity: its count delta must account for the row's ring ops) — and the local ledger verdict is that the "client+ingress" term is ~ALL RING INGRESS (publish→dequeue queue wait: mean ≈ 145 µs at 32×8 / ≈ 520 µs at 32×32, observation ≈ 0), so field attribution must read `ipc_ingress_ns` before blaming the client reap; the **CqeDoorbell batch-wake threshold** (`park_begin_batch(k)` + the 16-byte doorbell's `wake_at` mark; `park_begin` ≡ batch(1); loom `ipc_cqe_batch_parked_reaper_never_stranded`, fence weakening re-verified) replaces the client's deep-regime blind 50 µs sleep with an event park cut short at the k-th completion (k = `sizing::reap_batch_wake_threshold` = `clamp(pending/4, 2, pending)`, derived — never a knob; the quantum knob is now the park's AGE BOUND), and the reap pass ceremony went allocation-free (`pending_tokens_into`, pass-reused buffers). Counted A-B-B-A vs the shipped pair (tcp devsub): 32×8 clat p50 **−16 %** / IOPS +2 %, 32×32 wash locally (ingress-dominated); the flat-event-park herd row (`SQUEEZEFS_IL_REAP_PARK_MAX=4096`) loses 3–10 % IOPS with +16–36 % tails at 0.58–0.80 wakes/op vs the threshold's 0.14–0.47 — the batch mark beats BOTH prior postures at fan-in. **Round 3 — the drain-funnel campaign (2026-08-08, `.benchmarks/2026-08-08-shim-drain-funnel-r3.md`)**: the first field `ipc_ingress_ns` read attributed ~85 % of the 32×32 field clat (1,561 µs mean at 656 k) to RING INGRESS — ~1,000 of 1,024 in-flight ops pooled pre-dequeue with the device at a fifth of its concurrency — and the local profile convicted the dd rings' kernel **`uring_lock`** (31 % of svc-thread cycles: the flush-ALL sweep putting every svc thread on every shard's enter + TWA_SIGNAL completion task-work landing mid-drain). Landed: **lane-scoped flush** (a svc thread enters only its OWN lane's ring; `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_LANE_FLUSH=0` = the pre-r3 flush-all A/B lever — foreign-lane stragglers ride that lane's reaper's bounded 100 ms cadence), **COOP_TASKRUN dd rings** (task-work on ring entry, never by signal; pre-5.19 degrades loudly), span-form phase records (−2 clock reads/op), and the always-on funnel instruments **`ipc_drain_pass_ns`/`ipc_drain_flush_ns`/`ipc_drain_empty_passes`** (pass count/ceremony cost; ops÷count = live ops/pass). Counted A-B-B-A vs dev tip: **32×8 +40 % (931 k vs 667 k), 32×32 +28 % (1.186 M vs 927 k medians; sustained 1.223 M flat 90 s)**, ingress mean −67..−75 % (583→190 / 161→41 µs), per-op svc wall 15.6→10 µs; the `LANE_FLUSH=0` isolation leg gives most of the win back (977 k @ 32×32) — the lane scope is the dominant lever. Session-width re-derivation was FALSIFIED (sessions 12/16 no-ops — the funnel was contention, not capacity).

### Volume lifecycle & online maintenance program (Implemented 2026-07)

The v1.1 VL1–VL10 program (`docs/design-volume-lifecycle.md`, Rev 12; closing record `.benchmarks/2026-07-22-volume-lifecycle-closing.md`) shipped the six-requirement charter: dynamic add/remove of metadata AND data volumes with honest capacity preflight and automatic migration, online-first defrag and fsck (with per-class repair), percentage-throttled jobs distributed across mounted clients, and offline distribution over the same wire. Load-bearing machinery (do not regress):

* **Job fabric + coordinator (VL2/VL2b)** — every maintenance op is a job type on ONE fabric: durable `JobRecord`/`ShardRecord` state as `job:` xattrs on ino 1 (KD-2 — whole-tx atomic, torn-immune, offline probe-readable; the FUSE **reserved-xattr screen** makes `job:`/format-config invisible/EPERM through FUSE), coordinator = the D0 writer-claim holder, per-worker **duty-cycle throttle** (KD-3, live-retunable), pause/resume/cancel, crash-resume by idempotent plan regeneration (KD-6), R5 `job_copy_buffers` budget component (Red ⇒ jobs pause loudly, `job resume` is the operator's — interaction pin (e)). **Remote workers** ride the §5.1.6 job-shard execution wire (TCP/TLS via tokio-rustls — network, deliberately not-uring; NOT a networked DLM, KD-15): storage-trust HMAC enrollment against the `job:enroll` meta-KV secret, shard leases (TTL + heartbeat) with fencing-checked result *proposals*, the **pre-allocated-unpublished-destination law in both halves** (remote writes touch only coordinator-pre-allocated unpublished offsets; reassignment always mints FRESH destinations — expired-lease tuples enter a do-not-publish quarantine), coordinator-held **WERO (rtype 3)** on data namespaces + preempt-on-expiry where `RESCAP` supports it, plaintext ⇒ mandatory-100 % verify-reads before publish (sampled under TLS).
* **Durable volume identity + online data add (VL3)** — never-reused `vol-{hex}` ids in `FormatConfig` (KD-5; legacy basename ids grandfathered byte-identically); `volume add-data` is online (admin lane) or offline (guarded open); `KV_VOLUME_LIFECYCLE` = superblock incompat bit 3 (KD-14 — old binaries refuse loud). The `/dev/shm` runtime-config file is DELETED; health overrides ride the admin lane.
* **Drain/remove + movers (VL4)** — §5.2 capacity preflight (refusals print the numbers; checkpoint re-verification self-pauses `paused-capacity`), CoW evacuation via the `move_one` engine (copy-then-republish; source frees only after durable publish + tier purge; clone-shared blocks move ONCE via the pre-publish refcount ledger — whose pinned SOURCE also rides it, the VL9 pin-(b) fix), Draining volumes still serve reads, `undrain` cancels cleanly, retirement is permanent. Auto-rebalance is submitted on `add-data` by default (KD-12, `--no-rebalance` opts out).
