* **Balance-aware write placement (VL4b, KD-16)** — `health_effective = health − clamp(k×(fill−set_mean),0,300)` published as an ArcSwap'd `PlacementTable` refreshed by the health worker: lock-free per-write pick, balance never outvotes health (penalty capped), failover semantics unchanged, new-allocations-only. On a laned CO-WRITER (`BlockAllocator::lane_placement_governed`, `SQUEEZEFS_COWRITER_LANE_PLACEMENT`, 2026-09-07 — `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-cowriter-lane-aware-placement.md`) the weight is the lane-reachable fraction of the lane share instead (a lane exhausts and refills PER VOLUME; the device fill says nothing about it), the pick skips a drained volume / reaches a refilled one off the allocator's O(1) counters, and `BackendRouter::allocate_placed_block` (the ONE pick+allocate act every fresh-block site calls) fails over to the sibling volumes before the bounded park — single-writer/authority placement byte-identical, `backend_placement_lane_{failovers,exhausted_picks}` the ledger. The lane-reachable count those weights and every watermark decision read is the FUNNEL's reachable set (2026-09-08, `.benchmarks/2026-09-08-placement-refresh-race.md`): a block inside a KD-4.4 trim claim window is pending supply the allocation parks for, never a deficit — the count does not dip by the trim batch for the device command's duration (the membership-exact `lane_owned_free_blocks` still excludes it).
* **Meta slot migration + guest keyspaces (VL5a/VL5b, KD-7; width surface superseded 2026-08-02 by `docs/design-dynamic-meta-routing.md` — user ruling: the frozen user-chosen width must be dynamic)** — routing modulo frozen PER SET as the **DERIVED** `routing_width W = 2^16` (never a knob; the u16 slot-id namespace; W ≤ 1 identity pinned for the in-RAM test constructor; global inos eternally stable), **stride-run membership stamps under incompat bit 6 (`KV_DYNAMIC_ROUTING`, presence-REQUIRED — frozen-width-era volumes refuse loud, reformat required)** with encoding-budget caps `STAMP_MAX_RUNS`/`STAMP_MAX_CURSORS` replacing the retired 64-hosted-slot cap, **mint spread** (`MINT_SPREAD = 64` slots/volume rotor — what makes the width real granularity: any volume's load is divisible into ≥ 64 movable slices from birth; gauges `meta_routing_width`/`meta_slot_mint_spread`/`meta_slot_stamp_{runs,cursors}_max`), durable slot map + root-ledger membership stamps (order-independent bootstrap, URI-disagreement refusals), guest slots under incompat bit 2 (`KV_GUEST_SLOTS`), conveyor pass-task delta tee with overflow fallback, per-slot cutover gate checked **before 4a acquisition** (parked ops hold no locks; never escalates to fail-stop), the §5.5.2b target-first ordered flip with enumerated crash windows, and **KD-8**: meta set changes drain staging then **rebind** the generation stamp (crash-safe two-phase staged-payload rebind — durable staged payloads survive membership changes). `add-meta`/`remove-meta` are offline verbs (stated loudly); `migrate-meta-slot` is online. Stamped-ahead crash re-runs converge on hard resume evidence (VL8 item 8).
* **fsck detect/repair + the zero-FP ladder (VL6a/VL6b, KD-9/KD-10)** — seven classes (C1–C6 + the C7 data scrub: AEAD/frame/readability per stored form, KD-17), **verify-before-report** (suspect → settle → lease re-check; C2/C3 use the allocation-epoch side map whose two-epoch survival only ESCALATES to the in-flight-registry liveness check — age alone is never a verdict) and **verify-before-repair** (repair consumes zero-FP-verified findings only, dry-run default, quarantine-first, honest no-fabrication where redundancy does not exist), offline `--shards k/N` + `merge-reports`, distributed scrub shards over the wire. `fsck_findings` must stay 0 on healthy volumes. Since 2026-08 the class set is **C1–C10**: C8 = durable-vs-derived block-reference drift (see *Durable block refcounts*), and **C9 = unreferenced inodes** (`src/fsck.rs`, `tests/fsck_c9_tests.rs`) — the class `docs/design-cow-kv-metadata.md` §4.10a left owed by keeping cross-volume `create` un-wrapped, and the ONLY detector + cleanup path for **pre-S3.5 field damage** (a crash mid cross-volume `link`/`unlink` leaked the inode *and* every block it owned, permanently). Walk direction is load-bearing: ONE `TREE_DENTRIES` pass marks every dentry's TARGET ino into a slot-decomposed `InoBitmap` (1 bit per inode, ≈ 12.5 MiB at the ≥ 100 M cap) differenced against the live-inode bitmap the census already produces — never the per-inode reverse-dentry scan (POSIX-4's `meta_parent_scans`). Zero-FP rests on the **writer era's ino floor** (DLM S2's era, the §4.8 watermark captured per keyspace at open — `KvMetaBackend::minted_in_prior_era`): a live create legitimately holds its inode record before its dentry, so only prior-mount records are candidates (residue from the CURRENT mount is reported by the NEXT one — no coverage lost, since residue is by definition from a prior mount), composed with settle + a FRESH dentry pass re-checked under the ino's exclusive 4a lease; an INCOMPLETE dentry pass records no verdict. Scoped to `nlink ≥ 1` on purpose — the `nlink == 0` shape is POSIX unlinked-but-open (and POSIX-15's rename-overwrite orphan) and needs the live open-count registries to judge. Repair = quarantine the record + every xattr (the `layout` IS the block map) with the freed keys enumerated → `delete_file` (durable refs released, blocks freed via the terminal-free law/reclaim queue) → destroy record+xattrs in ONE tx; C9 actions run FIRST in the apply loop so block-class findings about a doomed inode's blocks verify as healed and refuse. No incompat bit, no env knob. **C10 = inode-plane reference consistency** (`tests/fsck_c10_tests.rs`) is C9's safety half — the two shapes the C9 author enumerated and left undetected: `nlink` ABOVE the name count (a leak: the inode and its blocks are unreclaimable forever) and `nlink` BELOW it / `nlink == 0` with a live name / a name whose inode has no record (**the loss direction**: once ordinary unlinks drive the count to 0, `destroy_inodes`' live-nlink skip stops protecting an inode a live path still resolves — S3.5's `24ef223c` made such a dentry removable and nothing FOUND one). It rides C9's ONE dentry pass: `InoBitmap::mark`'s return value separates the first name from the rest, so only inos named twice cost a small map, and the census's non-directory `nlink != 1` map is the mirror (the hardlink population, never the tree) — both empty on a healthy tree without hardlinks. The DANGEROUS arms are the REVERSE difference of C9's two sets (the census skips `nlink == 0`, so both shapes land in `referenced \ live`) split by one fresh record read, so the class degrades only in its LEAK direction when the count map hits its derived budget. Zero-FP is NOT the era floor (a prior-era inode can be hardlinked a microsecond ago): it is the record witness `(nlink, ctime)` read under the ino's exclusive 4a lease BEFORE and AFTER the fresh dentry pass (every same-volume name-count change mutates the record in the SAME tx — link/unlink move nlink, rename stamps Δctime — so the pass's read-over-time skew can never produce a finding), the open cross-volume intent exemption (`xv_scan_intents` — the inode plane's `inflight_contains`), distinct `(GLOBAL parent, name)` counting (a VL5b migration's mid-copy duplicate dentry dedupes), directory exclusion from the count arms (`.`/`..` are synthesized), and the completeness gates. Repair is asymmetric by law: raising is safe, LOWERING runs only when a third independent pass agrees, a dangling name can only be removed (key+value quarantined), and directories/healed/reversed/in-flight cases are refused loud. Repair order is C9 → C10 → the block plane, and an applied raise sets `census_stale` so the block classes re-walk — the census's `nlink == 0` skip means a zero-count inode's blocks read as C2-leaked, and freeing them would destroy what the raise restores. `nlink == 0` WITHOUT a name stays POSIX-15's, unclaimed. No incompat bit, no env knob.
* **Four-axis defrag (VL7, KD-11)** — D1 free-space contiguity / D2 file locality / D3 staged-extent pressure / D4 meta node occupancy, each a gauge (`frag_d1..d4`) + an independently invocable mover that REUSES existing machinery (`move_one` with contiguity-aware picks, the W2 fold, the KV SMO compactor); `--report-only` measures without moving; `--rebalance` is the operator arm of the VL4 pass.
* **Interaction policy pins (VL9, `tests/interaction_tests.rs`)** — mover-scope SERIALIZE-loud at `claim_next` (one mover-class job per volume; `job_serialized_waits`); drain-during-fsck both proceed with FP = 0; defrag naming a draining volume = queue-then-refuse-loud; migrate + drain both converge; R5 Red pauses jobs loudly (never self-resuming).
* **Instruments**: `tests/run_volume_lifecycle.sh` (the rig, 17 legs), `tests/run_lifecycle_soak.sh` (the canonical 9-step soak, rotating 10-op menu, abort-on-failure), `tests/run_vl9_matrices.sh` (the counted ×10 G-VL matrices). Stats-inode deltas are the engagement instrument (a drain row is INVALID unless `evacuate_bytes_moved` accounts for the victim's used bytes).
* **Env/CLI knobs:** `--throttle <1-100>` on fsck/defrag/remove-data + `job throttle` live retune; `squeezefs job worker <sqmeta-uri>` (remote enrollment); (`format --meta-slots` is RETIRED — a hard error naming `volume add-meta --take-slots`/`migrate-meta-slot`; widths are derived); `SQUEEZEFS_FSCK_SETTLE_MS` (suspect settle window).
* **Stats families added (stats inode):** `job_{submitted,completed,cancelled,failed,tasks_done,checkpoint_writes,copy_buffer_bytes,paused_mem_pressure,paused_capacity,serialized_waits}`; `job_remote_{workers,enrollments,enroll_refused,shards,submissions,refused_stale,lease_expiries,reassignments,bytes_moved,verify_read_bytes,quarantined_destinations,pr_preempts,fence_mode}` (`job_remote_shards` = the remote-engagement instrument; `enroll_refused`/`refused_stale` are security/fencing tripwires); `evacuate_{blocks_moved,bytes_moved,shared_blocks_moved,inflight_bytes,stale_token_noops,deferred_staged_blocks,replans,needed_bytes,avail_bytes,transient_bytes}`; `volume_preflight_refusals`; `placement_table_refreshes` + `backend_placement_picks` / `backend_fill_{ratio,spread}`; `fsck_{inodes_scanned,nodes_walked,blocks_checked,refcounts_checked,suspects,suspects_cleared,epoch_exempted,inflight_exempted,mover_ledger_exempted,findings,scan_secs}` (`fsck_findings` must-stay-0 on healthy volumes) + the C9 pair `fsck_dentry_refs_indexed` (the cheap-direction pass) / `fsck_current_era_exempted` (the live-create shield's engagement — the inode plane's `epoch_exempted`); the C10 family `fsck_nlink_mismatch_high` (the LEAK direction) / `fsck_nlink_mismatch_low` + `fsck_nlink_zero_named` + `fsck_dangling_dentries` (**the LOSS direction — nonzero is the stop-and-read signal**) / `fsck_nlink_names_counted` (the multi-named population the counting rides, 0 on a hardlink-free tree) / `fsck_nlink_transient_cleared` (C10's guard engagement — the inode plane's `inflight_exempted`); `scrub_{blocks_scanned,bytes_scanned,aead_verified,frame_verified,readability_only,failures}`; `fsck_repairs_{planned,applied,refused}` + `fsck_quarantined_{records,blocks,bytes}` + `fsck_repair_classC{1..10}` + `fsck_repair_classC12` (C8 and C12 are structurally 0 — reported, never auto-repaired; C11 is report-only and has no counter); `defrag_{blocks_moved,bytes_moved,folds_kicked,meta_compactions_kicked,passes}` + `frag_d1_{contiguity,reclaimable_tail}` / `frag_d2_locality` / `frag_d3_pressure_bytes` / `frag_d4_dead_bset_ratio`; `fuse_reserved_xattr_refusals` (tamper tripwire).

### Error Handling & Crash Recovery

* FUSE op deadline **watchdog** (per-op `tokio::time::timeout` wrappers were retired in M4 — overdue ops are logged loudly, ring-admission parking escalates to `disabled_volumes`); staging recovery on remount (the `NvmeStaging::new` mount-time recovery scan, `src/cache/nvme.rs`) with fence + layout checks.
* Stale fencing tokens discard staged work (the **remount** contract); missing inode meta discards orphan active blocks. Live writeback units apply the same law supersession-aware (FIND-M11-A fix): fencing-stale ⇒ verified no-op, reclaimed-ino NotFound ⇒ verified orphan-discard, transient ⇒ retry forever (never-lossy).
* **A write-path allocation refusal is TERMINAL for that write** (2026-09-06, `.benchmarks/2026-09-06-cowriter-enospc-wedge.md`): `BlockAllocator::allocate_block_grace_bounded` — the finding-29 park every write-path mint runs under its `BLOCK_FLUSH_LOCKS` guard — waits for grace-held space at most `free_grace::pressure_park_wall_ms()` = 2 × the routine fence bound floored at 1 s, measured in WALL time (a DURATION — never `bound()`, the reallocation label whose `u64::MAX` sentinel made the wall infinite on every co-writer and reader), and on a co-writer only while the last harvest reply witnessed a held ring; past it `StorageFull` propagates (`ENOSPC`, gauge `write_enospc_refusals` for synchronous refusals), the stripe releases, and the never-lossy ladder owns only ACKed custody (the writeback classifier's ENOSPC-is-transient arm governs staged bytes the drain frees space for — that is not misfiled). A write that neither replies nor errors under `alloc_lane_enospc_refusals` growth is the wedge shape and a first-class bug. Because the refusal is terminal, the funnel's PENDING-SUPPLY set must be complete — and since 2026-09-07 it includes the discard-elision trim's KD-4.4 claim window (`.benchmarks/2026-09-07-overlay-enospc-convergence-flake.md`, gauge `alloc_trim_window_parks`): an allocation that scans the free list empty while a window is open parks on its return edge, because on a full store (virgin tail 0 ⇒ the pressure venue trims every displaced block as it is freed) the window can hold the WHOLE free list, and the empty scan alone was a spurious ENOSPC the overlay R7 loop caught at 3–7 %.
* **The fsync ladder barriers only the data namespaces the ino touched and joins its independent legs** (W-5, e2e perf audit row 15, 2026-09-08 — `.benchmarks/2026-09-08-w5-fsync-economy.md`, `src/fsync_economy.rs`, contracts `tests/fsync_economy_tests.rs`): `fsync_phase_ns` (always-on, exact-sum: `intent_barrier` / `data_flush` / `staged_promote` (the `SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_PROMOTE_STAGED` leg, 2026-09-09 — ≈ 0 with the lever off) / `data_barrier` / `meta_publish` / `meta_barrier` / `extent_barrier` / `total`) names where an fsync's time goes; the per-ino touched set is a stripe word (per-device ordinal bits + a stamp generation, the DLM tables' width) stamped at every layout publish (`DataRouter::block_ref_ops` — the C8 oracle's own closure) and at the two in-place DMA shapes (W1 patch, in-place overwrite), observed before the barriers start and cleared only after every leg succeeded (a shared stripe costs one extra coalesced barrier, never a missed one); write-through namespaces skip the barrier; the staged-payload sync and the per-namespace Fsyncs are joined and ALL awaited (never cancel a sibling — a dropped barrier future fails the coalescer's queued waiters); the S10 intent barrier stays first, the whole barrier step still strictly precedes the meta legs that name the blocks (DUR-1), the meta barrier stays last. Knobs `SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_TOUCHED_NAMESPACES` / `SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_PARALLEL_LEGS` (default on; `0` = the shipped serialized all-namespace shape); ledger `fsync_{calls,noop_clean,data_namespaces_touched,data_namespaces_flushed,write_through_skips,touched_unresolved,parallel_joins}`. The `w_durable` + fsync-storm row on squeeze-test is owed.
* Write verification is **opt-in** (`--write-verification`, optional sample rate).
* **Metadata crash contract**: whole-transaction atomicity + torn-write immunity **by construction** (v3 CoW KV — see Metadata format section + `docs/design-cow-kv-metadata.md` §4.10; the historical D0/D1/D2 ladder it replaced is `docs/design-wal-crash-consistency.md` §3).
* Mount probes each meta volume's sector atomicity (sysfs) — purely informational, surfaced as `meta_volume_atomicity_physical` while the contract field `meta_volume_atomicity` reads `cow-checksummed`. (The `--strict-meta-atomicity` flag only ever gated v2 volumes and was deleted with them.)

### Lock order & connection scope (must not)

Always acquire in this order; **never invert** (P1-9):

1. `active_inode_locks` (per-inode `RwLock`) — FUSE op serialization
2. `lease_locks` (per-inode) — only while acquiring/refreshing DLM lease
3. `BLOCK_FLUSH_LOCKS` (per block) — active-block mutation
3.5. `INODE_META_LOCKS` (routing, per-inode `Mutex`, 4096-way) — the striped block-map merge domain (`DataRouter::merge_block_mappings`, §5.3 one merge discipline; documented in `src/stripe_locks.rs:15` and depended on by 20+ sites). **Never held across a terminal `free_block`** (RES-1): the at-cap reclaim enqueue parks up to `SQUEEZEFS_RECLAIM_CAP_PARK_MS` per key — collect the displaced keys under the lock and free them after the guard drops (the pattern `write_striped`, `truncate_layout` and `release_superseded_staged` use).
4. MetaLV metadata-transaction locks, in sub-order:
   - **4a.** DLM `I{ino}` / `D{parent:name}` (per-object; `DlmLockManager`). **Striped, DERIVED width (D-3, 2026-09-05):** `stripe_locks::dlm_stripe_width` = `SQUEEZEFS_DLM_STRIPES` explicit, else `next_pow2(max(4096 shipped, 16 × possible_cpus × q_depth))` — 16,384 on a 32-CPU box; the same law over the 1024 floor sizes the lease waiter / grant-floor pair (`src/dlm.rs`) and the S9 serve stripe (`meta_ship/publish.rs`). The guard is held across the whole commit park (D5), so a stripe collision there costs the colliding op a full commit; the census `<table>_stripe_collisions` (false sharing — the width's) vs `<table>_key_waits` (same key — the workload's) + `lock_phase_ns.dlm_guard_wait` says which. Guards drop at the terminal outcome BEFORE the ack is sent (the one permitted scoping — a committer never parks on its own previous tx). An op's guards are ONE canonical `lock_many` per volume, taken holding no other 4a guard; **the ONE sanctioned out-of-order ADDITION is `DlmLockManager::try_lock_many`** (symmetric PR 13b — a cross-owner step whose slot moved TO the initiator mid-plan, applied locally under guards that travelled to the old holder): try-only, all-or-nothing, a miss is the caller's retry class (the slot-moved re-dispatch); never a parking acquire while holding — that is the self-deadlock class `fe62cb0e`'s flip fix adjudicated. Evidence `.benchmarks/2026-09-05-d3-dlm-stripe-derivation.md`; field rows owed.
   - **4b.** (design-cow-kv-metadata §4.9 4b): **per-node write locks — the commit path takes leaf locks only, in ascending NodeId order, deduped, lock-then-revalidate-then-retry against SMOs; interior-node locks belong exclusively to the serialized per-volume checkpoint/SMO task (parent-then-child), which is what keeps the two lock populations acyclic. Node locks are never held across device I/O (commit apply is RAM-only; the journal entry write happens after unlock; writeback freezes under the lock and appends outside it; SMOs reserve in-window and write after release) and never held while waiting on ring space (ring admission happens before any node lock — §4.4 pt 5; the checkpoint task's own admissions never park, they drain-and-retry).** Since the M7 commit conveyor (design-metadata-throughput §5.5), the leaf-lock **taker** population is exactly {the per-volume conveyor **pass task**, the checkpoint/SMO task}: user committers take no node locks — they enqueue and park on oneshots, each queue entry co-owning its tx's 4a DLM guards until terminal outcome, and the pass takes the batch's **union** leaf set under the same 4b discipline while it *holds-but-never-acquires* DLM guards (no new wait-for edges; see `src/stripe_locks.rs`).
   - **4c.** The journal reservation — a wait-free atomic, not a lock; ordered inside 4b by protocol, it imposes no ordering edges.

**Must not:**
* Hold inode **write** guard across long backend I/O when block locks suffice (striped data path = meta-prep only under write lock — P1-8).
* Hold a **pooled MetaLV connection/handle** across durable NVMe / staging I/O (P1-10: open → short meta → drop → I/O → re-acquire for commit).
* Acquire (1) while holding (3).
* Burn fencing tokens on failed lock acquisition.

### OS / fabric notes

* Dirty ratios and fabric tuning remain operator concerns for large clusters.
* Primary data path uses the NVMe/NVMe-oF block storage backend.

---

## TDD Development Workflow

A disciplined, test-first development workflow for building industrial-grade, high-performance, highly scalable, asynchronous Rust systems. Tests define the contract. Code fulfills it.

### Core Principle: Tests First, Code Second

Every feature, bugfix, or refactor follows the same cycle:

1. **Define** — What should the correct behavior be?
2. **Test** — Write tests that assert that behavior (they will fail).
3. **Implement** — Write the minimum code to make the tests pass.
4. **Refine** — Refactor for clarity and performance while tests stay green.
5. **Commit** — Each logical step gets its own git commit.
6. **Merge** — Bring the code base back into the primary branch.

### Phase 0: Branch from `dev`

All work happens on feature/fix branches off `dev`. Never commit directly to `dev` or `main`.

```bash
git checkout dev
git pull origin dev
git checkout -b feat/short-description   # or fix/short-description
```

- **Branch naming**: `feat/`, `fix/`, `refactor/`, `perf/`, `docs/` prefixes matching conventional commit types.
- **One branch per logical unit of work**: a feature, a bugfix, or a hardening pass.

#### SqueezeFS I/O constraint (always io_uring when we can)

This repo is Linux + **io_uring**-first. The 2026-08-01 ruling applies here too: io_uring is the measured-fastest presumption, not dogma — a counted A/B showing a faster arrangement displaces it; a failing test never does. When planning or implementing:

- **Default to io_uring** for FUSE request traffic (FUSE-over-io_uring after arm), NVMe/block I/O (`NvmeBlockDev`), and local file I/O (`crate::uring_fs`) whenever the kernel can support it.
- **Do not** introduce or re-enable classical `/dev/fuse` or POSIX file I/O fallbacks to “make it work.” That is an anti-pattern here. Fix the uring path or fail loud.
- The intentional classical FUSE uses are the one-shot **`FUSE_INIT`** exchange (kernel requires it before REGISTER) and the post-arm **classical sideband session** for the traffic the kernel refuses to put on the ring (FORGET/INTERRUPT/resends + `fiq->ops` switchover stragglers) — serviced via io_uring `Readv`, never as a hot-path fallback. All other requests/replies are over-uring only after arm.
- If a failing test tempts you to disable over-uring or switch to blocking `read`/`write`, stop — write a failing test that encodes correct uring behavior, then fix uring.

#### No dead code

- Remove unused functions, fields, imports, and modules as part of the same change that made them unused.
- **Never** add `#[allow(dead_code)]` / `#[allow(unused_*)]` to park unused code. Delete it; git has history.
- Clippy with `-D warnings` failing on unused items means **delete**, not allow.

### Phase 1: Understand & Plan

Before touching any code:

- State the goal in one sentence.
- Identify the crate(s) and module(s) that will be affected.
- List the behaviors that need to be correct when you're done.
- Identify edge cases, error conditions, and concurrency concerns upfront.
- Determine `Send`/`Sync` requirements for all shared state.
- Identify async boundaries — which types must implement `Future`, which tasks cross `.await` points.
- **I/O path:** does this touch FUSE, NVMe, or local files? If yes, plan the **io_uring** design — not a classical shortcut.
- **New client-side machinery?** Check the reference fast-FUSE clients first (DAOS client, JuiceFS — `docs/reference-clients-survey.md`) for a proven pattern before inventing one.

Produce a brief execution plan:

```
Goal: [one sentence]
Affected: [crates/modules]

Behaviors:
1. [Expected behavior] → verify: [how]
2. [Expected behavior] → verify: [how]
3. [Edge case] → verify: [how]

Concurrency:
- Shared state: [Arc<Mutex<T>>, Arc<RwLock<T>>, lock-free, actor]
- Executor: [sqz_exec lane (which class), sqz_blocking pool, no async at all — NEVER tokio in product code]
- Cancellation: [CancellationToken, drop-based, deadline]
```

### Phase 2: Write Tests

Write the test file(s) BEFORE any implementation:

- **Happy path**: The expected, normal-operation case.
- **Error paths**: What happens when inputs are invalid, connections drop, `CancellationToken` fires, channels close?
- **Boundary conditions**: Empty inputs, maximum values, zero-length streams, single-node mesh.
- **Concurrency**: Race conditions under parallel access. Multiple tokio tasks hitting the same state. Use `#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread", worker_threads = N)]` to stress shared state.
- **Timeouts & Cancellation**: `tokio::time::timeout`, `CancellationToken` propagation, slow peers, hung connections.
- **Backpressure**: Channel saturation, bounded queue overflow, consumer lag.
- **Shutdown**: Graceful drain under in-flight work, partial completion, resource cleanup on abort.

#### Test Quality Checklist

- [ ] Each test has a clear, descriptive name (`test_broadcast_storm_uuid_dedup`)
- [ ] Tests are independent — no shared mutable state between test cases
- [ ] Parameterized tests via `rstest` (`#[case]`, `#[values]`) for combinatorial scenarios
- [ ] Async tests use `#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread")]` to expose data races
- [ ] Assertions include meaningful failure messages (`.expect("msg")`, `assert!(cond, "msg")`, or `pretty_assertions`)
- [ ] No `tokio::time::sleep` for synchronization — use channels, `tokio::sync::Barrier`, `watch` channels, or `CancellationToken` for coordination
- [ ] Tests that spawn tasks verify cleanup (no leaked tasks, no dangling `JoinHandle`s)
- [ ] Property-based tests via `proptest` or `quickcheck` for complex invariants

#### Commit: Tests

```
test(crate): define behavior for [feature]

- Happy path: [describe]
- Error cases: [describe]  
- Edge cases: [describe]
- All tests currently FAIL (no implementation yet)
```

### Phase 3: Implement

Write the minimum code to make all tests pass:

- Don't add features beyond what the tests require.
- Don't add abstractions for hypothetical future needs.
- Don't optimize prematurely — correctness first.
- Run `cargo test --all-features` after every meaningful change.
- Run `cargo test --all-features -- --test-threads=1` if order-dependent failures are suspected.

#### Implementation Checklist

- [ ] All tests pass (`cargo test --all-features`)
- [ ] `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` clean
- [ ] `cargo fmt --check` clean
- [ ] All async functions and shared types are `Send + Sync` where required
- [ ] Error types use `thiserror` (library) or `anyhow` (application) with `.context()` for operation context
- [ ] Resources cleaned up via RAII — `Drop` impls, `scopeguard`, or `_drop_guard` patterns
- [ ] No `unsafe` without a documented safety invariant and a safety comment
- [ ] No `unwrap()`/`expect()` in library code — propagate errors
- [ ] All `JoinHandle`s are awaited or explicitly detached with documented rationale
- [ ] No TODO/FIXME left without a tracking issue
- [ ] **I/O uses io_uring where applicable** — no new classical FUSE/file fallbacks; FUSE request path stays over-uring after arm
- [ ] **No dead code** — unused items deleted; no new `#[allow(dead_code)]` / unused allows to hide them

#### Commit: Implementation

```
feat(crate): implement [feature]

- All tests passing
- Clippy clean, fmt clean
- [Brief note on approach taken]
```

### Phase 4: Refine, Harden, & Benchmark

With green tests as your safety net:

- Refactor for clarity if the implementation is messy.
- **Mandatory Benchmarking**: EVERY public function must have a corresponding Criterion benchmark. Performance is a first-class feature.
- Profile allocations for data-plane code via Criterion's allocation measurements.
- Run `cargo bench` to record historical performance; commit results to `.benchmarks/` or use `cargo-criterion` with `--save-baseline`.
- Run `cargo loom` on lock-free or highly concurrent data structures to exhaustively check memory-ordering correctness.
- Add any additional edge case tests discovered during implementation.

#### Commit: Refinements

```
refactor(crate): [what changed and why]
```
or
```
perf(crate): optimize [hot path] - [result]
```

### Phase 5: Integration Verification

Before considering work complete:

1. `cargo build --all-targets --all-features` — full project compiles.
2. `cargo test --all-features` — all tests pass project-wide.
3. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` — zero warnings.
4. `cargo fmt --check` — formatting is clean.
5. `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --no-deps` — documentation builds without warnings (rustdoc's intra-doc-link lints are warnings by default and cargo never fails on them; the flag is what makes this line a gate).
6. `task audit` (= `cargo audit --deny unsound --deny yanked` over BOTH lockfiles — the root workspace and the excluded `crates/fuse3` fork). Vulnerabilities, unsound and yanked crates fail; the `unmaintained` residue is adjudicated in `docs/rc-manifest.md` §5. ENG-2: this line had no enforcement point until 2026-08-03 — it is now part of `task check`.
7. Review the diff: every changed line traces to the original goal.

If a `task check` or `cargo xtask check` target exists, run it — it is the authoritative quality gate.

**Required verification gate (must pass before commit — tiered by change class; user directive 2026-07-18: stop running full test suites on doc-only changes):**

| Change class | Required gate |
|---|---|
| **Docs/markdown only** (no `.rs` / `.toml` / tests / scripts touched) | markdown link/anchor check only — **no cargo gate** |
| **Manifest metadata only** (`Cargo.toml` non-dependency keys) | `cargo check` + `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` + `cargo fmt --check` |
| **Code, dependencies, tests, or harness scripts touched** | the **full gate** below (plus loom when a lock-free core changes) |

The **full gate** — `task check` runs exactly this (ENG-2/7/8/17 closed the
holes: audit, the shipped feature configuration, and the excluded fork):

```bash
task check                        # the authoritative gate; the lines it runs:
cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings   # the SHIPPED (default-features) config — ENG-8
cargo fmt --check
cargo test --all-features -- --test-threads=1
RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --no-deps   # rustdoc lints are warnings by default — the flag is the gate
cargo bench --benches -- --test   # criterion smoke: one iteration per bench, no measurement
task check:fuse3                  # the excluded fork's own suite/fmt/clippy/doc/bench — ENG-17
task check:loom                   # the isolated loom crate COMPILES against the cores it #[path]-includes (build+fmt, -D warnings; the model RUN is tests/run_loom.sh)
task check:fuzz                   # the excluded fuzz workspace TYPE-CHECKS on stable (check+fmt, -D warnings) — the twelve TEST-4 targets rotted silently for a month without this line; the instrumented RUN is the nightly tier
task check:docs                   # markdown link/anchor check (tests/check_markdown_links.sh)
task audit                        # cargo audit, BOTH lockfiles, --deny unsound --deny yanked — ENG-2
```

`--all-features` compiles `dhat-on`, which **replaces jemalloc** (and drops
the load-bearing `dirty_decay_ms:1000`): correct for a correctness gate, never
valid for a number. Such a binary says so in its own `--version` line and logs
it at mount and on `squeezefs bench` (ENG-8). Measurement builds are
`cargo build --release` — default features.

CI runs the same gate (`.github/workflows/gate.yml`, ENG-7): per-PR/push on
non-docs changes, docs-only changes get `docs.yml`, and `nightly.yml` re-runs
`task audit` (advisories land without commits) + the gate on `dev`. The
external POSIX suites, the bench baseline and every measurement rig stay OFF
CI by design — a shared cloud runner cannot produce a valid row.

### Phase 6: Merge to `dev` & Cleanup

After all checks pass, merge the feature branch back into `dev` and delete it:

```bash
git checkout dev
git merge --ff-only feat/short-description
git branch -d feat/short-description
```

- **Always `--ff-only`**: If `dev` has diverged, rebase the feature branch first (`git rebase dev`). No merge commits for linear history.
- **Delete the branch**: Once merged, the branch serves no purpose. Delete it immediately.
- **Push**: `git push origin dev` to sync remote.

### Git Discipline

- **`dev` is the integration branch**: All feature/fix branches start from and merge back into `dev`. `main` is reserved for releases.
- **Atomic commits**: One logical change per commit. Tests and implementation can be separate commits.
- **Conventional format**: `type(scope): description` — types: `feat`, `fix`, `test`, `refactor`, `perf`, `docs`, `chore`.
- **No direct commits to `dev` or `main`**: Always use a branch.
- **Commit messages explain WHY**, not just what. The diff shows what changed; the message explains the reasoning.
- **Delete branches after merge**: Stale branches are clutter. Merge → delete → move on.

### Anti-Patterns to Avoid

| Anti-Pattern | Correct Approach |
|---|---|
| Writing code first, tests after | Write tests first — they define the contract |
| Testing only the happy path | Cover errors, boundaries, concurrency, cancellation, backpressure, shutdown |
| Giant commits with tests + code + refactor | Separate commits for tests, implementation, refinement |
| "Improving" unrelated code while fixing a bug | Touch only what the goal requires |
| Skipping concurrency tests because "it's simple" | Always use `multi_thread` flavor. Simple async code has races too |
| Using `tokio::time::sleep` for test synchronization | Use channels, `Barrier`, `watch`, or `CancellationToken` |
| Speculative abstractions | Build what's needed now. Refactor when a real pattern emerges |
| `unwrap()` in library code | Propagate errors with `?` and `thiserror` |
| `unsafe` without safety comments | Document invariants; prefer safe alternatives |
| Blocking the async runtime | Use `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` for CPU-bound or blocking I/O |
| Unbounded channels in production code | Use `mpsc::channel(bound)` with explicit backpressure |
| Leaking tasks (fire-and-forget `spawn` without tracking) | Use `JoinSet` or structured concurrency patterns |
| Ignoring `Send + Sync` bounds | Design types to be `Send + Sync` from the start; document why if not |
| Falling back to classical `/dev/fuse` or POSIX file I/O to dodge an uring bug | **Always use io_uring when we can.** Fix the uring path or fail the mount/op — never reintroduce classical escape hatches |
| Leaving unused code with `#[allow(dead_code)]` “for later” | **Delete dead code.** Restore from git when needed; keep clippy `-D warnings` clean by removal |

---

## Build

System deps (Ubuntu/Debian): `build-essential pkg-config libfuse3-dev fuse3 clang libclang-dev`. The build links FUSE 3 and uses `io-uring` + `/dev/fuse` — it will not compile on non-Linux.

```bash
cargo build --release
```

**go-task is the packaged build path** (user directive 2026-07-25; `Taskfile.yml` + `docker/` — README §Building has the full workflow): `task build` = host dev build, `task build:{rocky8,rocky9,ubuntu2404,ubuntu2604}` / `task build:all` = containerized distro builds, each landing `dist/<target>/{squeezefs, libsqueezefs_il.so}` under default names (the folder is the same-commit daemon+shim pairing unit — KD-7). In-container assertions: `--version` never `unknown`, per-distro glibc ceilings. `task check` runs the authoritative full cargo gate. Plain `cargo build` remains valid for dev work.

### Optional Cargo features (off by default)
- `gds` — GPU Direct Storage RDMA path (pulls `libloading`).
- `dhat-on` — heap profiling (`dhat`); a static `dhat::Alloc` replaces the global allocator in `src/main.rs`.
- `coz-on` — causal profiling; the `coz_progress!` macro (defined in `src/lib.rs`) becomes active.

`[profile.release]` keeps `debug = true` so release binaries carry symbols for profiling. `tikv-jemallocator` is the global allocator on Linux.

**The two-profile LTO law (user directive 2026-09-02):** `release` is **thin LTO** with parallel codegen — the dev / field-A/B / gate profile (fat LTO + one codegen unit cost every rebuild minutes for a few percent). **Tagged releases only** ship from **`dist`** (`inherits = "release"`, `lto = "fat"`, `codegen-units = 1`; shim twin `preload-dist`) via `task dist:<distro>` — never the dev or gate profile. **Dist artifacts ship with SPLIT debug info** (2026-09-12): the daemon and shim are stripped of DWARF (the 1.2.4 daemon was 329 MiB, 305 of them `.debug_*`; stripped ≈ 24 MiB) and their debug info lands beside them as `squeezefs.debug` / `libsqueezefs_il.so.debug`, joined by a `.gnu_debuglink` whose CRC the artifact check verifies (`docker/check-split-debug.sh`) — perf/gdb resolve symbols from the same directory or `/usr/lib/debug`, so profiling rows on dist binaries stay symbolicated (ship the sidecar to any box you profile on). `release` keeps its symbols in-binary. `dist/<target>-dist/SHA256SUMS` covers all four files. The binary names its profile (`--version` … `profile <name>`; `.stats build_profile`), and a measurement row must state it: both legs of an A/B carry the same profile, and release-gate / scoreboard rows run `dist`. Pinned in `tests/cli_version_tests.rs` (only `dist` may carry fat LTO).

### First-party `fuse3` fork (`crates/fuse3`)

`crates/fuse3` is a maintained **fork** of the upstream crates.io `fuse3` crate (forked from v0.7.3, Sherlock Holo, MIT — upstream attribution retained in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and `crates/fuse3/LICENSE`). It has substantially diverged from upstream: the FUSE-over-io_uring transport, payload leases, and flag sweeps live here — and it now carries the **SqueezeFS release-train version** (1.2.x), not upstream's 0.7.x. Treat it as first-party code — edit it directly when FUSE behavior changes; **never** replace it with the crates.io version or "restore" `Cargo.toml.orig`. `Cargo.toml` declares the prepublish-style requirement `fuse3 = { version = "1.2", … }` plus `[patch.crates-io] fuse3 = { path = "crates/fuse3" }`: only the in-tree fork can satisfy it (crates.io fuse3 never can), and the patch indirection is **load-bearing for workspace topology** — the fork is its own excluded workspace root (standalone suite, own lock), and cargo rejects a direct path dependency to a nested foreign workspace root ("multiple workspace roots found in the same workspace"); patch entries bypass that check.

### Build features for profiling

| Feature | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| *(default)* | Production binary; `tikv-jemallocator` on Linux |
| `dhat-on` | Heap profiling via `dhat` (replaces global allocator in `main`) |
| `coz-on` | Causal profiling; `coz_progress!` active |
| `gds` | GPU Direct Storage path |

```bash
cargo build --release
cargo build --release --features dhat-on
cargo build --release --features coz-on
```

Release profile keeps `debug = true` for symbolicated profiles.

## Testing

Most integration tests execute against local file-backed MetaLV sandboxes.

### Required verification gate (tiered by change class)

Match the gate to what the commit touches (user directive 2026-07-18: stop running full test suites on doc-only changes). Same table as the TDD Phase 5 gate — keep the two in sync:

| Change class | Required gate |
|---|---|
| **Docs/markdown only** (no `.rs` / `.toml` / tests / scripts touched) | markdown link/anchor check only — **no cargo gate** |
| **Manifest metadata only** (`Cargo.toml` non-dependency keys) | `cargo check` + `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` + `cargo fmt --check` |
| **Code, dependencies, tests, or harness scripts touched** | the **full gate** below (plus loom when a lock-free core changes) |

The **full gate** — `task check` runs exactly this (ENG-2/7/8/17 closed the
holes: audit, the shipped feature configuration, and the excluded fork):

```bash
task check                        # the authoritative gate; the lines it runs:
cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings   # the SHIPPED (default-features) config — ENG-8
cargo fmt --check
cargo test --all-features -- --test-threads=1
RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --no-deps   # rustdoc lints are warnings by default — the flag is the gate
cargo bench --benches -- --test   # criterion smoke: one iteration per bench, no measurement
task check:fuse3                  # the excluded fork's own suite/fmt/clippy/doc/bench — ENG-17
task check:loom                   # the isolated loom crate COMPILES against the cores it #[path]-includes (build+fmt, -D warnings; the model RUN is tests/run_loom.sh)
task check:fuzz                   # the excluded fuzz workspace TYPE-CHECKS on stable (check+fmt, -D warnings) — the twelve TEST-4 targets rotted silently for a month without this line; the instrumented RUN is the nightly tier
task check:docs                   # markdown link/anchor check (tests/check_markdown_links.sh)
task audit                        # cargo audit, BOTH lockfiles, --deny unsound --deny yanked — ENG-2
```

`--all-features` compiles `dhat-on`, which **replaces jemalloc** (and drops
the load-bearing `dirty_decay_ms:1000`): correct for a correctness gate, never
valid for a number. Such a binary says so in its own `--version` line and logs
it at mount and on `squeezefs bench` (ENG-8). Measurement builds are
`cargo build --release` — default features.

CI runs the same gate (`.github/workflows/gate.yml`, ENG-7): per-PR/push on
non-docs changes, docs-only changes get `docs.yml`, and `nightly.yml` re-runs
`task audit` (advisories land without commits) + the gate on `dev`. The
external POSIX suites, the bench baseline and every measurement rig stay OFF
CI by design — a shared cloud runner cannot produce a valid row.

### External POSIX / IO suites (require root + a mounted FS)

These are **not** part of `cargo test`. Run as **root** on Linux/WSL after material write-path, layout, or FUSE lock changes:

```bash
# pjdfstest POSIX conformance suite (full run; single-test arg for fix loops)
sudo tests/run_pjdfstests.sh

# LTP filesystem syscall tests
sudo tests/run_ltp_syscalls.sh

# fstests filesystem checks
sudo tests/run_fstests.sh

# Mount-level IO benchmark
sudo tests/run_elbencho_mount.sh
```

Prerequisites: Volume formatted/mounted per `QUICKSTART.md`. Failures here can pass pure unit tests and still indicate mount regressions.

Dev boxes without spare raw NVMe: `sudo tests/dev_substrate.sh create` builds the preferred pseudo-everything substrate — memory-backed null_blk (mds) + zram (oss) exposed as real `/dev/nvmeXnY` namespaces via nvmet-loop (QUICKSTART → *Dev Box: Virtual NVMe Substrate*); `SQZ_DEVSUB_TRANSPORT=tcp` builds the same shape over **nvmet-tcp on localhost** (the fabric-sensitive benchmark venue — see *Benchmark substrates* under Benchmarks & Profiling). Do not measure barrier-bound work on file-backed-on-btrfs volumes: the substrate bracket is 165× on the journal barrier (`.benchmarks/2026-07-14-metadata-throughput-baseline.md`).

### Test tiering (do not run acceptance suites at per-commit cadence)

fstests/LTP are **wall-clock-bound** (fixed-duration fsx/fsstress soaks, mount-cycle overhead) — a full `-g auto` is ~5 h regardless of CPU speed. Running them per fix wastes hours re-failing tests already known to fail. Use three tiers:

| Tier | What | When | Cost |
|------|------|------|------|
| **Per-commit** | the change-class gate above (docs-only: markdown link/anchor check, no cargo; manifest-metadata: check+clippy+fmt; code-class: the full cargo gate — clippy/fmt/`test --test-threads=1`/doc/bench-smoke; loom when a lock-free core changes) | every commit, sized to its class | seconds–~5 min by class |
| **Per-PR (data-path)** | the full cargo gate + the relevant house rigs (`tests/run_preload_gate.sh` for ipc/preload surfaces, `tests/run_volume_lifecycle.sh` for volume-lifecycle surfaces) — **external POSIX suites (fstests/LTP/pjdfstests) do NOT run per-PR** (user ruling 2026-07-20: they added hours per code commit re-confirming known failures; they are the release gate below) | PRs touching the write/read/layout/FUSE paths | minutes |
| **Per-PR (preload/interception)** | `tests/run_preload_gate.sh` (leg 1, unprivileged: sanctioned cdylib build + Issue-4 guard proof + passthrough battery) / `sudo tests/run_preload_gate.sh` (leg 2: mount parity + §3 rule-4 engagement + dup/close_range/lseek rows + kill-9 and fork-kill-parent soaks) | PRs touching `crates/squeezefs-preload/`, `src/ipc_host.rs`, `src/ipc_service.rs`, or the fuse3 notify surface | leg 1 ~2 min; leg 2 ~5 min |
| **Per-PR (nvmeof/guard fidelity)** | `sudo tests/run_nvmeof_fidelity.sh quick` — kernel-nvmet product-verb round-trip + 1 guard kill-9 cycle, zero-residue snapshot assert; real kernel, zero mocks (design-nvmeof-target-management §6.8). **nvmet is THE target since 2026-09-12 (R-SYM-8, design-symmetric-metadata §5.8.1): SPDK was retired forward-only — `--target-stack spdk`, `target install`, `SQUEEZEFS_SPDK_TGT_BIN` refuse loud naming nvmet + the re-share sequence, `src/nvmeof/spdk/` is deleted, contracts in `tests/nvmeof_retire_spdk_tests.rs`** | PRs touching `src/nvmeof/`, `src/meta_backend/reservation.rs`, or the writer-guard gate | ≤10 min (2m20s measured dual-stack, zram/localhost dev box; the nvmet-only re-measurement is owed — `.benchmarks/2026-09-13-sym-retire-spdk.md`) |
| **Release gate (MANDATORY before any release tag)** | **pjdfstests (`sudo tests/run_pjdfstests.sh`) + full LTP (`sudo tests/run_ltp_syscalls.sh`) + full fstests (`sudo tests/run_fstests.sh`, `-g auto`) must ALL pass** (user ruling 2026-07-20: every release passes all three; known-failure exceptions require a documented adjudication in `.benchmarks/`; **every failure triggers the repro-port mandate below** — the fix lands with a cargo test reproducing it), plus `sudo tests/run_elbencho_mount.sh` and `long_validation.py` scale mounts. Standing adjudications (2026-07-23, the ONLY ones): fstests **generic/003 + generic/192** (one class — noatime by design + kernel-TTL attr observation; 192 is the atime-delta face of the same ruling), **generic/213** (thin provisioning by design), and **generic/634** (timestamps are i64 nanoseconds — a deliberate ±292-year range, the ext4-u34/xfs-bigtime finite-range class; the daemon saturates deterministically and pins it in cargo, but incore clamping needs `sb->s_time_max`, which the FUSE protocol cannot advertise — kernel-interface-only) — pinned expected shapes in `tests/run_fstests.sh`; **generic/464 and generic/074 are expected-PASS** (fixed 2026-07-21, `.benchmarks/2026-07-21-wedge-and-074-fixes.md` — any diff on them is a regression). First full three-suite pass: `.benchmarks/2026-07-22-volume-lifecycle-closing.md` | before EVERY release tag; also nightly unattended where a box is available | hours |
| **Release gate (cargo leg): require-mount** | `tests/run_require_mount_gate.sh` (= `task check:require-mount`) — the twelve live-mount suites re-run with **`SQUEEZEFS_TEST_REQUIRE_MOUNT=1`**, which turns every mount-class self-skip into a hard FAILURE, plus the machine-readable **skip ledger** (`target/skip-ledger.jsonl`, one JSON object per line, `##SQUEEZEFS-SKIP##` on an uncaptured stderr handle). Spec §11 **TEST-2**: before this leg existed, `cargo test --all-features` could be all-green on a box without `/dev/fuse` / `fuse.enable_uring` / `fusermount3` with the product's core mechanism entirely unexecuted, and libtest swallowed the notices without `--nocapture`. **Every environment skip in the tree routes through `crates/squeezefs-testkit`** — `tests/skip_ledger_tests.rs` fails the build on a private `[SKIP]`-and-return, which is what stops a 15th file from inventing its own | before EVERY release tag, alongside the three external suites | ~15–30 min |
| **Release gate (cargo leg): zc-capability** | `sudo tests/run_zc_capability_gate.sh` — the capability-class suites (the FUSE_URING_ZERO_COPY contract sets `fuse_zc_write_fusion_tests`/`zc_bridge_cqe_wedge_tests`, the `bench_tests` zc rows, the zcrx lib ring probes) run as **root on an sqz-kernel box** with **`SQUEEZEFS_TEST_REQUIRE_CAPABILITY=1`**, turning every capability-class self-skip into a hard FAILURE (user ruling 2026-08-14: "whats the point of tests that don't actually run?" — the kernel arms zc only for `capable(CAP_SYS_ADMIN)`, so unprivileged `cargo test` self-skips the whole zc surface even where the sqz kernel is present). The pin `the_capability_gated_suites_all_ride_the_zc_capability_gate` (tests/skip_ledger_tests.rs) auto-discovers every suite declaring `SkipClass::Capability` and fails the cargo gate until this script names it — a new zc suite cannot quietly stay out. Builds run unprivileged (`run_as_user`); root runs the binaries | before EVERY release tag on the sqz dev box (any box with the sqz kernel + root), alongside require-mount | ~4 min |
| **Nightly (fuzzing)** | `cargo +nightly fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=<secs>` over the twelve `fuzz/fuzz_targets/` targets (spec §11 **TEST-4**): the on-disk decoders (`kv_record`, `kv_bset`, `kv_node`, `kv_journal`, `kv_superblock`, `layout_wire`, and since 1.2 `kvmap_record` — the block-map tree's key/value/head codecs, incompat bit 16), the cluster wire (`job_wire_frame` — the job-shard vocabulary; since 1.2 `cluster_wire_frame` — the `RpcFrame` vocabulary under every class cap + the per-frame MAC path + the S8 verb bodies — and `publish_wire` — the S9 schema-13 multi-call request/reply frames, `MigrateBlockMap`/`MapMigrated` included), and the IPC shared memfd (`ipc_bootstrap`, `ipc_ring_header`) — the three untrusted-input surfaces. Requires a nightly toolchain + `cargo-fuzz`; `fuzz/` is its own workspace (sanitizer RUSTFLAGS must not poison the main graph) and carries its own `[patch.crates-io] fuse3` entry. **Per-commit cadence gets the proptest mirror instead** (`tests/decoder_property_tests.rs` — the same never-panic-on-arbitrary-bytes and round-trip laws on stable, inside the normal gate). A crash found here follows the **repro-port mandate**: the fix lands with a cargo test reproducing it (the inaugural one is `kv_bset_record_count_cannot_drive_allocation` — a lying `record_count` was an allocation authority, 128 GiB / SIGABRT under any address-space cap). Inaugural smoke: 9 targets × 60 s, ~74.9 M execs, zero crashes. **1.2 release campaign** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-03-release-1.2-fuzz.md`): 12 targets × 90 s × 2 jobs, 383.6 M execs, zero crashes on the final tree — after two finds: `cluster_wire::hex_decode` panicked on a non-ASCII `job:enroll` secret (str slice by byte index; a daemon abort on a corrupt/tampered xattr — repro-ported + fixed), and the workspace itself had ROTTED (the S3 `cluster_wire` port made `job_wire_frame`'s reader synchronous the day the target landed; nothing compiled `fuzz/` for a month — `task check:fuzz` now does, on stable, inside `task check`). On NixOS the nightly is the fenix flake (`nix build github:nix-community/fenix#minimal.toolchain nixpkgs#cargo-fuzz`), run inside `nix-shell` with the fenix `bin/` ahead on PATH and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` naming the gcc `-lib` output for the libFuzzer runtime's `libstdc++` — `rustup` is not the path here | nightly; MANDATORY before a release tag and after any on-disk/wire format change | ~10 min build + N × campaign |
| **Nightly + pre-merge (perf PRs): bench baseline** | `tests/run_bench_baseline.sh` — the full Criterion set (root + fuse3 workspaces) measured and compared vs the committed reference (`.benchmarks/criterion-baselines/reference.json`); exits nonzero past the per-group regression threshold (default 10 % ns/op — table in the script). SAME-BOX relative tripwire: pins to quiet cores, refuses hot CPU (≥ 80 °C) / foreign cargo work / cross-box compares. Per-commit stays bench SMOKE only (`cargo bench --benches -- --test`, unchanged). Refresh the reference (`save`) as part of intentional perf landings | nightly; MANDATORY pre-merge for PRs claiming or risking hot-path perf | ~20–40 min quiet-box |
| **Nightly (nvmeof fidelity)** | `sudo tests/run_nvmeof_fidelity.sh full` — guard kill-9 ×10 (restart-from-zero), PR matrix (RESCAP / register / fence / preempt / disconnect persistence — no PTPL: nvmet has none by design), loud-fail matrix (G3, incl. the R-SYM-8 retirement refusals), crash-window states (§6.4 law 6), adopt legs (§6.10), target-restart persistence (G2: configfs wipe → `restore` → reattach), soft-RoCE plumbing leg (rdma_rxe — plumbing only, no guard/perf claims), A/B smoke row (recorded), teardown-to-zero-residue proof | nightly + nvmeof program/release gates | ≤70 min budget (5m46s measured dual-stack, zram/localhost dev box; the nvmet-only re-measurement is owed — `.benchmarks/2026-09-13-sym-retire-spdk.md`) |
| **Per-release (competitive)** | `tests/run_scoreboard.sh` — the standing **multi-reference scoreboard**, the top-3 proof surface (PERFORMANCE IS PRIMARY, 2026-07-18; absorbed `run_vs_juicefs.sh` forward-only): SqueezeFS vs **JuiceFS**, **SeaweedFS native** (weed server + weed mount), **geesefs** + **mountpoint-s3** over one shared local RustFS store — pinned releases w/ checksums, matched substrate/budgets, 3 regimes × 6 elbencho workloads, **RW6 durability-leveled write rows** + **RW6-del durable delete rows** (relaxed published labeled; fsync/syncfs-inclusive *durable* rows govern write **and delete** verdicts — the old seq-write ACK allowlist retired to ∅, and the inaugural run's three adjudicated entries were deleted the day their follow-ups landed: **standing allowlist ∅, bare run**), declarative + empirically-verified **N/S capability matrix** (neutral cells, recorded refusals), per-row-family **top-3 rank adjudication**; exits nonzero on any unattributed LOSS vs any reference or INVALID sqz cell; `SQUEEZEFS_SB_ALLOW_LOSS` tracks attributed losses (legacy `SQUEEZEFS_VS_*` spellings honored). `SQUEEZEFS_SB_SMOKE=1` micro-grid is the per-commit-tier plumbing check | every release + after perf-relevant landings; baseline `.benchmarks/2026-07-18-multi-reference-scoreboard.md` incl. the 2026-07-18 allowlist-∅ addendum (JuiceFS-only lineage: `.benchmarks/2026-07-15-vs-juicefs-scoreboard.md`) | ~2–4 h (smoke ~10 min) |

**NVMe-oF fidelity tier scripts** (design-nvmeof-target-management §6.8, PR 5/N5; nvmet-only since R-SYM-8): `tests/nvmeof_target_substrate.sh` (create/teardown/status/mkzram/snapshot — product-verb-driven kernel-nvmet fabric, `:fideli-` ownership marker, test port slice 54000–54099, built-in zero-residue snapshot diff), `tests/run_nvmeof_fidelity.sh` (the quick/full orchestrator above), `tests/guard_smoke.sh` (writer-guard matrix on product-shared namespaces, `--stack nvmet --loops N`; `--stack spdk` refuses naming nvmet). They supersede the ad-hoc root gates that lived under `.agents/spdk-scoping/` (rig scripts removed from the tree 2026-07-18 — git history at `c615e3a`; the scoping record itself lives on as `.benchmarks/2026-07-17-spdk-target-scoping.md`). The multi-run discipline below applies verbatim to the ×10 guard matrix.

**The repro-port MANDATE (user ruling 2026-07-20 — non-negotiable):** for **every test we fail in pjdfstests, LTP, or fstests**, a **real cargo test that reproduces the found issue** MUST be written (the `tests/*_tests.rs` layer, red against the bug, green with the fix) and land WITH the fix. This is what makes the release-cadence external gate safe: a bug found once can never be reintroduced, because its repro runs on every commit forever. No fix for an externally-found failure merges without its cargo repro; where a scenario is genuinely not reproducible in-process (kernel-interface-only behavior), the exception is documented in the fix commit and the scenario joins `SQUEEZEFS_FSTESTS_QUICK` instead.

**`SQUEEZEFS_FSTESTS_QUICK`** (defined in `tests/run_fstests.sh`) is the **standing regression set**: every fstests case that has ever caught a real SqueezeFS bug, plus core fsx/fsstress soak, hole/punch/seek coverage, and mount basics. **Grow it whenever a new test surfaces a bug.**

**Fix-loop discipline (FAIL FAST — user rule 2026-07-22; retires the former "inventory once" posture for full runs):**
1. **Full runs fail fast** — `tests/run_fstests.sh` (full `-g auto` AND the QUICK set) aborts IMMEDIATELY at the first UNEXPECTED failure (nonzero exit, artifacts preserved, the failing test named loudly); `tests/run_ltp_syscalls.sh` aborts on the first FAILED/BROKEN (TCONF skips continue). The adjudicated by-design set (fstests 003/192 noatime, 213 thin provisioning) continues ONLY when a failure diff matches its pinned expected shape EXACTLY — any other diff on those tests aborts too (`expected_shape_diff` in the runner is the single source of the shapes).
2. **Fix immediately, red-first** — the aborted run's failure gets its cargo repro-port (red) and fix per the repro-port mandate, verified with the single-test mode (`sudo tests/run_fstests.sh generic/NNN` — classic one-shot, unchanged).
3. **Resume from the failure point** (user directive 2026-07-23 — fix-loop efficiency): after the fix, re-run the failing test, then `sudo tests/run_fstests.sh --resume-from <test>` continues the sweep from that position to the END of the persisted from-zero expansion order (the runner saves it on every from-zero run, so resumes are deterministic). Resume passes are DEBUGGING efficiency only; prior-position greens from the aborted pass are provisional.
4. **Final acceptance is one complete from-zero pass** — after the LAST fix, the FULL run must complete end-to-end fail-fast-clean from zero on the final binary (the counted-restart discipline below applies verbatim: resume passes and pre-fix progress are never acceptance evidence, and the closing report cites only the from-zero pass). The release gate's "all three suites pass" means exactly that.

**Multi-run discipline (counted runs: soaks, ×N repro suites, acceptance medians):**
1. **A deterministic or attributable failure on any early run aborts the count** — stop, fix, then **restart the count from zero**. Runs completed before the fix verified the old binary; they are not creditable toward the fixed one's acceptance.
2. **Completing the remaining rolls is legitimate only as *declared* rate/signature gathering** — measuring how often a flake fires or capturing its tape for attribution — and must be labeled as such in the evidence note, never counted as acceptance.
3. **Acceptance counts always restart post-fix** (e.g. "green ×10" means ten consecutive greens of the final binary, not eight-before plus two-after).

## Benchmarks & Profiling

### Cloud runs require EXPRESSED approval (user mandate 2026-08-28)

**No AWS/cloud cluster may be launched without the user's expressed
approval for THAT run.** The cheap-first pipeline (2026-08-28) is the
standing order of operations: every binary passes a FREE local fleet
probe first; the cloud venue exists ONLY for the final sustained
verdict, and each launch is individually approved by the user before
`tests/cloud_bench_cluster.sh launch` is invoked. A gate/fix train never
implies cloud approval.

### Benchmark VENUE: A/B and A-B-B-A rows run on squeeze-test, never the dev box (user directive 2026-09-07; scope narrowed 2026-09-14; restated 2026-09-20)

**Every A/B, A-B-B-A bracket, per-lever leg and acceptance row runs on
`squeeze-test`** (the 32-core Xeon field box). The dev laptop has **heat
soak**: its clocks fall as a row sequence runs, so the second arm of a
pair is measured on a slower machine than the first and the delta is
skewed — in most cases beyond the effect being measured. Dev-box rows are
**scoping evidence only** (mechanism engagement, tripwires, a first read
of a shape) and every note carrying one must say so; the acceptance
pair is re-run on the box before any verdict is written. This is the
same fact the criterion baselines already record ("the thermally-capped
dev box gives relative truth only") applied to every measurement class.

**Restated by the owner 2026-09-20 (verbatim): "this machine has thermal
issues and should not be a ABBA test that holds merit / record but more of
a 'it works' and then we have squeeze-test for ABBA numbers testing."**
The two roles are exclusive: the laptop answers *does the mechanism
work* (legs green, engagement laws met, tripwires 0, crash/storm counts
from zero); **no laptop number holds merit, enters a record as a verdict,
or decides a gate** — a laptop row that reads a MISS on a timing-shaped
or throughput-shaped law (a flush-ceiling overrun, an ingest multiple, a
wall-clock ratio) is *venue-attributed pending the box*, never a MISS and
never a MET, and the record says so in the row. Only a squeeze-test
A-B-B-A bracket adjudicates a number. (PR 13's `sym-walls` row is the
standing example: one `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns` on the laptop
under a 3.1–3.7 GHz throttle is a venue reading; the box bracket decides.)

**The box is for the acceptance rows ONLY, at the minimum count (user
directive 2026-09-14: "run the minimal amount of ABBA rows on
squeeze-test … don't use it for verification of something working").**
Everything that proves a mechanism WORKS — cargo suites, the flat/stamped
matrix, live-FUSE legs, the fidelity tier, smoke mounts, "does it
engage", crash/kill matrices, from-zero ×N determinism runs — is LOCAL
and is finished BEFORE a row is scheduled on the box. A box row is one
A-B-B-A bracket per row set, with the reversed re-run bracket only for a
row that read DELTA; the scoping pass on the laptop (mechanism engaged,
tripwires 0, the shape read once) is the filter that keeps the box quiet.
Program gates name WHICH PRs get a box bracket (this program: the PR-1
solo re-gate — met — and the PR 13/14 acceptance; every other rung takes
the cheaper local re-gate), and a box row that exists to confirm
correctness is a rule violation, not evidence. The box has NO Rust
toolchain and no internet: arms are built on the laptop
(`task build:rocky8`, the `release` profile for dev A/Bs — the two-profile
law — both arms the same profile) and copied over with checksums; every
file placed on the box is listed in the row's note (§Box footprint).
The multi-writer fleet rig's ACCEPTANCE rows run on the box too
(`tests/mw_fleet.sh` + `tests/run_mw_matrix.sh` over the dev substrate;
Open MPI is the vendor build at `/usr/mpi/gcc/openmpi-*/bin`, put it on
`PATH`); its functional legs run locally first. Cloud runs still need
expressed approval (below).

### Benchmark substrates: the two-substrate rule (2026-07-27)

The 2026-07-27 amplification campaign proved the nvmet-**loop** rig HIDES bandwidth-economy and network-stack effects (the 1.85×-amplification field capture reproduced on nvmet-tcp and was invisible on loop — `.benchmarks/2026-07-27-shim-write-amplification.md`). Both substrates come from `tests/dev_substrate.sh` (same backings — memory-backed null_blk mds + zram oss; same create/teardown/status verbs; the two coexist on one box via disjoint names/state dirs/ports):

| Substrate | Build | Use for |
|---|---|---|
| **loop** (default) | `sudo tests/dev_substrate.sh create` | Controlled-latency A/B: per-op decomposition, barrier-bound metadata work, latency-shaped micro-comparisons — the lowest-noise venue |
| **tcp** (`SQZ_DEVSUB_TRANSPORT=tcp`) | `sudo SQZ_DEVSUB_TRANSPORT=tcp tests/dev_substrate.sh create` | **MANDATORY for fabric-sensitive rows**: writes, bandwidth-bound shapes, multi-connection workloads. nvmet-tcp on `127.0.0.1` runs the real NVMe/TCP queue + softirq machinery, so device-bandwidth economy (write amplification, request-size collapse, per-connection contention) is measurable. TCP service-port slice **54100–54199** (default 54129) — never the fidelity tier's 54000–54099 |

Every measurement states its substrate alongside its instrument (the standing instrument-alignment lesson). A loop-only result on a fabric-sensitive row is scoping evidence, never acceptance.

**Write-amplification instrument (standing row requirement for write/bandwidth rows):** report **device bytes ÷ user bytes** on the DATA namespace (per-row `/proc/diskstats` deltas — meta rides its own namespace, so the data delta is exact), **`wareq-sz` vs the volume block size** (request-size collapse is the fragmentation face of the same failure), and the **`block_free_*` reclaim counters** (`block_free_{discards,discard_bytes,file_punches,punch_bytes,reclaim_skipped}` — a freed-block path that writes instead of deallocating shows here; the 2026-07-27 Write-Zeroes-on-free conviction — plus the async-reclaim family `block_free_reclaim_{queued,queue_bytes,batches,sync_drains,fence_halts,commands,cap_parks,cap_overflow}` (write-wall campaign, 2026-07-31: blocks ÷ `commands` = the live coalesce factor; `cap_parks` = at-cap enqueues parked by park-don't-spill — device commands NEVER issue from the enqueue context; `cap_overflow` = park-bound expiries soft-overflowed into the queue, ≈ 0 steady state): terminal frees queue their discard/punch to the background reclaimer (`src/block_reclaim.rs`, `.benchmarks/2026-07-27-async-block-reclaim.md`) so the displaced-block discard stream never rides the write path; `sync_drains` is the ENOSPC pressure valve and must stay 0 except under real space pressure, `fence_halts` counts entries dropped-without-finish_free because the D0 writer guard fenced/fail-stopped — the reclaimer observes the same `failed` latch the journal-barrier escalation sets, so a fenced zombie never issues destructive discards (0 on healthy mounts; investigate alongside `writer_guard_fenced`) — and the discard/punch ledger still counts per displaced block). `tests/write_amp_rig.sh` is the packaged instrument (kernel/shim/shim-frag sides, per-row stats-inode deltas, charter-rule-4 engagement checks); a write row without its amplification columns is INVALID for scoreboard/acceptance purposes.

**A-B-B-A alternating-order brackets (standing comparison rule, 2026-07-27):** any A-vs-B comparison where a shared store AGES across the runs — overwrite rows against an already-written file set, before/after-binary brackets over one formatted volume, anything where run order changes free-list shape, staging temperature, or device thin-state — must run **A-B-B-A** (both orders) and cite BOTH brackets; a single-order delta on an aging store is an ordering artifact until the reversed bracket reproduces it (the first-write-vs-overwrite field conviction was proven order-independent exactly this way).

**Sustained-state rows (standing measurement rule, 2026-07-29 — user directive: speeds must SUSTAIN):** every headline perf claim must include a **sustained steady-state row** — ≥ 60 s (`--infloop --timelimit` or equivalent), throughput flat across the window (no decay beyond noise between the first and last third) — alongside any short bracket rows. A burst number that decays is a FAILED row, not a result: report the sustained figure as the claim and the burst separately if informative (the field's 1.2 M → 250 k seq-read collapse — a warm-tier burst masking the cold ceiling — is the motivating case). Cache-warm bursts, first-pass-only rates, and any row shorter than the machinery's convergence horizon (governor probing, reclaim drain, tier churn) are label-only.

### Criterion benches (the microbench program, 2026-08-04)

The hot-path machinery carries Criterion coverage (all `harness = false`; coverage table + inaugural numbers: `.benchmarks/2026-08-04-microbench-program.md`). Every bench documents its FIELD-derived input shape in-file with the evidence-note citation — toy inputs are program violations:

- `cargo bench --bench high_concurrency_bench` — in-memory lock / pool / cache contention + dynamic-meta-routing (`route_ino_width`).
- `cargo bench --bench squeezefs_bench` — full FS stack + `CryptoCompressState` compression/encryption micro-benches.
- `cargo bench --bench meta_lv_bench` — KV metadata: bset build/search/merge/fold, tree lookup/insert/scan, the D7 fold regimes, and the `kv_journal` group (entry encode+xxh3 checksum at the D4 create shape + the lever-B publish-batch-64 shape; the §4.4 pt 5 wait-free admit/reserve core).
- `cargo bench --bench ipc_hop_bench` — IPC protocol floors: ring push/pop, slot cycle, payload moves, and the `cqe_doorbell` group (complete elide/wake decision, park ceremony — both Dekker fences priced).
- `cargo bench --bench copy_path_bench` — copy primitives: NT-vs-std ladder across the `SQUEEZEFS_NT_COPY_MIN` floor (near-zero-copy campaign), the `SeveredPool` hit-vs-alloc-miss sever cycle (ingest economy), the zero-heap `StackKey` serve-prelude keys (op economy — the ns/op face of the `SQZ_ALLOC_TRACE` allocs/op==0 law).
- `cargo bench --bench zcrx_bench` — `SpanLedger` grant/release + contended clone-drop, and the chunk-stream PDU parser at page-grain chunk shapes (32×4 KiB-PDU stream / single 128 KiB MDTS-face PDU).
- `cargo bench --bench read_path_bench` — `ReadLaneHold` insert/serve/retire + credit-0 probe + miss probe (EXA 4 MiB-block / 1 MiB-sub-read shape), and the §5.3 classifier's three arms (match, classified-membership reorder, foreign claim).
- `cargo bench --bench write_path_bench` — coverage-union `record_write` (in-order + kernel-split OOO), extent overlay park/fold (`fold_fill` = 16 shape), supersession snapshot + CoW + the `BLOCK_FLUSH_LOCKS` stripe route, and layout publish encode (full-save-1024 vs delta-64 vs delta-apply).
- fuse3 (own workspace): `cd crates/fuse3 && cargo bench` — `fuse3_hot_bench`: ent header codec (in-header decode, attr/entry-out reply encode — the exact session bincode shape), commit-batch drain accounting, kmbuf attach/recycle law on the `sim_anon` venue.

**Bench smoke is part of the required gate:** `cargo bench --benches -- --test` runs one iteration of every bench without measurement (seconds, not minutes). It exists because `meta_lv_bench` sat silently broken from PR 8 until a perf investigation tripped over it — a panicking bench must fail the gate, not wait for the next baseline run.

**Baseline teeth (nightly / perf-PR tier):** `tests/run_bench_baseline.sh` runs the full set (both workspaces), extracts every bench's median, and compares against the committed reference `.benchmarks/criterion-baselines/reference.json` — exiting nonzero past the per-group threshold (default 10 % ns/op; looser only where the venue is structurally noisier: contention 25 %, async fixture-heavy KV groups 20 %, crypto throughput 15 % — the table lives in the script). Baselines are **SAME-BOX RELATIVE tripwires** (the thermally-capped dev box gives relative truth only): the script pins to the quiet cores, refuses to measure when Tctl/Tdie ≥ 80 °C or foreign cargo work is running, and refuses cross-box compares (stamped hostname). Refresh with `tests/run_bench_baseline.sh save` after intentional perf landings and commit the new reference. Per-commit cadence stays SMOKE-ONLY — never the measured compare.

### Profiling command set (release)

#### CPU (perf)

```bash
# One-shot sample while a mount + workload runs
perf record -g --call-graph dwarf -o perf.data -- \
  target/release/squeezefs mount …   # or attach: -p $(pidof squeezefs)

perf report -i perf.data
```

#### Heap (dhat)

```bash
cargo build --release --features dhat-on
# Run workload; on exit dhat prints / writes heap profile per its config
DHAT_OUT=dhat-heap.json target/release/squeezefs mount …
```

#### Causal (coz)

```bash
cargo build --release --features coz-on
coz run --- target/release/squeezefs mount …
```

Use coz/dhat **after** a known-good cargo test gate, against a representative mount.
